# Тема: мотивация

Дословные слова авторов по теме «мотивация»: 301 фрагментов из 159 роликов. Читай подряд — это опыт практиков в их собственных словах; «Мысль» и «Для Напарника» — вывод разметчика.



# 🎬 "He Built a Game For His Girlfriend — The New York Times Paid Millions" — Brain & Behavior Explained


### Неповторимый секрет: делать для одного человека, а не для метрик  ·  [07:22](https://youtu.be/nsg9FZucQts?t=442)
**Мысль:** Типичный дизайн спрашивает, как максимизировать вовлечение; Wordle спрашивал, как сделать что-то милое для одного человека. Аудитория чувствует разницу, а сдержанность нельзя сфабриковать.

> Almost all of them have failed. Why? Because of a fundamental difference in intent. Typical app design asks, "How do we maximize engagement and retention?" But Wordle's design asked, "How do I make something genuinely lovely for one person?" At scale, those two questions might produce similar psychological mechanics, but audiences instinctively sense the difference. You simply cannot manufacture genuine restraint. We all know the difference between a product engineered to hack our attention and one built purely out of care. So, the next time you're building something, ask yourself, does it need to be engineered to perform, or does it just need total genuine honesty? The ultimate takeaway from the whole whle phenomenon is that sometimes the most powerful psychology in the world isn't manipulation or growth hacking or artificial virality. Sometimes the real secret to reaching millions is simply restraint, simplicity, and building something completely true for just one person. If you can do that well enough, the millions will recognize it when they see it.

**Для Напарника:** Принцип Напарника: сделан с заботой для одного человека с СДВГ, без хаков внимания; проверять каждую механику на честность.

# 🎬 10 ChatGPT ADHD Hacks That'll Change Your Life! 🔥 — Antonia's Universe


### Застревание часто ложная дилемма: ищем середину, а не силу воли  ·  [03:52](https://youtu.be/NUU1n5O6d4o?t=232)
**Мысль:** Выбор «A или B» обычно ложен; промежуточный вариант, к которому тянет, снимает паралич. Совет «заставь себя» не работает.

> Getting unstuck or overcoming decision fatigue. Like we will often be like, "I want to do this, but I actually have to do this. So therefore, I'll be on my phone or I'll be doing nothing." You will be surprised how often that happens. But as a coach, I've noticed that it's often a logical fallacy. It's not do A or do B, and you can't do that. There are often middle grounds, you know, like the previous one where we talked about removing the emotional barrier first. And what I'll do is I'll just tell the whole scenario to ChatGPT. I'll be like, "Hey, I want to write a script, but I can't because I actually feel like to complete the script, I need to have a better website, which will be linked underneath the video." Literally happened to me this week. And ChatGPT said, "Well, you can write the script draft. And once you have done that, you can go back and do some decisions for the website. They will help you move forward with the next steps, and eventually when that's done, you can add that to finalize your script." And there you go, you're no longer stuck because there is a middle ground. It's not all or nothing. Tell whatever your story is to ChatGPT and ask for some perspective or some options that you can take that are ADHD friendly. Things you are inclined to doing because if it tells you, "Oh, just force yourself to do the thing you don't want to do," that's not going to work.

**Для Напарника:** В разбиении задачи (api/breakdown) предлагать «середину»: начерно один кусок вместо всего-или-ничего; без принуждения.

**Мысль:** Проигранный в игре сценарий превращается в пережитый опыт, и страх первого раза уменьшается.

> And practice for learning and confidence. Because if you were, let's say, a Renaissance person and you were still alive today, you would definitely ace a history test on the Renaissance because your brain would remember the sounds, the sights, the people, the daily struggles. It wouldn't just feel like some facts on a piece of paper. It would be your real life experience. And obviously, you can't time travel unless you know something that I don't, but you can do something very similar in ChatGPT, which is to play pretend and help yourself learn faster, better, and actually remember things in a way that sticks. Like, I'm learning about this thing. Can we pretend that I'm actually doing it? Give me a character, give me a task. Let's run through the scenario where I have to use my knowledge like I actually would need to in real life. That also removes some of the anxiety about trying something in a real situation because now you've practiced it. Like it gives you the confidence that you would know how to act if it did happen.

**Для Напарника:** Идея для напарника: «давай прорепетируем» трудный разговор/звонок перед запуском задачи.

> Encouragement. Let's say you want to raise your prices, but telling your customers that you'll be charging more makes you feel guilty. And you can't make yourself bring it up. You can really use some new perspective from ChatGPT. So, go ahead and say, "Hey, I want to do this, but it's hard. I need encouragement. Don't lie to me. Don't give me empty compliments because that doesn't work. Just tell me real reasons why I can do this and why it's the right decision and why I shouldn't feel guilty about it." Like raising your prices isn't selfish, it's sustainable. If you undercharge, you will burn out and then you can't help anyone. Reading a few of these will make you so much more assertive and help you go and charge what you're worth, assert your boundaries, do things that push you out of your comfort zone, stick to your decisions without constantly overthinking them or spiraling into self-doubt. Use that encouragement to validate you and affirm you so you can go and do the thing.

It does help that when you finish a complex task your brain releases massive quantities of endorphins and you just get happy. There's also an internal tension that occurs when a checklist or progress bar appears incomplete. This is called the Zeigarnik Effect. When people feel the need to finish and complete a task. This is a massive win for user onboarding. Simply framing to do items or assign a process as incomplete can be a huge win.

# 🎬 5 Game Design Secrets That Fix Retention & LTV — Kutovaya Elena


### Сегментируй по мотивации, а не по поведению; SDT как простая модель  ·  [01:42](https://youtu.be/EAfYEhCve6Q?t=102)
**Мысль:** Каждое действие в продукте либо закрывает потребность, либо нет; без потребности человек приходит только по необходимости. Простейшая модель SDT: автономия, компетентность, принадлежность.

> User psychology. Product analytics usually splits users by their behavior. How often they visit? How did they go in the funnel? How much they spend. Game design splits users differently. It start with motivation. These are two very different approaches and they lead to very different product architectures. Here is the basic tool, a model that sorts players into groups based on what motivates them. There are similar models for products too. They point to a few core types of motivation. Your job as a product architect is to figure out how your product meets each one. Every action in your product either meets one of these needs or it meets none of them. If a feature doesn't give the user what they are after, they'll come back only when they really have to. They won't get pulled in. They definitely won't be happy around it. That means lower engagement, low retention, lower altitude. Gamified systems follow one simple rule. Give every type of user a way to meet their needs. Motivation should shape everything. Your features, your tone of voice, your UI UIX, even how your design, your offers. If you don't keep every user type in view, some of them will just drop off. Not because they're the wrong users, because your product has no depth for them, no triggers built for them. And that shows up as retention decrease. and exactly those segments. The easiest model to learn and to use is SDT, selfdetermination theory. A huge number of modern products already run on it. It names just three core motivations. Autonomy, competence, and social belonging. Find out how your users meet each of them in your product.

**Для Напарника:** Проверять каждый экран Напарника по SDT: даёт ли он автономию (выбор), компетентность (прогресс острова), принадлежность (кот рядом).


### Полезное трение: IKEA-эффект, но не в основном цикле  ·  [09:46](https://youtu.be/EAfYEhCve6Q?t=586)
**Мысль:** Игры вводят трение намеренно: то, что строишь сам, ценнее. Но трение добавляют только перед бонусом, никогда в core loop; бесполезное трение (формы, навигация) убирают.

> The last one, the fifth one is friction. Usually, friction is any effort your product asks from the user before they get a result. The standard product playbook says cut friction everywhere. Always game design does the opposite. It builds friction in on purpose. No effort, no reward and that means no dopamine hit from overcoming it. Some friction is just bad. For example, I don't know extra form fields, confusing navigation, typing the same thing twice. this kind your conversions, it cost you your day one retention. But some friction is designed on purpose to make the result feel more valuable for the user. Here is a classic example. The EK something you build yourself feels more valuable than the exact same thing handed to you finished completed. Even though it's objectively identical for hot products, here is my advice. Go carefully. First rule, only add friction in front of something extra, something added. A chunky UI for example is just friction with no PO that's a fast track to drop off was sessions was retention was DO but a temporary cash back boost for a bit of extra effort that's a real benefit I guess and the lever on our pool second rule never put friction in your core loop that kills your product it filters people out it ts retention but friction around a gift that's a different that's a chance for the user to invest something of their own in non-game products that this looks like personalizing your setup, building your own profile, building your rowing dashboard, any step where the user puts in a little effort because before they get the result, cutting that kind of friction is not always a win for retention or LTV. Sometimes it just makes the product feel less valuable. The real job isn't to remove all friction. It's to sort it into two piles and only clear out the kind that's useless.

**Для Напарника:** Личная настройка (имя, выбор шага, оформление) даёт чувство своего; но запуск сессии должен быть в один тап, без трения в ядре.

> The short answer is no, because ultimately you were still asking the user to do the hard work of learning. But this formula is incredibly empowering for educators and designers. You don't actually need to beat social media at its own game entirely. The app only needs to be a about 80% as engaging as TikTok. Why? Because the remaining 20% is covered by the user's internal motivation. People naturally have a genuine desire to improve themselves and do something meaningful. Just look at the contrast here. When you mindlessly scroll on social media for 2 hours, you usually close the app feeling hollow, like you just wasted a huge chunk of your day. But gamified education provides internal satisfaction. You get actual meaning out of it. That desire for self-improvement is the secret ingredient that makes these stolen social media techniques actually work for education. The engagement tricks get them in the door for sure, but the meaningfulness of the content is what keeps them there.

Like, I start off the day and eventually I get stuff done, but I just kind of go with the flow and move from task to task to task to task. Now, I know what that is called. # 🎬 ADHD and Motivation — How to ADHD


### Мост мотивации: у СДВГ половина «досок» отсутствует, это не лень  ·  [00:11](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=11)
**Мысль:** Разрыв между намерением и действием у большинства закрывается мотивацией; при СДВГ важность задачи не даёт достаточно «досок», поэтому сила воли не работает, а окружающие ошибочно видят лень.

> We don’t always do what we intend to do. Why? Because between intending to do it and actually doing it, there's a big chasm. With most tasks, for most people, that chasm is bridged by motivation. We’ll call this “Motivation Bridge.” And it’s built using motivational planks like “This will be good for my career.” “It will make my family proud.” “I told a bunch of people I would.” Motivation bridge isn’t always perfect, but little gaps in motivation can be overcome with will power. Self-discipline. I don’t want to do the thing, but if it’s important to me, I will. I might not have all the motivation, but I have enough that I can get there. Unfortunately, ADHD is what Dr. Russell Barkley often refers to a “motivation deficit disorder.” Something might be just as important to us as it is to someone without ADHD but that importance doesn’t give us as many motivational planks. We don’t have little gaps in motivation. Half the bridge is missing. Which means we might want to cross that chasm and do the thing we might put a ton of effort into trying to. We just…can’t. This can be really confusing and frustrating for people who can’t see all the missing planks and don’t understand why we don’t just cross the bridge and do the thing. They can get over their gaps, why can’t we get over ours?

**Для Напарника:** Ключевая рамка для текстов без стыда: невозможность начать подаётся как нехватка опор, а не как вина человека.


### Что мотивирует СДВГ: срочное, новое, интересное  ·  [01:57](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=117)
**Мысль:** Мозг СДВГ плохо справляется с длинным, повторяющимся и скучным; зажигают срочность, новизна и личный интерес; игры дают это по умолчанию.

> According to research, ADHD brains tend to have trouble with tasks that are lengthy, repetitive, or boring. On the flip side, there are a few things that give ADHD brains a bunch of motivational planks: Things that are urgent. Things that are new, or novel And things that are of personal interest. In other words, things that are stimulating, or engaging to our brains. Which is also why stimulant medication and exercise can be helpful. This need for stimulation isn’t a choice, it’s how our brains work. Some tasks naturally include these motivational planks -- Video games, anyone? Which is why they hold our focus so well. But for those that don’t, we can add them.

**Для Напарника:** Для каждой механики Напарника проверять, даёт ли она срочность, новизну или интерес; недостающее добавлять.


### Новизна: новая обстановка, новые способы, итерация, геймификация  ·  [03:50](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=230)

> 2. Make it new, or novel We can’t always do new things, but we can do them in new environments or in new ways. Do your homework in a hammock. Learn new ways to fold laundry. I was kinda meh about folding laundry until I learned Marie Kondo’s method, and now it’s kinda fun. If you’re having trouble getting yourself to floss, try getting a variety of floss flavors to choose from. I tend to get bored of my hobbies really quickly, but I’ve learned to accept that once they get old, I will put them down for a while and I’ll cycle back to them when they feel new again. For tasks you have to do a lot, you can iterate on them -- challenge yourself to find easier, better or faster ways to do them. Or weirder ways! Studying might be boring, but what if I had to do it upside down? Gamification can make repetitive tasks novel.

**Для Напарника:** Вариативность в реакциях напарника и редкие находки на острове, чтобы повторяющиеся сессии оставались свежими.

**Мысль:** Важное, но скучное годами не делается, а срочное и эмоциональное делается сразу; «ложь про время» — попытка мозга добыть дофамин.

> ADHD timelines we tell ourselves. This will only take 5 minutes. It doesn't. I've got plenty of time. You don't. I'll do it later. You won't. I work better under pressure. Okay, maybe. But do you feel better when you do it that way? Often times, you're disregulating your nervous system, which doesn't really benefit you in the long run. These lies aren't a sign that we're delusional. They're your brain doing its best to create dopamine where there isn't any or limited access. Because here's the truth. ADHD brains don't respond to importance. They respond to interest. That's why things that are urgent, exciting, or emotionally charged get done fast. And boring but important tasks sit untouched for weeks. And I say weeks, but maybe years, right? We have interestbased nervous systems, not priority based ones.

**Для Напарника:** Делать скучный шаг интересным: игровая обёртка, мини-награда после старта, а не рассказ о важности.

> Then there's the fascinating role of dopamine dysregulation. Your brain's reward system, which relies heavily on dopamine, plays a huge part in motivation. For ADHD brains, dopamine pathways can be dysregulated, meaning it's harder to get that internal kick to start tasks that are perceived as boring, mundane, or lacking immediate stimulation. Dr. Russell Barkley has famously highlighted this, noting that motivation for individuals with ADHD is often driven by novelty, interest, challenge, or extreme urgency, rather than a steady, consistent motivational loop. This means the laundry or that report for work can feel like pulling teeth, while an exciting new project might get your full intense focus.

**Для Напарника:** Старт сессии должен давать новизну/мини-вызов (например, лёгкая игра с таймером), а не просто «надо».


### Это не лень: сила воли конечна, кризис-режим истощает  ·  [06:31](https://youtu.be/nq_olCwfmcc?t=391)
**Мысль:** Сложность старта — нейрологическая; «просто постарайся» не работает, а жизнь на дедлайнах ведёт к выгоранию и плохому образу себя.

> Now, it's crucial to address some common misconceptions. This isn't about laziness. This isn't about a lack of effort. Experts consistently emphasize that task initiation difficulty in ADHD is a neurological performance issue stemming from executive dysfunction and dopamine regulation, not a character flaw. Telling someone with ADHD to just try harder is like telling someone with a broken leg to just run faster. It doesn't address the underlying challenge. The notion that individuals with ADHD should rely solely on willpower is largely refuted by research showing fundamental differences in brain wiring. Willpower is a finite resource, and if your brain isn't producing the right neurochemicals to kickstart action, willpower alone won't get you there consistently. Many adults with ADHD learn to rely on extreme pressure or crisis mode to initiate tasks. While effective in the short term, this constant reliance on urgency leads to increased stress, burnout, and negative self-perception. It's not a sustainable or healthy long-term strategy.

**Для Напарника:** Тон продукта без стыда: никаких «ты не выполнил»; кот не торопит и не подгоняет срочностью.



# 🎬 Alex Hormozi's Advice on Landing Pages — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Уравнение ценности: результат, вероятность, время, усилие  ·  [10:22](https://youtu.be/CNRiNz0YYlM?t=622)
**Мысль:** Ценность растёт с желанным результатом и уверенностью в нём и падает с ростом времени до результата и усилия/жертв. Идеал: мгновенно, без усилий, гарантированно, ровно то, что хочется.

> The value equation has four components, and I'm not going to go super deep on it, but I'll just walk you through it real quick. So, you've got the dream outcome, which is what's the big thing that people want. So, in this instance, it'd be they want to make more money in their gym. They might want to lose weight. They might want to have a fix their relationship. They might want to look cool in front of their friends. The next is the perceived likelihood of achievement or the equal opposite of that is risk. How likely is it that when I buy this thing, I get what I want? Which when he asked the question, well, how likely is it? This is what he's teasing out. Alex Heroszy's value equation is directly correlated with landing page optimization. When we look at the first two items here, the first thing is the dream outcome. What is that dream end state or dream goal that your target audience really wants to achieve? That dream outcome needs to be immediately articulated in your headline and your sub headline above the fold. Do they want to save time? Do they want to make more money? Do they want to close more deals? Whatever it is, it needs to be immediately articulated and we will go through some examples soon. The next part is reducing the perceived risk. Do you have case studies and testimonials to back up what you're saying? Do you have a free trial? Or maybe you have no credit card required if you're a B2B or SAS company. Or maybe you have a return policy if you're an e-commerce firm. Whatever it is, you need to reduce that perceived risk for your customers. The next question is time. How long is it going to take between when I start and when I get? And the longer that is, the less valuable the thing is. And then you have effort and sacrifice, which is how hard am I going to have to try? What am I going to have to start doing that I don't want to do or stop doing that I do want to do? In a hypothetical example, you'd want something that would be immediate, be no effort. It would be guaranteed, and it would be exactly what they want, the way they want it.

**Для Напарника:** Для Напарника: вся ценность в «время до первого результата» и «усилие». Старт сессии — 1 тап, первая награда за пару минут; в тексте лендинга обещать конкретно «через минуту начнёшь».

# 🎬 App Onboarding Best Practices: Why Users Quit Before They Start — Kris Greenwald


### Умный дефолт превращает открытый вопрос в «да или нет»  ·  [06:10](https://youtu.be/lMOMplCvfZA?t=370)
**Мысль:** Плитки рекомендаций (80% просмотров Netflix идут из рекомендаций) заменяют поиск и выбор лёгким согласием.

> I say open Spotify, or in my case, YouTube Music. I don't open this app, search for a song, listen to it, then search for another one, listen to that. Almost nobody does. I tap one of the first tiles on the screen, maybe my liked songs, supermix, something built for what I've already played as a recommendation. Netflix started learning from its initial indecisions and published a paper on their own recommender system. About 80% of hours streamed comes from recommendations now, and like 20% from the search. So, that 80/20 in favor of not deciding, but going with a recommendation. So, what those tiles do is convert an open-ended question into a yes or no. And a yes or a no is mentally cheap on your user. That is your dominant option and the thing that makes choice overload disappear. We call this strategy smart default.

**Для Напарника:** Дом предлагает одну готовую рекомендацию («25 минут, начнём с…») с возможностью поменять, вместо пустого выбора.


### Дефолт воспринимается как совет: автозаписи подняли участие с 37% до 86%  ·  [07:06](https://youtu.be/lMOMplCvfZA?t=426)
**Мысль:** При автозаписи 61% остались на значениях по умолчанию, тогда как среди выбиравших сами тот же вариант взяли 1%; предзаполненное значение читается как социальный сигнал нормы.

> There's a really interesting study on 401(k)s, and this company changed theirs from an opt-in to an automatic enrollment. And from that alone, participation went up from 37% to 86%. But, a statistic that's more useful for us is that the plan had default contribution rates and a default fund. And 61% of the auto-enrolled employees stayed on both. They just accepted those smart defaults. What's interesting about this is that among the employees who had joined the old way, so they opted in themselves and chose for themselves, only about 1% picked that same combination. So, did you catch that? With a smart default, user behavior changed from selecting a choice 1% of the time to 61% of the time. That default didn't just influence a decision, it was the decision. So now, why would that happen? People don't read a default as a placeholder. They read it as advice. Employees describe taking the default as investment guidance. So, a pre-filled value is a social signal. It says, "This is what people like you choose." And we as a people rate that heavily because copying everyone else has been excellent survival strategy for most of human history. So, when you pre-select two guests and 7:00 p.m. for your restaurant, you're not just saving a few taps. You're saying, "This is normal. You're in the right place. You don't have to think about this one.".

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает Default Effect из §4: метка «рекомендуем» на 25 минутах, пока человек сам не изменил выбор.


### Готовые варианты вместо свободного ввода: утро/день/вечер, рекомендованные дни, план до регистрации  ·  [13:36](https://youtu.be/lMOMplCvfZA?t=816)
**Мысль:** Выбор из готовых вариантов легче свободной формы; время суток вместо часов уменьшает нагрузку; выдача недельного плана до входа включает закон взаимности.

> This is much easier to get me continuing. So, I get consistent and then which days work best. So, I'm recommended to run Tuesday, Thursday, Saturday. That seems reasonable. I don't really have to think about my whole schedule and everything. I kind of go with this recommendation. If I truly want to switch some things up, maybe Monday, Wednesday, Friday, it's much easier for me to do that with this kind of selectable interface than maybe most would be. And then, what time do you usually have free? So, instead of giving me all the hours in the day and everything that I could do, all the different time increments and everything, it just gives me morning, afternoon, and evening. Around 7:00, around 12:00, around 6:30. Very easy, very welcoming, and I can just select one of these. I'd like to run in the morning. That's easy. And now we can see I already have some value from this. I have a whole week one plan. So, I can get started without even needing to sign into this application yet. That makes me much more likely to want to use this software because of the law of reciprocity when it comes to humans. So, once we get something, we want to give something. That's how we work.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-ин: «когда обычно легче начать?» с тремя чипами (утро, день, вечер) и дефолтом; сразу показать что-то ценное в ответ.

**Мысль:** Пройти по повестке дня и спросить, что запомнилось из каждого блока: люди сами создают хайлайты, и общее ощущение фиксируется.

> Tip number one is to end with a highlight session so what this means is generally you just put up the agenda for the day that you just finished just put it on a board put it on a screen whatever just write up these are the things we did for today then simply point at parts of the agenda so for example if you ran the expert interview session in the morning simply point at that and say hey this morning we started with expert interviews what's something you remember what's something you enjoyed what's something that changed when you did this exercise and you ask the group and always someone will put off their hand and say actually you know for me it was really interesting to see that i was able to you know get all my ideas out without talking and so what you've done is you've had someone in the group create a highlight for you and it feels good in the group then i'll go to the next section okay we did the math what's something positive that someone remembers from the map what's something that stood out for you what's something that you know was different from how you normally work someone put up their hand and they'll say hey this is what i remember from it and the cool thing is by the time you get to the end of the agenda people in the audience or your participants have basically highlighted peaks and interesting points during the workshop for you right and for everybody else and this leaves everybody going home with a really good vibe of the highlights and the memories and interesting things that happened during the workshop and it also allows people who had special experiences during certain moments it basically allows them to talk about their peak which maybe other people didn't have and that basically gives other people the experience of oh yeah there was this cool moment that happened there and it really locks in some really nice memories at the end of the workshop.

**Для Напарника:** Вечерняя сводка на Доме: кот перечисляет сделанные шаги дня и спрашивает, что понравилось, не стыдя за несделанное.


### Показать прогресс «было/стало»: пустая комната и полная  ·  [05:35](https://youtu.be/So8aseCO3hY?t=335)
**Мысль:** Фото пустой комнаты до и полной после, скриншот пустой доски и итоговой показывают, как далеко ушли; рождает гордость и ощущение закрытия, а не затухание.

> This is really you showing the progress that happened in the workshop we do this in a couple of ways if we're doing an in-person workshop we'll take a photo of the empty room before the workshop started and the empty room with the people in it before the workshop actually started the walls are empty there's nothing there nothing has happened yet then we'll take a picture of the full room with all of the workshop materials and i'll simply show that on a slide at the end of the workshop and i'll say when we got here this morning how did you feel you know what did you think was going to happen you know we talked about what happened before and i talked to them like what were your expectations then i showed them the picture at the end of the day and i really highlight how far they've come when you came here today you had all of these broad ideas right now you have something tangible you're moving forward you all did amazing work here's what the room looks like here's what the day looked like so i essentially take pictures in an in-person workshop or i take screenshots of the miro board for a remote workshop and i show them that at the end here's what the mural board looked like just three hours ago we had nothing now look how much progress we've made look how much we've moved forward it's a really great way to lock in the feeling of progression at the end of the day and give people the feeling of pride that what they've created is something special it's a great way to like put a cap on the end of the day because it really should feel like closure it shouldn't just fade out because sometimes the last exercise in the day can be a tough one you really need to close down the workshop properly and showing the progress that people have made like the previous step asking them for the highlights but doing it visually is another fantastic way to end stronger.

> The feeling of success is what wires in the habit yeah it's not repetition it is a feeling of emotions great habits and specifically in tiny habits what we focus on is the feeling of success so when you do something and feel successful that behavior becomes much more automatic you're more likely to do it in the future so if people can do a behavior like two push-ups or you know eat broccoli or any little thing and feel successful then that behavior will become more automatic and there's ways to hack that and help yourself create habits very quickly very quickly if you know how to fire off a positive emotion the other thing that the feeling of success does is it motivates you to do it again in the future that's different than wiring in a habit into your brain so that's you know effect number two is increases your motivation and the third effect there's more but i'll stop here i call it success momentum as people do behaviors and feel successful their confidence you could call it self-efficacy increases and that means when they hit roadblocks or little bumps in the road they can get through them that's why i like calling it success momentum they can get through it and with that increased confidence with that success momentum then they can tackle other challenges in their life more effectively.

### Мотивация колеблется, поэтому низкая планка + успех = идентичность «я могу меняться»  ·  [05:17](https://youtu.be/EAQlXfX4Lnc?t=317)
**Мысль:** Планка, достижимая при низкой мотивации, создаёт ощущение успеха, он повышает мотивацию, и человек делает больше; это переходит в идентичность.

> Wow okay well let me start with you know his ambition to do this huge workout so when often when people decide that they're going to change their behavior they're in a high state of motivation so if he's sitting down with you in the clinical setting he's feeling motivated in that moment and then he truly believes he can do these hard workouts what he doesn't and what we as human beings don't account for very well is what's going to happen to our motivation in the future and the motivation goes up and down over time and so you know three days later his motivation sags he's not able to do the hard work out what you did so brilliantly is then you matched him with a really simple exercise routine that didn't require much time or money or physical effort and you set the bar really low and so if something's really easy to do it doesn't require high levels of motivation can be high or relatively low he can still do it and you were just right on to say just do it two times a week so that was the bar now what he did was he exceeded that and you set him up to succeed not fail you set him up that the bar was low enough he could do it so he felt successful then he did even more so now he's feeling like a superstar he's like the a plus student he's the student in the front row of the class that knows all the answers and so that then helps him wire in the habit and it helps him increase his motivation which means he can do more and harder things and i imagine as he was doing more than five minutes twice a week every time he did it he's like oh my gosh when i go back to the doctor i get to tell him this and so on and then that becomes part of his life and very importantly part of his identity he starts thinking i'm the kind of person that does strength training and more generally i'm the kind of person who can change.

**Для Напарника:** Низкая планка на старте и отсутствие наказания; остров как зримая идентичность «я человек, который делает».

> In my research i see this week after week in tiny habits week after week about 75 of the people who do tiny habits report doing other behaviors besides the three new habits that they designed at the beginning and this is within one week so they start doing other behaviors about 18 of people do a big change within those five days not one that was planned not that one that was part of the tiny habits program or what they thought they would do but change leads to change and success leads to success and this dynamic of you do something and feel successful your identity shifts and then that naturally ripples out and affects other behaviors in your life and i didn't in the first i would say three years of doing tiny habits i saw the data and i was like wow this is really interesting these ripple effects are there i get it but what about these big leap changes that people are making this 18 i didn't totally understand that until i started asking some other questions in the research and i did see clearly that as people do something and feel successful it shifts their identity in these positive ways and then they naturally start doing other related healthy behaviors.

**Для Напарника:** Крошечные победы — достаточное ядро продукта; не нужны большие планы.

When that loop breaks and people start completing things they've avoided for months or even years, self-esteem recovers quickly. And self-esteem is load-bearing for almost everything else.

**Для Напарника:** Главная цель продукта — разорвать петлю стыда: любой завершённый микрошаг отмечается теплом, пропуск — нулём, не минусом.

# 🎬 Comment RÉUSSIR l'onboarding de vos applications :  ASTUCES et TUTORIEL complet — Lucas & Nathan Simula


### Самооценка уровня ненадёжна: измеряй поведением и подстраивай сложность  ·  [05:04](https://youtu.be/9QmD5wcHobg?t=304)
**Мысль:** Оценка «от 1 до 5» субъективна; лучше дать пробные задания и по скорости и точности подобрать уровень, это ощущается прогрессом.

> A mistake to avoid is asking for their level just like that. Example of who is, who is, sorry, self-assessing. If I tell you, what’s your level of Japanese between one and five stars, well, that’s useless, you see. Because you have one guy, he learned Japanese 8 years ago, he’s going to say, "Well, I’ve got two stars." The other one, he went to Japan for a week and he’s going to say, "Oh, it’s good, I managed to ask for a dish from a waitress, I’ve got three stars," you see? And in the end, the experience will be terrible later because you’re going to push content that isn't adapted at all. The best solution, since they have time, once again, they have time, so you can add slightly longer onboarding screens, you can push content to them. For example, test them ; actually, you send them a simple question, a translation to do. In Japanese with three choices. If, for example, they get it right, you adjust. Well, they got it right, so you give them a slightly more difficult question . They got it right, but they still took 3 minutes to answer, right? That means they might have had some difficulties. I'll push content that's maybe a little simpler, or a little harder than the first question, but less so than if they had answered quickly. If they get it wrong, I'll offer simpler content and I'll do that for 4, 5, 6, 7 questions. I have no idea how many questions are needed for my algorithm to have a sort of assessment to then push the right content. And doing that will allow them to get that addictive quality and feel like they're progressing, that they have something at their level.

**Для Напарника:** Не спрашивать человека, сколько он может фокусироваться; предлагать длительность по реальным прошлым сессиям и мягко подстраивать (не наказывая за недотянутое).

> Yeah so and sometimes the scope boils down to triggers of course we know the italysis framework and white hat is you feel good but there's no urgency and there's and there's blackhead that creates urgency right and so this is a typical standard like literature type question because you know a lot of times we want to do things that are important but not urgent which usually are the white hat activities and you know improving yourself training growth when there's no urgent need right because it's not just self-improvement there's an urgent need that means if you don't do it you'll feel miserably you'll have consequences that goes back to the loss and avoid its blackhead so usually even General self-improvement is a white hat component which means again there's no urgency and the thing is that well how do we get people to do more of this and it's hard to do it because always surrounding them are these black hat triggers right they are they are the deadlines and then there's the scarcity the exclusive you know you have to do this in the next two hours for the opportunity whatnot or it's very exclusive you feel special or unpredictability it's actually more interesting so I think I talked about one point before probably with Michael is there's two scenarios of one is just that you absolutely just have zero time to do the important things right it's just a almost a mathematical problem you just don't have that many hours you have to do the things you need immediately and after that you have zero time and then there are questions about okay how to re-prioritize your time which is you know just about motivating them to do more of this and so theoretically if the problem is you want them to do white hat activities but there's too many black hat triggers there's only two ways to resolve that number one is to remove the other black hat triggers and they know and the second one is to add black hat triggers to the white hat activity.

**Для Напарника:** Для людей с СДВГ срочное всегда побеждает; Напарник может мягко убирать шум (один шаг на экране) вместо давления страхом.

> Your bot should focus not on features but on the benefit of the benefit right like the example with the raincoat is an example of the person trying to find out what the weather is that's the benefit of the benefit of that in the context of that question and if they decide to buy a raincoat they're buying it not because they want to own a raincoat but because it's raining outside and it's and they're not gonna get wet so it's gonna keep them dry right so that you know that's why like you know people in alaska aren't buying bikinis right you need it's it's you need to focus on the benefit of the benefit when it comes to your to your bot and then last but not least is you have to consider the emotion of your user so every interaction that a person has with your bot is going to do some sort of thoughts and feelings within that person so as you're designing your bot you need to have empathy and put yourself in that user shoes and see what those thoughts corresponding thoughts and feelings you're going to be like and when you do that then you can better predict what they're most likely to say or do next.

# 🎬 Cozy Games Need an Edge — auranym


### Отсутствие контроля делает мир живым  ·  [10:10](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=610)
**Мысль:** Нельзя выбирать планировку города или кто переедет: ощущение, что мир живёт с тобой и без тебя, и ты лишь часть его.

> There are plenty of things that can happen that are completely outside of the player's control. There's no control over the town layout, the villagers moving in or away, even what your character looks like. It's abrasive, and some could say it doesn't respect the player. But I personally find that the opposite is true. While it can take this abrasiveness a bit far at times, I think the lack of control adds to the feeling that you're living in a world that's alive. You have agency over yourself, and you choose how to live in a world that moves around you, in a world that lives and breathes with or without you, and where you're just a part of it. That is the magic of Animal Crossing to me. making the most of what you have, the neighbors you meet, the furniture you find, the time of day and year. Living in a town where you participate, can live in, but not dictate.

**Для Напарника:** Остров может сам удивлять небольшими событиями (редкая находка, гость у костра), которые игрок не контролирует, но принимает.


### Неидеальный мир, который ты улучшаешь, делает уют значимым  ·  [13:16](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=796)
**Мысль:** Мусор на карте и свалка в Animal Crossing и Stardew создают чувство, что мир далёк от совершенства, а игрок на нём что-то меняет; город стартует с низкой точки.

> It's a pretty scrappy experience, honestly. And it helps sell the feeling that you're just barely getting by despite the coziness. I really think that the scrappiness, though, helps sell the coziness. It makes it feel more real and meaningful. Stardew Valley is pretty filthy as well, at least at the start of the game. There's trash found everywhere, like in Animal Crossing, especially the southernmost point of Cindersap Forest where the sewer drips green sludge into the ocean. And you can also find old CDs manufactured by Joja all over the map, the same company that's trying to turn the community center into a warehouse. All of this again adds to the feeling that the world is far from perfect and adds to the feeling that you, as the new farmer, are doing something about it. It adds some depth by establishing the town at a low point.

**Для Напарника:** Остров стартует «бедным и пустым» (без стыда), а рост видимо улучшает мир: так прогресс виден контрастом.



# 🎬 Creating Our Duolingo Characters • Greg Hartman • Duocon 2020 — Duolingo


### Персонаж как «мысленный мяч»: меньше трудности, больше цели  ·  [01:33](https://youtu.be/m-3-D7S0piw?t=93)
**Мысль:** Аналогия со спортом: с мячом и соревнованием думаешь меньше о физическом усилии. Персонаж и мир делают долгий трудный путь приятным.

> One of the biggest reasons we're adding characters is because we believe characters can play a large role in motivating people. A lot of games we play internally have great characters and those characters help to keep us engaged. At Duolingo we love the benefits that games have on our brain health. I think of this the same way sports benefit our physical health. Sometimes it's hard to just get up and run around your yard, but once you introduce a ball and healthy competition, you think less about how physically hard it is, and more about the ball and the goal. Because let's face it, language journeys can sometimes be long and difficult. So Duolingo strives to introduce that mental ball to help keep you motivated. And characters can help with that, by dropping an audience into a world that they enjoy spending time in.

**Для Напарника:** Кот и остров как «мяч» сессии Фокус: внимание переключается с усилия на присутствие напарника и рост острова.



# 🎬 Dark Patterns: Are Your Games Playing You? — Chris Wilson


### Endowed Progress: бесплатный старт трека толкает довести его до конца  ·  [07:20](https://youtu.be/OCkO8mNK3Gg?t=440)
**Мысль:** Подаренный прогресс создаёт обязательство завершить трек, похожее на эффект невозвратных затрат.

> There's also a phenomenon called the Endowed Progress Effect, which shows that if people are given a head start on a reward track, then they are far more likely to finish the rest of that track so that they don't waste the progress they have so far, even though they didn't even make that progress themselves. It's a bit like the sunk cost fallacy. This is why some battle pass schemes start with the first items already completed, or have some really easy ones that are completed inadvertently while playing. This manipulates players into committing to complete a reward track that they may not have chosen to attempt without the bump of free progress at the start.

**Для Напарника:** Осторожно: использовать только честно (например, уже посаженное деревце на острове как приветствие), без скрытого давления; важно, чтобы это не вызывало вины.

# 🎬 Designing AI Allies - How Games Create Great Party Members and Companions ~ Design Doc — Design Doc


### Микроменеджмент приемлем, когда он заявлен как часть игры (gambits в FFXII)  ·  [07:11](https://youtu.be/tIze-mre0ss?t=431)
**Мысль:** Одна и та же ручная настройка раздражает в одной игре и радует в другой: решает ожидание игрока.

> Just make the AI yourself, you should try Final Fantasy XII. The gambit system in FFXII lets you program exactly the steps, or ‘gambits’, that your AI party members will take in battle in a kind of Lego brick sort of way. For example, you can set a gambit to have a character cast healing spells whenever someone’s HP drops below a set threshold, or automatically cast Haste on the party whenever you can. More advanced and specific conditions for gambits can be found in shops as the game progresses. You also have to consider the priorities of your gambits, placing more situational instructions at the top and common ones at the bottom. If you have attacking nearby enemies as a top priority, the other gambits will never be used. It's not that far off in concept from what an actual AI programmer might code, but this way you feel like you’re in control if the AI screws something up. Even though the gambit system needs micromanaging like in Kingdom Hearts the framing is completely different. Managing gambits is a huge part of the game, so it's not as frustrating as the micromanaging was in Kingdom Hearts. Players expect it, so they’re OK with putting in the time.

**Для Напарника:** Настройки Напарника скрыты по умолчанию; кому нужно, найдёт, и не будет раздражаться.

> We do micro leaderboards because the thing is about leaderboard is that most of time there's only you know 10 winners and like a thousand losers right and so if we want to ensure there's core drive to develop an accomplishment we want to make sure there's the max amount of time people can feel accomplished like they feel like they're a winner so first of all we do micro leaderboards you know you there's all these way you can break it down by this week compared to only our stats met status members only for core drive three and so with all these variations there's over 70 micro leader boards but then the thing that i believe we're the only company in the world that do this thus far is we have an intelligent algorithm that figures out every what leaderboard people look best on and so when they open the leaderboard instead of say hey these are the all-time top 10 people that are going to make you depressed because you only have 2 000 and they have 8 million we'll show you hey this is a one of the micro leader boards that you're number five on you are you capture the most geo mods this week among the blue members and so the idea is that the moment they open the leaderboard they feel accomplished well i didn't even know that i didn't know i was this good in geo months this week and so they can take a screenshot they can share they can brag about it so instead of just throwing all the data there and allowing people to just explore and find places to feel accomplished and most likely they're going to see people way better that makes them feel sad we show them hey this is how you can feel accomplished now of course if they want to then go to the all-time leader board and look at the super high level users that make them feel sad that's up to them but the idea is that we want to maximize times people feel accomplished.

**Для Напарника:** Принцип «показывай, где человек сильнее»: на Доме и в Мире выделять личные рекорды и «лучший день недели», а не общее сравнение.



# 🎬 Designing Through Uncertainty: Craft, AI, and Discomfort | Hugo Raymond | Layers 2025 — Friends of Figma, Portugal


### Эксперимент с фотографами: количество приводит к качеству  ·  [06:11](https://youtu.be/-uC_TbYYhK4?t=371)
**Мысль:** Группу, оцениваемую по числу снимков, результат получился лучше, чем у группы, оцениваемой по одному идеальному: те много теоретизировали и получили один посредственный кадр.

> Gabriel Valdivia, who helps early stage companies, provided a helpful approach which he believes can benefit organizations of any scale. His core directive progress, not perfection, is the engine of innovation. Maybe not a huge surprise, but the insight came not from valuing quality over quantity, but understanding that quantity is often how we get to quality. He gave the example of an experiment conducted by Jerry Uelsmann, a photographer and teacher who split his students into two groups for an activity. You may have heard of this one before. Group A would be graded based on the total quality of the photos that they took, whilst Group B only had to produce a single photo and that alone would be based on quality. Group A, who were tasked with taking as many photos as possible. Naturally spent more time in the practice, which yielded far greater results than the team asked to focus on quality. They spent too much time theorizing and were left with a single mediocre shot.

**Для Напарника:** Близко к принципу Напарника: маленькие шаги и много коротких стартов важнее одного идеального; можно использовать в текстах про прощение несовершенства.

> Win number three is the companion character. So, in the prompt, I did ask that there is going to be a sort of character in the app that's going to guide users through their whole journey of completing all of these tasks or completing their projects, and it created this character called Pip the Crane, which I assume is going to react contextually, and it's going to have different expressions at different moments in the flow. and I think that's a great emotional design working very well. **Для Напарника:** MascotSvg: выражения привязаны к моментам потока (начало, середина, финал, возврат после паузы).


### Дай править разбивку от ИИ: контроль повышает доверие и вовлечённость  ·  [09:08](https://youtu.be/_5EMOZGY9yg?t=548)
**Мысль:** Когда ИИ решает, а человек принимает, это неверная динамика. Даже мелкий контроль (править, убирать, добавлять) делает человека вовлечённым, а разбивку — подходящей его реальности.

> My concern is it's very much, you know, AI deciding and user just accepting it. And for this I think that's the wrong power dynamics. So, I want to add a layer of user control over here. Let them edit the levels, remove what doesn't fit them and add something specific if they need to and reorder if they want to. Just give them a the ability to make it theirs before they can commit to it. So, why? The reason is when people feel in control of their experience, even in like tiniest ways, they're more likely to trust it, engage with it, and come back to it. And if the AI hands you a breakdown and you have no say in it, you're just a passive recipient. But if you can tweak it, then you become really invested in it. And there's also a persona personalization argument. AI will make assumptions based on the prompt, but every user's context is a little bit different. So, giving users ability to edit means the breakdown actually fits their own reality and not just the AI's interpretation of it.

**Для Напарника:** После api/breakdown дать лёгкие инлайн-правки шагов (переименовать, удалить, добавить) перед стартом, без «режима редактирования».

**Мысль:** Достаточно одного сильного драйва, но на годы нужна их смесь. Игры на чёрной шляпе дают отличные метрики в короткую, но люди выгорают и уходят с плохим вкусом. Игры на внутренней мотивации держат без очков.

> First of all you only need one coordinate that's really strong you know drive out of behavior of only one of metaphor if you hear in any given one point of time however if you want an engaging experience or the long run you know one year three years some games people are addicted here for 10 plus years generally speaking you will see a combination a variety of those core drives but some games are more heavily focusing with a white hat for Drive which are tribes that make people feel powerful in control they feel good so there's no sense of urgency some games focus more on black hats for drugs and makes or urgent assess even addicted so the law in the short run all their metrics look amazing like Farmville game but in the long run people it leaves a bad taste in people's mouth because they feel like they're not in charge of their own behavior so then they burn out and leave then some games focus or on extrinsic motivations the things you do for reward a purpose or goal so the hey here's a new challenge collect all this in a level up to 100 but the activity itself is gameplay itself may or may not be fun and so one people hit the milestones or no the bad is no longer interesting then they stop doing the behavior whereas other games focus on intrinsic motivation so things that they just enjoy doing like there's no point there's no stages is no progress every time you play you know when you're done you lose all progress so people still love playing it because the gameplay itself is so engaging.

**Для Напарника:** Опираться на белую шляпу (смысл, творчество, мастерство, остров как ощущение роста), а чёрные драйверы использовать минимально и без вины.


### Геймификация это не цифровые очки, а то, что вызывает драйвы  ·  [07:37](https://youtu.be/6AEK6S_0nic?t=457)
**Мысль:** Игра может быть без технологий (прятки): важно, вызывает ли механика ощущение достижения. Баллы и значки без чувства успеха бесполезны, а слова учителя «ты в победившем классе» работают.

> Gamification really just understanding how the brain responds to stimuli right and how and everything else we call them Steve a mechanic to deliver that motivation so just like games could be World of Warcraft which has a lot of technologies but it can also be hide and seek right no technology and the female mechanic they're just basically other kids keeping score and last time I checked you know kids have World of Warcraft's but they also enjoy playing hide and seek so you know a lot of solutions I design for organizations or universities or clients you know are our offline solutions sometimes just a poster on the wall sometimes it's just flashcards sometimes just a policy change you know or just a game rule the key is that does it bring out those eight four drives right you could have a digital game with a lot of fat points and badges but people don't feel accomplished but you can have a teacher just say hey great job you are among the winning class right and then suddenly you feel accomplished so again does it have to be digital it as long as it really brings out those four drugs.

**Для Напарника:** Ценность награды в ощущении (тёплая реплика кота, рост острова), а не в числе. Реплика напарника важнее очков.


### Внутренняя мотивация: творчество, выбор, социальное признание, любопытство  ·  [09:30](https://youtu.be/6AEK6S_0nic?t=570)
**Мысль:** Вместо апгрейдов и наказаний делай саму деятельность интереснее: осмысленный выбор, обратная связь, приятная неожиданность. Тогда человек делает это потому что нравится и вкладывается больше.

> But the key about education is it really needs to be intrinsic motivation so core Drive three and count up creates a new feedback giving people meaningful choices strategy giving them autonomy core i5 social influence of relatedness so to collaborate like and edit a play feeling a lot of social appreciation and chord Estevan hum project building curiosity so every time they do the activity it needs to be delightfully surprising you know there's always you know they look forward to what's going to happen next and so instead of thinking about upgrades or scores or punishment you want to make learning fun again you want to make that tube itself more interesting and when you can build that into the learning environment and then kids are you know they're not doing it just to pass the class they're doing it because they enjoyed so much and of course when you enjoy something you spend you exert yourself more and you get way more out of it.

**Для Напарника:** Сессия Фокуса даёт выбор (длительность, шаг) и приятные неожиданности (реплика, находка), а не только очки и таймер.
