**Для Напарника:** Тяжёлый запуск задачи упаковывать в мягкую метафору («шаг для кота», «подбросить дров к костру»), а не в сухую формулировку.



# 🎬 Executive Function, Productivity & ADHD: Essential Strategies, Tools and Systems — ADHD Support Talk


### Без плана день диктует то, что попало в поле внимания  ·  [07:14](https://youtu.be/Cw2uijd2SOY?t=434)
**Мысль:** Планирование нелюбимо: одни перепланируют, другие избегают; но без него остаётся реактивность и тушение пожаров. Назвать можно как угодно.

> Most of my clients don't love the word planning. Most of us with ADD have sort of a love-hate relationship with planning. Some of us are over-planners, so we burn ourselves up, we use, you know, sort of use all of our energy up with making the perfect plan. Others of us find it really hard to sit down and force ourselves to plan. But, when you're determining what action to take when, that's a form of planning. So, if you can call it whatever you want, I actually call it setting myself up for the next day. I have clients that just call it the deciding, and that's okay, right? But, that form of planning is an important thing for us to do. If we're not planning in some way, again, no matter what you call it, if you're not planning in some way, you're letting whatever comes into your attention direct your action. So, you're reactive, you're your whole day is dictated by email, your whole day is dictated by whatever comes into your attention, or whatever sort of fires need to be put out in the moment. And that's not proactive, that's not moving anything forward, that's just basically treading water and keeping up. And most of us do a lot of that.

**Для Напарника:** Назвать планирование мягко («настроить завтра», «набросок на день»), не «план»; не требовать идеального.



# 🎬 Finch App Review - Your Digital Best Friend (Mental Wellbeing App) — Holistic Healing Choices


### Микро-цели для тяжёлых дней: малые победы складываются  ·  [02:24](https://youtu.be/7opbdtb9XgQ?t=144)
**Мысль:** Для плохих дней в Finch есть крошечные задачи (выйти на улицу, встать на 10 секунд, посмотреть в зеркало), чтобы день всё равно засчитался.

> These ones sort of get you to just do the little things. So, little wins add up, which I really am a fan of these apps including in here. There's like things like step outside once, stand up for 10 seconds, look in the mirror and say I can do it, and literally survive the day.

**Для Напарника:** Для Дома: мягкий вариант «сегодня минимум» (один крошечный шаг вместо плана), в голосе напарника, без вины за пустой день.


### Привязанность к питомцу: милота, выражения и чувство заботы  ·  [09:49](https://youtu.be/7opbdtb9XgQ?t=589)
**Мысль:** Питомец выбран так, чтобы его было трудно не любить; мелкие выражения создают ощущение, что ты о ком-то заботишься и помогаешь ему расти.

> And just on that, sending him on his little adventures, you get that little choice at the end of it on the how he grows and how he develops. And of course you want the best for him because he's, you know, cute enough not to hate. If anybody has a problem with him, you probably just hate birds in general. It's really well designed in that way that he gets you to like him. And there's enough little expressions on him and everything else as well to be able to give you that sense of that you're actually caring for something, that it is actually something that you're taking care of, you're helping him develop cuz he's only a new little hatchling.

**Для Напарника:** Кот как объект заботы: выражения и рост острова усиливают чувство «я забочусь о нём», но без наказания за пропуск.


### Награда за базовые вещи отвлекает фокус от проблем  ·  [12:26](https://youtu.be/7opbdtb9XgQ?t=746)
**Мысль:** Приложение помогает людям с тревогой: мягкое внимание на питомце и награда за простое (встать с кровати, меньше TikTok) смещают фокус.

> I personally believe that it would be helpful for the right people because it would take their focus away from some of their issues then and there while you're sending this little guy on an adventure as well as giving you those little basic things that you might miss as well as some that are very important like expressing gratitude towards people who you love, getting organizing and stuff with your friends and just that sort of stuff in general. And you can add as much as you like or as little as you like. And I think the quality of what they are, so using less YouTube, using less TikTok, being less distracted, focusing on this, at least just getting out of bed or literally just surviving the day, you get rewarded for.

**Для Напарника:** Награждать за малое (начал шаг, вернулся) в голосе кота, не только за результат.



# 🎬 Game Loops: Designing the Heartbeat of your Game — Vertical Nice: Game Design with Chillen


### Игра - решение задач; удовольствие во многом от обучения  ·  [00:33](https://youtu.be/XA1F8r1D8eg?t=33)
**Мысль:** Игра ставит цель и инструменты, учит ими пользоваться; радость игр - это радость обучения.

> A game is a problem-solving activity entered with a playful attitude when we play games the game presents us with a goal and some tools and then it tries to teach us how to use those tools to get what we want it's not far-fetched to say that a lot of the fun of games is really the fun of learning.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус как маленькая задача: цель, инструмент (таймер, шаг) и ощущение, что научился делать.


### Игровой цикл: ментальная модель, цель, действие, правила, обратная связь, обновление  ·  [05:08](https://youtu.be/XA1F8r1D8eg?t=308)
**Мысль:** Игрок исходит из модели, ставит цель, выбирает действие, правила мира реагируют последовательно, обратная связь обновляет модель. Последовательность правил укрепляет уверенность в обучаемости мира.

> Okay so what is the game loop then the game loop much like the loop involved with the scientific method is all about solving problems it's about learning skills it's about being presented with questions and doing your best with the tools provided to answer them sufficiently the idea of the game loop is very well described by spryfox co-founder dan cook's 2012 essay loops and arcs available on his lost guarded blog and also in his igda seattle talk game design theory i wish i knew we start with a mental model and what do we know about this game and things related to this game what goals do we have in mind in terms of the scientific method what's our question and what's our hypothesis maybe we're fighting a boss in a zelda game with a big yellow eye and based on our experience with previous zelda games we can predict that hitting the boss in the big yellow eye will deal the most damage maybe we're playing a puzzle game with ice cubes on an icy floor and we know from real life that ice is slippery and we might be able to push them around once we have a goal we instinctively predict which actions might be available to us and choose an action which we think is going to bring us closer to that goal no way nope okay victoria because this is our own world the next step is to have the rules of the game take place it's important for us as designers to consider this step because we're the ones designing the cause and effect of actions we want our actions to behave consistently to reinforce a strong mental model and boost the player's confidence that our game world really is learnable finally the all crucial feedback we give the player some kind of data that they can analyze and use to update their mental model this could be in the form of rewards like coins points or access to new areas or it could be in the form of evocative stimuli like animations or sound effects but of course this is a loop once we have our feedback this updates our mental model we have a better understanding of the game's rules and we can either try again at our goal or we can formulate a new goal and take actions towards that.

**Для Напарника:** Использовать цикл как чек-лист для Фокуса: цель ясна -> действие -> согласованная реакция кота/острова -> награда, обновляющая ожидания.



# 🎬 Gameplay Loops Are Out, Chains Are In — Indie Game Clinic


### Цепочка действий нарастает эмоционально  ·  [02:56](https://youtu.be/6SYX17NzNqE?t=176)
**Мысль:** В комбо каждое успешное действие повышает ставки следующего; ошибка на четвёртом-пятом шаге ощущается сильнее, чем на первом. У цепочки есть эмоциональная дуга и нарастающая вовлечённость.

> I think chains in games are interesting because of how they map on to emotion. And the most simple chain that I can think of that most people would probably think of if you said a chain of actions in a game would be like executing a combo in a fighting game. >> When you commit to doing a specific set or string of actions in a particular order, what happens is every one that you successfully complete raises the stakes about what's coming next. If you're trying to complete five actions in a row and you get the first or second one wrong, you're going to feel a bit bummed out. Oh no. When you're getting wrong on the fourth or fifth one, that is when it starts to feel like an epic fail. Oh, I almost had it. There is a emotional arc to a chain. You're not button bashing. You're trying to hit a very specific list of things in a specific order. It creates this rising sense of emotional investment with each thing you do.

**Для Напарника:** Очередь шагов (Zeigarnik) использует эту механику; осторожно: обрыв поздней цепочки больнее, поэтому нужны мягкие реплики кота при срыве.


### Ценность цепочек: сбор ресурса значим из-за цели  ·  [04:03](https://youtu.be/6SYX17NzNqE?t=243)
**Мысль:** Подбор палок сам по себе не весел; смысл ему даёт то, что игрок планирует с ними сделать и как это служит эмоциональной фантазии игры (Dan Cook).

> There is an essay by game designer Dan Cook. I will link it in the description. You should go and read it. It is about value chains. If you've got a economic chain in a game, like you collect these things and you use them for this. It's the what you use them for that gives the collection meaning and makes you feel something about it. So the example that Dan gives is picking up sticks is not inherently fun. You can make it as fun as you can. You can make it feel really toyike and they could be really sparkly sticks or whatever, but it's not an inherently fun activity. It's not enough to carry a game. What makes the activity enjoyable is what the player plans to do with the sticks and how that fulfills the like core fantasy of the game, the core emotional thing that the game is pulling at.

**Для Напарника:** Сессия Фокуса значима, когда видна цель: монетки или энергия идут на конкретное развитие острова.


### Конечная цель делает смысловым каждое действие в пути  ·  [04:55](https://youtu.be/6SYX17NzNqE?t=295)
**Мысль:** В Fallout 4 строительство превратило хлам в осмысленные ресурсы, и решения «что поднять» стали богаче. Понимание цели делает сбор интереснее.

> Another really good example of this would be there was a little bit of crafting in Fallout 3 and then in Fallout New Vegas there was a little bit more crafting and then in Fallout 4 you're now like the mayor of Boston and you need to rebuild Boston using twigs and super glue. There are people that don't like those base building mechanics. quite possible to play the game while ignoring those mechanics. But my experience of it was personally being able to build stuff out of the junk in the game changed everything. It meant that objects that previously were just a piece of junk to be collected and sold and maybe you would think a little bit about like the cost to weight ratio of different objects as you picked them up, they were now imbued with a different sort of meaning because now I'm trying to build the lighting for my 1950s Hawaiian Aloha bar and I need to look out for items that have copper wiring in them. So, I'm learning which fans and telephones have copper wiring in them. the destination of the chain that we know we're working towards changes the feel of engaging with mechanics earlier on because knowing that I'm trying to rebuild Boston with twigs and glue means that every time I go into a place and I'm looting it, the decision space of what do I pick up, what do I leave behind is suddenly richer because I'm not just taking every gun to sell at the merchant because that has the best weight to cost ratio or something like that. having a kind of forward destination for these resources I'm collecting. The action of collecting the stuff becomes more meaningful and interesting and exciting to do because the player understands what they are trying to achieve by doing it.

**Для Напарника:** Показывать, к какой постройке ведёт текущий шаг или сессия, чтобы малые действия получали смысл.


### Два вида цепочек: явная цель и скрытое открытие  ·  [09:09](https://youtu.be/6SYX17NzNqE?t=549)
**Мысль:** Явные цепочки определяются мотивацией построить нечто; скрытые дают чувство «ага, я сам догадался».

> You can get clear chains where players know where they're going and the feeling of the chain is defined by the player's motivation to build a particular thing. Or you can get these weird secret chains where the player feels smart because aha now I realized that I was actually making the fairies spawn by.

**Для Напарника:** Сочетать явные цели (следующий ориентир) и тихие скрытые находки на острове.



# 🎬 Gamification to improve our world: Yu-kai Chou at TEDxLausanne — TEDx Talks


### Геймификация — не очки/бейджи/лидерборды, а мотивация через базовые драйвы  ·  [01:38](https://youtu.be/v5Qjuegtiyc?t=98)
**Мысль:** Элементы игры сами по себе не делают игру весёлой (большинство игр скучны); сначала нужно понять, какие драйвы мотивируют человека.

> So what is gamification? Gamification is the craft to take all those fun and exciting elements of games and pouring them into boring non-game context. Things you have to do, but you don't necessarily want to do. So when I say gamification, some of you might start thinking about what we call the PBLs, the points, badges, and leaderboards. And this is where a lot of people think, "Oh, if I just take points and put into a product or badges, it'll make it fun and exciting." And that's a big misconception. When I started in gamification in 2003, it was a very lonely passion for me. Very few people cared about it and very few people understood it. Now about 3-4 years ago, gamification became a buzzword and a lot more people and companies, organizations, schools started to care about it. But still very few people truly understand it. So if you think about what makes a game fun, if you ask gamers that, they're not going to say, "Oh, because the game has points." They'll say, "Oh, because it challenges me, it makes me use my creativity, it allows me to hang with friends, it makes me be more than who I am today." And if you think about it, every single game out there has game elements and game mechanics in them. But most games are still boring. Most games are still not engaging. So it's very naive to think that, "Oh, if I just take these game elements and mechanics from that you can find in games that are even boring and pour them into products and experiences, it'll automatically make it super fun and exciting." So what we know here is that good gamification does not start with game elements, but really starts with how it motivates our core drives.

**Для Напарника:** Остров — это не набор очков, а ощущение роста и принадлежности; избегать PBL-шаблона.


### Epic Meaning: Pain Squad — ты часть команды, а не пациент с дневником  ·  [03:52](https://youtu.be/v5Qjuegtiyc?t=232)
**Мысль:** Рамка «секретная полиция против боли» и реплики-подбадривания превращают скучную обязанность в вовлечённую роль в команде.

> The first core drive is epic meaning and calling. This is a core drive that says you're motivated because you feel like you're part of something bigger than yourself. So then in a game, it does that often. The world's about to end and for some reason you're the only person qualified to save the world. You feel big. But there's a lot of other examples to make people feel bigger than they are. It could be used to get children with cancer in children's hospital to update their pain journals. And that's what Pain Squad does. So Pain Squad is an app that gets kids to record their pain journal so doctors can diagnose them better. And you can imagine if you're a child and you have cancer, you feel lonely. You feel like no one cares about you. You're in pain. You're not very motivated to keep up keep track of your pain journal. So the Pain Squad is an app that pretends that you're in the secret police force dedicated to hunt down this thing called pain. And the motto is keep pain in its place. Whenever there's pain two times a day, you got to record it down. But to reinforce on that epic meaning and calling, look what they do in the app. Hey rookie, welcome to Pain Squad. It's really great you're here. We need all the help we can get to help put pain in its place. You are now officially a full-fledged detective in Pain Squad. Well done. At this rate, you might even be the next chief. They just don't make them like you anymore. You truly are one of Pain Squad's best and brightest. Keep it up. But this case isn't closed yet. Your squad is still fighting. We couldn't have done it without you. It's a way to go. So now, when the child updates her pain journal, she's not just doing something because she has to. She feels like she's part of something important. A team depends on her. She's fighting with the team against this force called pain. And that motivates her. That's epic meaning and calling.

**Для Напарника:** Кот-напарник как рамка «мы вместе»: его реплики подтверждают, что человек часть общего дела (остров как общий дом).


### Development & Accomplishment: короткие достижения вместо долгосрочной выгоды  ·  [05:56](https://youtu.be/v5Qjuegtiyc?t=356)
**Мысль:** Мозг плохо ценит долгосрочные выгоды, поэтому нужно показывать краткосрочный прогресс и добавлять персонажа, который празднует вехи.

> So we all know that health and exercise is very important. But it's a health is a long-term thing and our brains are terrible at processing long-term benefits. We like short-term gratifications. So what the Nike+ does really well is it shows you short-term accomplishments. It shows you how, "Hey, you have run an extra mile compared to last week. You're improving. Your Nike+ score is getting higher." And so you feel accomplished.

**Для Напарника:** На финале сессии показывать конкретный прирост («ещё один шаг к вчерашнему») и пусть кот празднует с характером.


### Opower: сравнение с соседями сэкономило $250 млн  ·  [10:18](https://youtu.be/v5Qjuegtiyc?t=618)
**Мысль:** Социальное сравнение меняет поведение, потому что все считают себя чуть выше среднего.

> Opower is a utility SaaS company that tries to get people to lower their utility bills. And they saw that the best way to change people's behavior is to show them how their neighbors are doing. You show them their utility, how much you're using, your best neighbor, and your average neighbor. And everyone who always thinks they're somewhat a little above average, which is impossible. You can't have everyone above average. When they see that, they're like, "Oh, I need to change my behavior." So, with Opower, within a year, they saved over $250 million of utility bills. And that's a lot of electricity, over 1 terawatt.

**Для Напарника:** Не применимо: сравнение с другими даёт стыд при СДВГ; можно сравнивать только с собой прошлым («на 5 минут дольше, чем на прошлой неделе»).


### White hat vs black hat: чёрная шляпа мотивирует сильно, но оставляет плохое послевкусие  ·  [13:58](https://youtu.be/v5Qjuegtiyc?t=838)
**Мысль:** Драйвы смысла, роста и творчества дают ощущение контроля; страх потери, неопределённость и дефицит мотивируют, но несостоятельны на длинной дистанции.

> Every single thing you do is based on one or more of these core drives. But it's also put together on octagon shape for a reason. The left side core drives are more extrinsic motivation core drives, which means you're doing these things for a goal, for a purpose, for a reward. The ones on the right are the intrinsic motivation core drives, which means you're doing it because it's just enjoyable. You don't need a reward to use your creativity. You don't need a reward to hang out with friends. And you don't necessarily need to gain anything by being in the suspense of unpredictability. In fact, most people have fun losing money in casinos. And if you notice, the core drives on the top are more positive. I call them white hat gamification techniques. So, if you're always doing something because you feel like you're being something part of being a part of something bigger than yourself, and you're growing, you're improving, and you're using your creativity, it feels very good. It feels like you're in control, you're powerful. The ones on the bottom, I call them black hat game techniques. And if you're always doing something just because you want to avoid a loss, just because you don't know what's going to happen next, or you can't have something, it's going to be very powerful in motivation, but sometimes in the long run leaves a bad taste in your mouth. And it's not a very sustainable long-term design.

**Для Напарника:** Ядро Напарника — белая шляпа (смысл, рост острова, творчество); без FOMO и страха потери стрика (согласуется с CLAUDE.md §3 про стыд).



# 🎬 Gleam: Lessons on App Design When Vibecoding | App Breakdown #35 — App Breakdown


### Ритуал обязательства: приложи палец, дай слово, тактильный отклик и анимация  ·  [04:15](https://youtu.be/XtpCUmsVMcc?t=255)
**Мысль:** Жест (подпись, отпечаток, несколько тапов «я согласен, я сделаю») превращает намерение в обязательство, и хаптика с анимацией делают этот момент запоминающимся.

> Oh, okay, this is an interesting pattern. So, your journey starts now, and basically they want me to give my word to the app. And some apps do this in different ways, for example, with multiple taps on something like, "Yes, I agree, and yes, I'm going to do it." And then click continue. Other apps, for example, like Honk have the little signature. So, for example, you do something on the app, you give your word, and then you sign as a sign of like commitment. In this case, it looks like I'm going to put my fingerprint to give my like commitment to it. So, this is a very cool pattern. The design itself is not out of this world, but I really like this structure here. So, let's actually put my fingerprint. Nice little haptics and animation on the back. Boom, coming in and loading what it unlocked with my fingerprint. I really love this interaction. It's my favorite part of the app this far.

**Для Напарника:** Ритуал старта сессии: удержание пальца на камине/коте «зажечь очаг» с хаптикой (hapticStart) и анимацией; вызывать без давления и вины, как приглашение.



# 🎬 Hacking Your Brain’s “Reward System” to Change Habits — DrJud


### Привычка = триггер, поведение, награда; побеждает поведение с большей наградой  ·  [01:02](https://youtu.be/WQ40hNdZmfQ?t=62)
**Мысль:** Мозг строит иерархию поведений по ценности награды и выполняет то, где награда больше. Привычка формируется через обучение на вознаграждении, а не через повторение само по себе.

> We've learned a habit based on how rewarding the behavior is this is called reward based learning and it has three components a trigger a behavior and a reward let's say you see your shoelace is untied that's the trigger tie your shoes that's the behavior and then don't trip when you walk that's the reward the more rewarding a behavior is the stronger the hell in fact our brain is set up a hierarchy of behaviors based on their reward value the behavior with the bigger reward is the one we act out.

**Для Напарника:** Проектировать петлю Напарника как триггер (приход на Дом) → микро-действие (Фокус) → ощутимая награда (рост острова, реплика кота). Награда должна быть реально приятной, иначе привычка не закрепится.


### Сила воли не меняет привычку: нужно обновить ощущаемую ценность награды  ·  [03:04](https://youtu.be/WQ40hNdZmfQ?t=184)
**Мысль:** Привычка хранит свёрнутую «ценность награды» и срабатывает автоматически; совет «думай о последствиях» редко работает в долгую. Единственный устойчивый путь — обновить ценность награды по живому опыту.

> Think of learning a habit as set-and-forget set the reward value and forget about the details this is also why it's so hard to break bad habits if you're trying to cut down on automatically eating every piece of cake you see what is the standard advice that you get well use your willpower and just don't eat it think of all the calories and what think your way out of eating this might work sometimes but more often than not in the long run it fails why that's not how our brains work to change a behavior you can't just focus on the behavior itself you have to address the felt experience of the rewards of the behavior if it were as easy as thinking our way out of a behavior we just tell ourselves to stop smoking stop eating cake stop yelling and our kids when we're stressed or whatever and it would work but it doesn't the only sustainable way to change a habit is to update its reward value that's why it's called reward based learning after all.

**Для Напарника:** В текстах не давить на силу воли и самообвинения («соберись»). Строить опыт, где приятное ощущение от начала сессии становится новой наградой.
