# 🎬 Hooked by Nir Eyal  | Book Summary Podcast | Habit Forming Products| Hook Model Explained — RevisitBooks - English


### Витамин превращается в болеутоляющее по мере формирования привычки  ·  [04:48](https://youtu.be/lkjkC8osdgU?t=288)
**Мысль:** Новое приложение сначала приятная новинка, потом снимает боль скуки, ФОМО или одиночества и становится необходимым.

> So, in the startup world, investors always ask, are you building a painkiller or a vitamin? And the conventional wisdom is you want to be a painkiller. >> You want to solve an immediate burning problem. >> Exactly. If I have a headache, I need Tylenol. I will pay for it. I will hunt for it. >> Whereas a vitamin is a nice to have. I take one, but I don't really know if it's working. If I skip a day, I don't freak out. >> Right? You don't turn the car around if you forgot your vitamin C. So investors usually say don't build vitamins. But Annal argues that these habit forming technologies are actually both. >> They start as vitamins. >> They start as vitamins. When you first signed up for Instagram, you didn't need it. It was a nice fun thing, a novelty, >> a vitamin. >> But over time, as that habit formed, it became a painkiller. >> Because now if you take it away, I feel pain. >> You feel the pain of boredom, the pain of the fear of missing out, the pain of loneliness. the product becomes the solution to that specific emotional itch. It transforms from a nice to have into a must-have.

**Для Напарника:** Первые дни — приятная новинка (остров, кот), далее — опора в моменты застревания.


### Фасилитатор: психология помогает делать то, что человек и так хочет  ·  [16:02](https://youtu.be/lkjkC8osdgU?t=962)
**Мысль:** Те же триггеры, стрики и социальные награды этичны, если помогают человеку в его собственной цели (пример: Bible app, Duolingo).

> He introduces something called the manipulation matrix to help creators figure this out. >> A matrix. Okay. How does it work? How do we know if we're being evil? It asks two simple questions. One, does this product materially improve the user's life? And two, does the maker use the product themselves? >> Okay, so let's walk through the four quadrants. >> All right. If you don't use it yourself and it doesn't improve the user's life, you are a dealer. >> O, a dealer. That's a loaded term. >> It is. It implies exploitation. Like a drug dealer. You know it's bad. You don't touch it, but you sell it to others to get rich. That's where you find casinos or maybe certain predatory games. >> What if you don't use it, but you think it helps people? >> Then you're a peddler. This is often where you find, say, altruistic advertising or some educational apps made by people who don't really engage with the material, it usually lacks authenticity and it often fails. >> Okay? And if you use it, but it doesn't actually improve lives, >> then you're an entertainer. Like a casual game or a TV show, there's nothing inherently wrong with that, but it's just entertainment. It's fleeting. >> So the golden quadrant, yeah, the goal is to be a facilitator. >> The facilitator, you use the product yourself and you genuinely believe it materially improves the user's life. This is where I wants creators to be. >> So an app like Duolingo or a fitness tracker. >> Exactly. Or the Bible app, which is a case study in the book. They use the exact same psychology, triggers, streaks, social rewards, but they're helping you do something you already want to do. That feels like a really key distinction. Using psychology to help someone do what they want to do versus trapping them into doing something they don't.

**Для Напарника:** Оправдывает механики роста при условии, что они помогают человеку исполнять его собственную цель, без штрафов.



# 🎬 Hooked by Nir Eyal | Full Audiobook 🎧 — WE WE TV


### Витамин или болеутоляющее: продукт, закрывающий психологический зуд  ·  [01:17](https://youtu.be/TW_yZhdWt0g?t=77)
**Мысль:** Продукт становится необходимым, когда снимает неприятное состояние (скука, одиночество, неопределённость).

> Comparison, vitamins versus painkillers. Think about it. When a lot of tech products first launch, they feel a lot like vitamins. They're nice to have, maybe they enrich your life a bit, but you don't truly need them. However, once a product successfully forms a habit, it morphs into a painkiller. And by pain, we're talking about a psychological itch, like the sudden discomfort of boredom, or loneliness, or uncertainty. The product suddenly becomes a must-have remedy that provides instant relief from that itch.

**Для Напарника:** Позиционировать Напарника как болеутоляющее для «не могу начать»: быстрое облегчение в состоянии застревания.



# 🎬 Hooked by Nir Eyal: How Apps Turn Into Habits (The Hook Model) | Animated Book Summary — Spoiler Alert: Everyone Dies


### Три вида награды (племя, охота, себя) и награда должна совпадать с причиной прихода  ·  [05:08](https://youtu.be/az-VpnXi-FM?t=308)
**Мысль:** Переменные награды усиливают тягу; награды делятся на социальные, ресурсные и личного удовлетворения; деньги провалились там, где люди приходили за уважением.

> Step three, the reward. A trigger gets you to try something. You'll only come back if product reliably delivers what it promised. That's the flaw. Eyal says the real pull comes from rewards that vary. He cites studies suggesting that wanting a reward can stir us up more than getting it. Anticipation does a lot of the work. When the reward is predictable, the excitement fades fast. When it's unpredictable, the drive gets stronger. And Eyal says that variability changes our brain chemistry in a way that keeps us chasing, which is why scrolling feed works so well. Every pull on the Twitter feed could bring news, a joke, a reply, or nothing much. You don't know which, so you pull again. Eyal sorts these rewards into three kinds: social ones like likes and friend requests. He calls them rewards of the tribe, resources like a payout from a slot machine, rewards of the hunt, and personal satisfaction like finally beating a hard level in a game, rewards of the self. The products that hook us hardest tend to mix them. The reward also has to match the reason people came. Eyal describes question and answer sites where people answered for the respect of others. Some sites tried paying them cash instead. It flopped because money was never why they'd shown up.

**Для Напарника:** Награда за сессию должна соответствовать мотиву «я сделал(а) это» (награды себя): рост острова, реплика кота, а не абстрактные очки.



# 🎬 Hooked by Nir Eyal: The Full Summary — Yapping Books


### Четыре вопроса: какое чувство, самое простое действие, награда, инвестиция  ·  [12:04](https://youtu.be/0A-Do6ePZyY?t=724)
**Мысль:** Автор признаёт, что удержание — не проблема сообщений, а вопрос, ответом на какое чувство быть.

> The deeper idea is a loop with an order to it, and the order is the argument. If you are building something, the whole book reduces to four questions. What feeling brings the user here? What is the easiest useful thing they can do? What reward keeps it interesting enough to return for? And what can they invest that makes the next visit better than this one? The thing I would tell myself before reading this. Every time I treated retention as a messaging problem, I was working on the scaffolding. The question underneath was always which feeling we wanted to be the answer to.

**Для Напарника:** Записать для Напарника: чувство — «не могу начать», действие — один тап, награда — рост острова, инвестиция — история.



# 🎬 How a handful of tech companies control billions of minds every day | Tristan Harris — TED


### Рефлексивный мозг против «мозга ящерицы»  ·  [14:39](https://youtu.be/C74amJRp730?t=879)
**Мысль:** Алгоритмы оптимизируют импульсивные клики, а не то, чего человек хочет, подумав; нужна более тонкая модель человека.

> So much of this is justified in terms of human preference, where we've got these algorithms that do an amazing job of optimizing for human preference, but which preference? There's the preferences of things that we really care about when we think about them versus the preferences of what we just instinctively click on. If we could implant that more nuanced view of human nature in every design, would that be a step forward? TH: Absolutely. I mean, I think right now it's as if all of our technology is basically only asking our lizard brain what's the best way to just impulsively get you to do the next tiniest thing with your time, instead of asking you in your life what we would be most time well spent for you?

**Для Напарника:** Дофаминовые награды Напарника привязывать к осознанным целям (сессия завершена), а не к импульсивному вознаграждению.



# 🎬 How Duolingo Built a $1B App Empire (And You Can Too) — Anything 


### Стрики и неприятие потери: данные Duolingo  ·  [05:22](https://youtu.be/i9S0pp97fEw?t=322)
**Мысль:** 7-дневная серия даёт в 3,6 раза больше вероятности остаться; заморозки, амулеты защищают серию; рычаг это неприятие потери, оно мотивирует сильнее награды.

> Streaks are the foundation, consecutive days of completing at least one lesson. Their data shows users who maintain a 7-day streak are 3.6 times more likely to stay engaged over long term. Over 6 million users have active streaks of 7 days or more. The longest active streak is over 4,100 days. Someone has completed a lesson every single day for more than 11 years. They've built an economy around protecting your streak. Streak freezes cover a missed day. Weekend amulets protect weekends. Streak wagers let you bet gems on maintaining your streak. The lever is loss aversion. Losing a 200-day streak feels devastating. That's way more motivating than getting a reward.

**Для Напарника:** Не применимо в лоб: страх потери серии противоречит тону без стыда. Берём идею защиты (прощение пропуска), но не угрозу.



# 🎬 How Duolingo Engineered a Billion-Dollar Habit — Company Mechanics


### Открыл не из желания, а потому что потеря хуже продолжения  ·  [00:22](https://youtu.be/ayDAXF7dKgY?t=22)
**Мысль:** Усталый человек в 23:59 заходит ради сохранения числа: инженерия возвращения, а не желания.

> You did not open the app because your desire to learn suddenly returned. You opened it because losing felt worse than continuing. That tiny decision, repeated across an audience of millions, is part of the mechanism behind one of the world's largest education businesses. Duolingo did not invent the desire to learn a language. It engineered a system that makes people come back tomorrow.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник строит возвращение через тепло и присутствие, а не через страх потери.


### Серия превращается из счётчика во владение  ·  [01:45](https://youtu.be/ayDAXF7dKgY?t=105)
**Мысль:** Чем длиннее серия, тем меньше она счётчик и тем больше «построенное»; мотив сдвигается от получения дня к избеганию потери; пламя, вехи и анимации усиливают.

> A one-day streak means almost nothing. Seven days feels like momentum. Thirty days feels like proof. 365 days can feel like part of your identity. The longer the streak grows, the less it resembles a counter. It becomes something you have built. And once people feel ownership over progress, the motivation changes. At first, you complete a lesson to gain another day. Later, you complete it to avoid losing hundreds of days behind you. This is loss aversion: the tendency to experience a loss more strongly than an equivalent gain. Duolingo has explicitly described designing parts of the streak experience around this behavior. The flame becomes more prominent. Milestones are celebrated. Animations make the streak feel alive. A missed day threatens the visible history of your consistency.

**Для Напарника:** Остров как «построенное» без угрозы потери: накопление остаётся, пропуск не разрушает.


### Геймификация смещает цель: очки и серия вместо обучения  ·  [08:12](https://youtu.be/ayDAXF7dKgY?t=492)
**Мысль:** Активность не равна результату; люди повторяют лёгкие уроки ради очков и защищают серию минимальным упражнением. Система производит две цели: учиться и сохранять число.

> There is another tension. A system can increase activity without guaranteeing that every activity produces meaningful learning. A peer-reviewed study assessed 225 adults who had completed the beginning content of Duolingo's Spanish or French courses and reported using Duolingo as their only learning tool. Their reading and listening results were comparable to those of university students after four semesters of language study. But the research did not assess speaking or writing, did not cover every course, and did not demonstrate complete fluency. Gamification can also change what users optimize for. Some may repeat easy lessons for points. Some may care more about winning a league than understanding a sentence. Some may protect a streak with the shortest possible exercise and then close the app. A qualitative study analyzed 357 forum posts, more than 30,000 comments, and interviews with 15 users. It documented cases in which people became overly focused on points, streaks, badges, and competition. The study could describe that behavior, but not establish how common it is across Duolingo's entire audience. That does not mean gamification is a trick. And it does not mean Duolingo does not teach. It means the system can produce two goals: learning consistently, and keeping the number alive. Is the streak still serving your learning... or has your learning started serving the streak?

**Для Напарника:** Награды Напарника привязывать к реальному действию по делу, а не к пустым заходам; метрика результата, а не активность.



# 🎬 How Duolingo Turns Your Streak Into Money — Brand Casefile


### Промежуточные награды закрывают разрыв между усилием сейчас и результатом потом  ·  [00:57](https://youtu.be/415kRd__tQM?t=57)
**Мысль:** Награда за языковой навык далеко, а усилие сегодня; Duolingo вставляет между ними мелкие завершаемые вещи (очки, квесты), а стрик даёт повод вернуться завтра и со временем становится идентичностью: «я тот, кто приходит».

> On your first day, you can imagine your future self ordering dinner in Italian. 3 weeks later, your present self has dishes to wash. The reward is far away while the effort is due now. Dualingo puts smaller rewards in between. Finish a lesson, earn points, complete a quest, or move up a leaderboard. You might not be ready to discuss politics in French, but you can finish something before the kettle boils. That gives you a reason to complete today's lesson. The streak gives you a reason to come back tomorrow. At first, it's just a counter. Then you have a week, then a month. Eventually, it becomes evidence of something you like about yourself. You are someone who keeps showing up.

**Для Напарника:** Остров растёт от каждой завершённой сессии (малая награда рядом), а долгий горизонт выражается как «ты тот, кто приходит». Формулировать идентичность тепло, без счётчиков-обязательств.


### Стрик сам по себе подталкивает к лёгким активностям, а не к полезным  ·  [05:53](https://youtu.be/415kRd__tQM?t=353)
**Мысль:** Когда главная цель — сохранить число, люди выбирают короткое и лёгкое. Duolingo признал, что метрика по числу сессий поощряет такое поведение, и ввёл показатель, взвешивающий время продвижения. Счётчик не равен ценности.

> Even getting people to return isn't the whole answer. Imagine you have 10 minutes. Do you attempt a difficult lesson or repeat something comfortable to collect a quick reward? Review can be useful and so can a short session on a busy day. If protecting the streak becomes your main goal, you may start choosing whatever keeps the number alive. Dualingo has acknowledged a related problem. In a 2024 explanation, it said that counting completed sessions could favor short, easy activities over longer, harder lessons. It developed a measure that gave more weight to time spent advancing along the course. Five completed sessions can look better than three on a dashboard, but that doesn't automatically mean they taught you more. This brings us back to the person getting ready for bed. The streak can get you to open the app tonight. Whether those visits remain worthwhile depends on what happens after you arrive. Duolingo turns that habit into opportunities, including advertising, subscription offers, and an ongoing relationship with someone who might eventually pay. Asking too much can break the routine. Selling too aggressively can push people away. Keeping a number alive isn't the same as giving someone a reason to value the product.

**Для Напарника:** Не оптимизировать Напарника по числу сессий или длине серии: измерять ощущение пользы (завершённые шаги, возврат без давления). Награды привязывать к реальному действию.



# 🎬 How Gameplay Loops Keep You Playing — Adam Millard - The Architect of Games


### Длинные циклы дают причину вернуться  ·  [03:54](https://youtu.be/Sk-nbAtIUko?t=234)
**Мысль:** Длинные циклы (кампания, рост персонажа) мотивируют абстрактно, тайнами и ростом, и возвращают, когда сиюминутный азарт угас.

> However, for much larger, longer gameplay loops, the opposite is true. Because a single cycle might take hours to resolve, it’s much harder to directly tie it to the mechanics. Take the cycle of conquest in a strategy game like total war. First, you pick where you want to invade, muster your armies, duke it out with the enemy, either win or lose the war and then either cement your hold on your new territory or slink back home to regroup. You might only get through one or two of these loops in a given play session, and it’s completely disconnected from the moment-to-moment gameplay, which is mostly clicking through menus or telling your dudes which orcs to shoot. Longer gameplay loops are much more concerned with offering an abstract source of motivation and something to come back to, rather than immediate fun. Old-school JRPGs turn a lot of people off because their core loop, that being individual turns in a combat encounter are usually pretty boring. However, other people love RPGs because they place a massive emphasis on high-level meta gameplay loops, where you get to see your characters and the story evolve over tens of hours, and the smaller gameplay loops are just steps towards that eventual goal. By offering up plot mysteries and the opportunity to see character growth, games can not only keep you entertained, but also give you a goal to shoot for, and the need to experience the satisfying resolution to this loop is something that will bring you back to the game after the more immediate thrill of the gameplay has faded.

**Для Напарника:** Остров и ориентиры на горизонте — длинный цикл; усиливать ощущение «что-то растёт и скоро будет», не давя стрик-страхом.


### Два связанных цикла и постоянный недобор до покупки  ·  [07:12](https://youtu.be/Sk-nbAtIUko?t=432)
**Мысль:** В Steamworld Dig 2 цикл «шахта и улучшения» построен так, что вам всегда чуть-чуть не хватает на всё желаемое, что рождает новую цель.

> A lot of games, rather than having a single main loop, have two main loops instead that each feed into each other. Steamworld dig 2 is a great example of this particular style, with the entire structure of the game being built around the dual loops of mining in the caves, and then upgrading back in town. Here’s how it works. As you adventure deeper and deeper into the mines of El Machino, mining minerals and killing baddies, eventually, your pack will fill up or your torch will run out, and you’ll be forced to return to the surface. This means you’re invariably going to have to say goodbye to some precious goodies or an unexplored part of the dungeon. Once you get to the surface, you can sell your haul to buy fancy new upgrades but things are balanced in such a way that you’ll never quiiite have enough to buy everything you want, giving you a new goal to shoot for as you drop back into the mines in search of that loot you abandoned before in a constant cycle of effort and rewards, and new opportunities.

**Для Напарника:** Награды острова могут давать лёгкое «ещё чуть-чуть до следующей постройки» без дефицита и без вины за недобор.



# 🎬 How I Fixed a Client's Onboarding and Won the Contract — Bear Liu


### Продавай огненные шары, а не цветок: онбординг — помощь стать лучшей версией себя  ·  [04:55](https://youtu.be/S7eGDOkd_Pg?t=295)
**Мысль:** Покупатель цветка Марио хочет стрелять огнём, а не слушать про два листика. Онбординг должен показывать, какой потенциал открывается, а не перечислять функции.

> People understand what they can do with this app or with this product to be a better version of themselves. So yeah, it seems quite not a very academic type of definition, but it's all about them, it's all about the people who are using your product and how you can help them to become a better version of themselves. And let's see this example, I think this is a great one if you are a manufacturer, if you are a vendor in the Super Mario world and you are selling a fire flower, and Mario or other plumbers they are your customers, they want to be strong, they want to be able to fire some fireballs, and if you want to sell this product, the product feature is not important to them, it's not important for Mario — what Mario wants to do is to be able to become a stronger version and able to fire some fireballs to hit those monsters, to kick out those mushrooms, and that's what they want — that is the version in their mind, and if you help them to become a better version that's the success of your product. So many of us want to sell something by promoting "we have a really great product, this product — wow, look at this fire flower, so beautiful, wow it's got two leaves and so many different features, you can buy one and get one free" for example, but it's all about us, it's all about the features, and what people care about is what they can do with this product — what's the potential that will be released, will be unleashed by this product, and this is all about user onboarding, this is all about the value of onboarding,.

**Для Напарника:** Главная формула текстов Напарника: не «таймер, ИИ-дробление, остров», а «ты начнёшь то, что откладывал, и сможешь гордиться сделанным». Для лендинга и первого экрана Дома.


### Принципы: «продавай огненные шары» и ценность пользователя прежде функции  ·  [11:03](https://youtu.be/S7eGDOkd_Pg?t=663)
**Мысль:** Если брать из книги одно: сместить тон, внимание и фокус с «нас» на людей, которым служите; сначала достижение пользователя, затем фича.

> And the first principle I think is about this: it's about selling fireballs, not the fire flower. I said it. This is one thing, one major principle — if you want to take away one thing from this book, just take this one. And the second one is about putting the value and achievement for users first and your feature second. So it also means that if you want to sell this, it's not about "oh, this is the best, now we have this — we have this green, such a beautiful flower for you" — it doesn't matter to them. What they want is this. So this is something we want to focus on — we want to shift the tone, the attention, the focus from us to the people you're serving.

**Для Напарника:** Проверка любого текста в Напарнике: о ком это предложение, о нас или о человеке? Особенно реплики кота и заголовки.



# 🎬 How Idle Games Infected The Entire Games Industry — Shoobaloo


### В реальной жизни прогресс невидим, поэтому тянет к играм с видимым ростом  ·  [01:06](https://youtu.be/ZjrVc_5QNKY?t=66)
**Мысль:** Мы часто не получаем награды за домашние дела и работу в короткой перспективе, и прогресс кажется отсутствующим. Игры, где число растёт, дают то, чего не хватает в жизни.

> As you go through life, do you ever question whether you're making progress? Sometimes it's difficult to tell because you often aren't rewarded for things in the short term or even at all. When was the last time you received a tangible reward for doing the dishes or cleaning your room? Does your mommy still fork up for your allowance? My wife gives me mine on Tuesdays, I was just curious. Progress can sometimes feel invisible or even non-existent in real life.

**Для Напарника:** Ключевая ценность Напарника: сделать невидимый прогресс (маленькие шаги по делам) видимым через остров и кота.


### Игра ради устранения необходимости играть: начальный труд как фундамент  ·  [02:24](https://youtu.be/ZjrVc_5QNKY?t=144)
**Мысль:** Ты думаешь, что играешь за деньги, но реально играешь, чтобы убрать необходимость играть. Начальное рутинное кликанье - основа дизайна.

> Here is the key. You thought you were playing to get more money. Sure, it started out that way, but now you're actually playing to remove the need to play at all. That's the real loop. That's the addiction. That initial tedious, repetitive clicking, it's actually fundamental to an idle game's design.

**Для Напарника:** Идея автоматизации усилия: со временем привычка и ритуал облегчаются, и остров помогает выстраивать их.


### Единственное правило: число растёт, прогресс certain и виден  ·  [15:10](https://youtu.be/ZjrVc_5QNKY?t=910)
**Мысль:** Число символизирует прогресс; игры делают прогресс гарантированным и видимым, чего не делает реальная жизнь.

> You see, all these incremental games essentially function the same. The one and only rule of an incremental game is the number must go up. That number, regardless of how it's presented on screen, is a representation of progress. These games make progress certain and visible. When's the last time real life did that for you?

**Для Напарника:** Гарантия видимого прироста за любую завершённую минуту фокуса (ноль не минус).


### Игры дают структуру: понятные правила, усилие равно награде, прогресс неизбежен  ·  [17:20](https://youtu.be/ZjrVc_5QNKY?t=1040)
**Мысль:** Реальная жизнь не гарантирует прогресса, а идле-игры гарантируют. Структура, где правила осмысленны, а усилие вознаграждается, - то, чего людям не хватает.

> Real life will always be messy, unpredictable, and full of friction that we can't just automate away with a hive mind of cookie-baking grandmas that bring about the grandmacalypse. Real life doesn't guarantee progress, but these boring games do. And that is exactly why they are so magnetic, so addictive. They provide a kind of structure where the rules make sense, where effort equals bountiful reward, and progress is imminent. I think that is truly the problem that these games solve.

**Для Напарника:** Внешняя структура для людей с СДВГ: ясные правила, что считается прогрессом, и надёжное вознаграждение за усилие.



# 🎬 How Ramp and Plaid create a culture of craft — Dive Club 🤿


### Работа с открытой дверью: поздняя демонстрация превращает отзыв в нападение  ·  [02:20](https://youtu.be/36iCkzRbC0Y?t=140)
**Мысль:** Чем глубже в работе, тем сильнее эмоциональная привязанность и тем больнее обратная связь, плюс времени менять уже нет; делиться нужно на этапе наброска на салфетке.

> That's a huge part of my philosophy as a design leader is the designers need to always have again the grass door up like you need to let people see what you're working on comment on it and really give you that sort of ongoing feedback that was so crucial problema in very many different design teams struggle with where designers wait a little too long before they share their work and so one when you too deep into the work you sort of have an emotional attachment to the solutions and so the feedback you get feels like an attack on you not on the work and people get like start of defend their solution their ideas rather than listening to feedback and it's also often a little too late really to kind of shift like you wen't the designer is likely running out of time and so they're showing the final thing like well you know there's only so much you're going to be able to change before you need to go and build this and so making sure designers sh share the work at the beginning of the idea like hey like here's a real concept I have I made a sketch on a napkin like let's talk about my sketch on a napkin.

**Для Напарника:** Созвучно тону продукта без стыда: показывать и просить помощи рано (первый шаг, черновик), пока нет привязанности и страха оценки.



# 🎬 How to ALWAYS Stay Motivated (if you have ADHD) — ADHDVision


### Культура хаслa - как джанк-фуд для СДВГ-мозга  ·  [01:17](https://youtu.be/LqouZ6in6yY?t=77)
**Мысль:** Быстрый дофаминовый всплеск от воображаемой работы и последующий провал; мозгу нужен всё более сильный стимул.

> The self-help and motivational industry have been incentivized to lie to you. They sold you the idea that the secret to success is to hustle harder, but hustle culture is like junk food for ADHD brains. Fast dopamine spike, faster crash, and over time your brain needs more extreme stimulus just to feel normal. You get the dopamine hit from imagining yourself doing the work, but when you sit at your desk, nothing happens.

**Для Напарника:** Не строить на мотивирующих цитатах и «хайпе»; награды за реальное действие, а не за намерение.


### Интерес-ориентированная нервная система: активация приходит снаружи  ·  [01:50](https://youtu.be/LqouZ6in6yY?t=110)
**Мысль:** Мозг СДВГ включается интересом, вызовом, новизной и срочностью, а не важностью; нехватка внутреннего топлива делает силу воли бесполезной.

> Dr. William Dodson, one of the world's leading ADHD researchers, spent years studying why standard productivity and self-help advice consistently failed people with ADHD. What he found changed how we understand the condition entirely. He calls it the interest-based nervous system. Neurotypical brains activate for importance, for deadlines, for long-term reward. Our brains activate for one thing, interest. And here's why that matters, because a peer-reviewed study published in Molecular Psychiatry confirmed that ADHD brains have a structural deficit in the dopamine reward pathway, the circuit responsible for generating internal motivation. Which means willpower was never going to work, not because you're weak, but because you are missing the fuel at a neurological level for it to work. What Dr. Dodson found is that every single thing that activates our nervous system comes from outside of us. Challenge, novelty, urgency, they're all different triggers in the same category known as external.

**Для Напарника:** Дизайн-принцип: создавать внешние условия активации (вызов, новизна, срочность) прямо в Фокусе, а не требовать воли.


### Конкретная подотчётность: кто, когда, что, на сколько  ·  [11:29](https://youtu.be/LqouZ6in6yY?t=689)
**Мысль:** Расплывчатое «поработаю сегодня» слабо; чёткие время, активность и длительность с другими создают социальное обязательство и идентичность «я из тех, кто приходит».

> Make accountability specific. This is the one most people miss. Let's say you're looking for a job in the age of AI. Telling someone I'm going to work on this today doesn't do much even if you're sitting in that cafe. Motivation may fade because your ADHD brain doesn't just run on intention, it runs on identity. We follow through on what people like us are doing. When you're working with random people at random times, there is no signal for who you are in that moment. But the moment you say I'll be on a call at 2:00 p.m. with other people who are also submitting job applications and I'll be on that call until 2:50 p.m., something fundamental happens. You've just made the time and activity you're working on specific. Specific accountability has four elements your brain can actually process. Who, when, what, and for how long.

**Для Напарника:** Старт сессии как маленькое обещание напарнику: что и на сколько минут; кот «ждёт к 15:00».



# 🎬 How to Choose the Best Colors for UI/UX Design | Color Theory, Contrast & Accessibility Tips — Chandini Creates


### Выбор цвета начинается с тона продукта и желаемого чувства  ·  [00:24](https://youtu.be/SdWDrFqkax0?t=24)
**Мысль:** Сначала решаешь, как пользователь должен себя чувствовать (игриво или спокойно), и от этого выбираешь оттенки: тёплые - энергия, синий и серый - надёжность.

> First things first when you start picking colors start from the basics what is the tone of your product think about how you want your users to feel when they use it is it more fun and playful or is it more calm and professional for example colors like yellow and orange feel energetic and friendly while colors like blue and gray gives a very calm and reliable feeling so colors can instantly set the vibe or the mood of your product.

**Для Напарника:** Фиксирует подход Напарника: тёплая, спокойная палитра как следствие заданного чувства присутствия.



# 🎬 How to Get Stuff Done with ADHD Part 1: Task Initiation | Neurodivergent Magic — The Neurocuriosity Club


### Ты не ленивый: ты работаешь вдвое больше ради половины результата  ·  [01:09](https://youtu.be/WQuYHPg3Tjo?t=69)
**Мысль:** Если лень измеряется усилием, человек с СДВГ её противоположность; самоуничижение не работает, нужна самосострадание.

> Before we get started, I just want to address a really important point of fact, and that is you are not lazy. I'm sure that you've been called lazy, and I'm sure you've started to call yourself lazy, but the truth is this, you're not. In fact, if laziness is a measurement of how hard you're working, you are the opposite of lazy. You are working double time to get half the results. That's not laziness, that's disability. I officially give you permission to let go of the lazy label because it's holding you back even more. If beating yourself up worked, it would've worked by now. So let it go and start practicing some self-compassion and understanding that yes, sometimes things are difficult for you, but that doesn't mean that you are bad. It just means that your brain works differently.

**Для Напарника:** Ключевая фраза для тона Напарника и онбординга: сочувствие без снисхождения, без стыда.


### Начинать больно по-настоящему; выбери меньшую боль сейчас вместо большей в дедлайн  ·  [05:20](https://youtu.be/WQuYHPg3Tjo?t=320)
**Мысль:** Боль начала реальна, желание избежать её естественно; признание делает возможным выбор: поболеть чуть сейчас, чтобы не мучиться втрое позже.

> Step number five is to acknowledge the mental pain you're about to experience. And yeah, maybe that sounds dramatic to someone without a DHD, but if you have a DHD, you know what I'm talking about. Starting a task is literally painful, and this pain is real. So often we are told to ignore it. We are told that it doesn't count or that it's in our heads, but it's not. The A DHD brain works differently, and part of that includes dealing with more mental pain when it comes to starting boring or difficult tasks, and because this pain is real, your desire to avoid it makes sense that you don't want to do the thing. Of course, you don't want to do the thing, it's going to hurt, but we know the thing needs done anyway. So what we have the power to do is to choose to face one mental pain in order to avoid another. So yeah, you could avoid the mental pain of starting this specific task right now, and then you wouldn't be in mental pain right now, but you will be in mental pain in three days when you're facing a deadline that you can no longer meet because you put it off too long. And I know this is hard for a lot of us to grasp when we have a DHD because our sense of time isn't very linear. It's much more of like now or not now, but because you took the time to be emotionally aware and to do your stimming, I think this step, you're going to be able to look inward and say, no, I don't want to suffer in three days, 10 times harder because I didn't want to suffer. Now I am going to choose to suffer because I know it will spare me suffering in the long run.

**Для Напарника:** Валидация в чате: «начать правда тяжело»; не отрицать трудность. Осторожно с формулировкой «страдать» - использовать мягче.



# 🎬 How to Keep Players Engaged (Without Being Evil) — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Долгая цель плюс короткие вехи (пример Stardew Valley)  ·  [05:27](https://youtu.be/hbzGO_Qonu0?t=327)
**Мысль:** Мечта о преобразованной ферме держит часы рутины, потому что есть самовыражение, множество коротких целей и стратегические решения.

> Now, one of the most compelling things a game can do is let you make progress towards a long-term goal. Give someone a level cap they can work towards, or a map full of collectibles they can find, and they can be stuck in for weeks. But let’s dig a bit deeper into that. So a game that does this really well is the charming farming sim Stardew Valley which comes with an implicit long-term goal - you start with a messy patch of land and no money, but in time you’ll have transformed the land into an incredible farm that makes you loads of cash. And it’s that dream that can sustain many hours of toil, hard work, and, to be honest, kinda repetitive gameplay. So why does it keep us going? Well, I think it’s cool that you can make your farm however you like. You get to express yourself, set your own goals, and build something you are really proud of. The game has lots of short term goals to keep you going. Smaller rewards like the community centre and important milestones like adding buildings or improving your house give you something immediate to shoot for on the way towards the longer term ambition. There’s also a huge element of planning, as you must think long term about decisions when it comes to different crops and animals, seasons, romantic partners, and so on. You’re not just trudging mindlessly towards the goal, but making strategic choices to help you get there faster.

**Для Напарника:** Рост острова как долгая цель, с маленькими вехами после каждой сессии; игрок сам выбирает, как строится остров.


### Состояние потока и провал как норма: короткие попытки, ощущение роста  ·  [08:16](https://youtu.be/hbzGO_Qonu0?t=496)
**Мысль:** Поток возникает, когда вызов не слишком лёгкий и не слишком трудный; к провалам возвращаются, если попытки короткие, есть ощущение улучшения и следующая будет другой.

> Another huge motivator is a compelling challenge. A game that is constantly pushing you to show your mastery of the game, in varied and interesting situations, can really keep you going. This requires pitch-perfect difficulty, though. As we talked about in the Resident Evil 4 episode, players only get into an engaging flow state when they meet a challenge that isn’t so easy as to be boring, but isn’t so difficult as to be stressful. Resident Evil uses dynamic difficulty to keep players in that sweet spot, but for most games, a finely tuned and fiercely play-tested difficulty curve is the way to go. Remember, though, that failure isn’t a bad thing. Some of the most engaging games like Tetris and Spelunky are filled with failure, but people will keep coming back if the runs are relatively short, if they feel a sense of getting better each time, and if they know the next session will be markedly different from the one before - often due to random generation of some kind.

**Для Напарника:** Дробление задачи на микрошаги и короткие сессии: неудача сессии не штраф, а следующий шаг выглядит иначе; тон без вины.



# 🎬 How To Make Change Work | BJ Fogg | Tiny Habits — Dr. Maika Steinborn


### Рой пчёл: собрать все возможные поведения, представив волшебную палочку  ·  [01:57](https://youtu.be/54Nxzhwja7E?t=117)
**Мысль:** Сначала нужно собрать много вариантов действий без оценки реализуемости, а потом выбирать.

> Step 2 explore options next collect any behavior that could help you achieve your aspiration and outcome anything that you could start stop or do once BJ Fogg calls this collecting a swarm of bees around your aspiration or outcome and he recommends to do it from a headspace of holding a magic wand assume that you can get yourself to do anything and that nothing will stand in your way.

**Для Напарника:** В режиме дробления задачи можно сначала предложить 3–5 вариантов микрошагов, потом человек выбирает один.


### Focus Map: выбирай поведения с высоким влиянием и высокой выполнимостью («золотые»)  ·  [03:55](https://youtu.be/54Nxzhwja7E?t=235)
**Мысль:** Оценка вклада и реализуемости (смогу ли в самый трудный день, хочу ли, а не должен) отсеивает идеальные, но нереальные привычки.

> Now you want to take a closer look at the swarm of bees that you've collected and examine each one for its impact and feasibility and then you'll Place each behavior on this map this Focus map as BJ fog calls it pack means to ask how much will this Behavior actually contribute to achieving my outcome and feasibility is the question of can I get myself to do it given my resources my ability can I do it and do I want to do it is there more of a motivation and a want-to behind it than a should can you see yourself doing this even on the hardest day of the week and the habits that are most promising the ones you want to select are those in the top right corner with the highest impact and feasibility and BJ fog calls them the golden Behavior.

**Для Напарника:** При выборе первого шага предлагать «самый выполнимый и самый полезный»: вопрос «смог(ла) бы ты это сделать в самый тяжёлый день?»



# 🎬 How to Make Learning as Addictive as Social Media | Duolingo's Luis Von Ahn | TED — TED


### Сделать «брокколи» на вкус как десерт: те же приёмы, что у соцсетей  ·  [05:12](https://youtu.be/P6FORpg0KVo?t=312)
**Мысль:** Смартфон конкурирует с TikTok и играми; чтобы люди хотели учиться, нужны те же психологические приёмы вовлечения, но направленные на полезное.

> However, if you're trying to deliver education with a smartphone, you run into a humongous problem. And it is that smartphones come equipped with some of the most addictive drugs that humanity has ever engineered. TikTok, Instagram, mobile games. See, delivering education over a smartphone is like hoping that people will eat their broccoli, but right next to it, you put the most delicious dessert ever made. (Laughter) If you really want to deliver education to everyone, not only do you have to make it accessible, but also you have to make it so that people want to actually learn. And with Duolingo, we've been able to do this. And at the highest level, the way we've done this is by making the broccoli taste like dessert. I'll say it another way. What we've done is that we've used the same psychological techniques that apps like Instagram, TikTok or mobile games use to keep people engaged, but in this case, we use them to keep people engaged but with education.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник применяет дофаминовые приёмы (награды, неожиданные находки, рост острова) ради фокуса, но без тёмных паттернов.


### Полезный продукт не обязан быть столь же затягивающим, как TikTok  ·  [09:57](https://youtu.be/P6FORpg0KVo?t=597)
**Мысль:** Осмысленность даёт 10-20% внутренней мотивации; пользователь после учёбы не чувствует потраченного впустую времени, в отличие от скролла.

> And this is a really important point, let me say this. I don't actually believe that there's a way to make an educational app be as engaging as something like TikTok or Instagram or mobile games. But the good news is that -- And by the way, the reason I don't believe that is because ultimately you have to teach people something. And it's hard to compete with, like, cats and celebrities. But the good news is that I don't think you have to. See, here's the thing. When you're learning something, you get meaning out of it. Whereas when you're scrolling for two hours on Instagram, a lot of times afterwards, you feel like you just wasted your time. So I think it's actually OK if your educational product is only 80 or 90 percent as engaging as something like TikTok, because the other 10 or 20 percent will be provided by people's internal motivation, though of course, not much more than that.

**Для Напарника:** Не гнаться за «залипанием»; ценность в ощущении смысла после сессии: итоговый экран подчёркивает сделанное, а не время в приложении.



# 🎬 How to Make Your Vibecoded Mobile App Feel Human | Product Delight — Build Native Mobile apps with AI


### Продукт решает и функциональную, и эмоциональную потребность в одном опыте  ·  [03:39](https://youtu.be/Hvnk76SKmtc?t=219)
**Мысль:** Лучшие продукты не делятся на функциональный и эмоциональный: они объединяют «сделать» и «почувствовать себя защищённым, гордым, под контролем» в одном опыте.

> Actually, the definition of pleasurability is that the product has to solve for two problem, meaning that it has to solve for the functional need, like I want to send an SMS, I want to book a flight, I want to send an email, okay? These are functional need. But also, what that's the most important part, it has to solve for emotional need, meaning that it need to make you feel secure, it has to make you feel proud, it has to make you feel maybe control in control and reassured. And so, the very best product in the market today are those who try to make and combine those two needs within the same product experience. So, it's not a product that is functional and a product that is emotional. No, it has to be the same product that combine the two together within the same experience.

**Для Напарника:** Каждый экран: решает задачу (начать шаг) и даёт эмоцию (присутствие кота, безопасность без стыда).


### Восторг = радость + удивление, но у каждого продукта своя эмоция  ·  [06:35](https://youtu.be/Hvnk76SKmtc?t=395)
**Мысль:** По Плутчику восторг — вторичная эмоция из радости и удивления, но продукт может решать задачу чувства гордости, безопасности, контроля. Инженеры привыкли спрашивать только про скорость.

> So, delight is an emotion. And there've been a lot of studies and a lot of researchers trying to study emotions, which is one of the most complex topic on the world. But, there's one professor who's a Professor Plutchik who actually said that delight is a secondary emotion. He actually conceptualized emotions into primary emotions and secondary emotion. And he said that delight is a secondary emotion, which means that it is a combination of two primary emotions. And he came to the conclusion that delight is actually the combination of joy and surprise. Which means that if you're in the moment, try to feel it. I know it's it's a it's nice to feel it. if you're in a moment experiencing at the same time joy and surprise, you are delighted. So, that's the theoretical definition. Now, when it comes to the practical definition, having been experiencing this myself and building that myself. I believe that it is more than joy and surprise, because not every product has to be built to provide you joy. Some product have to exist in order to make you feel proud, or to make you feel reassured, or you want to feel empathy out of using the product. You want to feel safe, in control. You want to feel confident. I mean, you see, I'm I'm I'm exaggerating, I'm giving more emotions here, because I want you to realize that every product exists for a different emotional need. And so, indepen- or dependent on the product that you're building, you need to understand what are you solving for from an emotional perspective. I know this concept is new for you, because we're so much trained, especially engineers here in the room. Like, we're so much trained in trying to understand what should we solve for from a functional perspective. How can I make it faster? How can I make it more efficient? now, I want you to step back and also ask, "How do I want my customers and my users to feel while using the product?".

**Для Напарника:** Явно сформулировать эмоцию Напарника (рядом, не одиноко, прощено) и сверять каждую правку с ней.


### Мотивационная сегментация: кто, что и зачем  ·  [11:29](https://youtu.be/Hvnk76SKmtc?t=689)
**Мысль:** Демография и поведение уступают сегментации по мотивации: зачем человек пользуется продуктом. Интервью обычно дают сюрпризы.

> So, the very first step, and I really insist because it's the most important and it's the first step that you need to take, which is identifying motivators. What motivate your users to use a certain product? And it's really important to also recognize that there are different needs and there are different personas as well among your customers. I mean, if you're a product builder, you know that the very most important step to start with is to do segmentation and segment your customers. And having been building product for many years, I know that some people would do segmentation based on demographics, like a gender, age, location, language. So, this is the what we call demographic segmentation. There is a second segmentation which is a little bit better, which is behavioral segmentation. It's actually the what are they doing? What's their behavior toward the app or toward the journey? And the third segmentation, which is by far the most important one, which is the motivational segmentation. It's actually the why. Like, why your customers are using your product? So, you see, you have the who, you have the what, and you have the why. And you I highly insist that it's important to spend some time trying to understand what motivate your customers to use your product. You think you know, but believe me, you will get a lot of surprises. I love these users interview where you start to realize that your users are using your product for different reasons.

**Для Напарника:** Опросы и интервью с пользователями Напарника: зачем приходят (тревога, прокрастинация, одиночество), а не кто они.


### Функциональные и эмоциональные мотиваторы на примере Spotify  ·  [13:08](https://youtu.be/Hvnk76SKmtc?t=788)
**Мысль:** Для каждого продукта выписывай оба слоя: функциональный (плейлисты, поиск) и эмоциональный (погружение, меньше одиночества). Строй по ним, а не по идеям основателя.

> Now, it's important also to know that there are functional motivators and there are emotional motivators. Remember I said earlier that there are functional needs and emotional needs. So, it's important when you're doing this segmentation to say like what motivate my users from a functional perspective and what motivate my users from an emotional perspective too. So for example, let's take Spotify as an example. I've been building product at Spotify for more than 4 years and so from a functional perspective, we can say that user want to enjoy high quality music. They want to create playlist. They want to search for audio content. They want to shuffle. They want to play on another device. I mean it does so many functional motivators. On the other side, it's really important to recognize the emotional motivators as well. People want to feel immersed or self-expressed or they want to feel connected to the music world. They want to feel less lonely. They want to feel nostalgia or entertainment as well. So you see it's important to recognize those two because if you start with this step, then you will build for those motivators. You're not build for the let's say the founders need or brilliant idea. You will build for what exactly motivate your users.

**Для Напарника:** Выписать для Напарника функциональные (запустить шаг, таймер) и эмоциональные (не быть одному, прощение) мотиваторы и сверять фичи.



# 🎬 How To Power Through ADHD: Proven Strategies to Crush Tough Tasks — Dr. Tracey Marks


### Аверсивные задачи: почему мы избегаем (большая, скучная, долгая, многошаговая)  ·  [00:10](https://youtu.be/yj6_1t1PAcE?t=10)
**Мысль:** Избегание рождается из масштаба задачи: слишком большая, рутинная, долгая или с большим числом шагов.

> With ADHD, procrastination is huge. No one wants to do unpleasant things, but with ADHD, you can avoid doing things just because the scope of the task seems too great. It may have too many steps, or you think that it's gonna take too long. So, with cognitive behavior therapy, we call these aversive tasks, meaning they seem too painful to start or complete. And the usual reasons that they seem aversive are being too big to get started, it's boring and routine, it takes you too long and you lose interest in it, it has too many steps and you don't feel like doing all the steps.

**Для Напарника:** Диагностический вопрос напарника при застревании: что тут тяжело - размер, скука, длительность? Подбор шага под причину.


### Соедини самый неприятный подшаг с наградой; список наград под рукой  ·  [01:08](https://youtu.be/yj6_1t1PAcE?t=68)
**Мысль:** Список реалистичных наград (с их длительностью) заготовлен заранее; награда не еда, чтобы не закреплять эмоциональное переедание.

> Take the ones where you feel the most resistance to do it, and pair it with a rewarding task. Some examples of rewarding tasks would be watching something on television, going for a walk, group chatting with friends, listening to an interesting podcast episode. You don't want it to be something to eat, like having a slice of cake, because you don't wanna associate reward with food because that perpetuates emotional eating. It's best to create a list of rewarding activities to have on hand so that when you're faced with something that you don't wanna do, you can immediately pull from the list. On the list, include the time that it takes to complete the reward. If you can't think of things to do that you find rewarding, think of what you'd rather do instead. And it needs to be something that you'd reasonably do, not like, "I'd rather be on a trip to Bali." But if you think to yourself, "I don't feel like doing this. "I think I'm just gonna go watch an episode of Westworld," then, that becomes the reward that motivates you to finish the task.

**Для Напарника:** После Фокуса напарник предлагает мини-награду из собственного списка пользователя; сама награда всегда тёплая.


### Делай неприятное в приятной среде: иначе закрепляешь отвращение  ·  [03:00](https://youtu.be/yj6_1t1PAcE?t=180)
**Мысль:** Если просто продавливать, задача закрепляется как мучительная и сопротивление растёт; уютная обстановка снижает неприязнь в следующий раз.

> Another way you can use the reward system is do the pleasurable activity at the same time that you're doing the unpleasant one. So, with this strategy, you would refer to your planner to see the things that you would need to do that day. And then, are there things that you can pair with something enjoyable, like folding clothes while watching television, or listening to a podcast episode while you're exercising. Let's say there isn't a specific activity that you wanna do while you're performing the task, because it takes a lot of concentration or focus. In that case, find a way to create a soothing environment to do the activity. For example, instead of sitting at a desk to file an online insurance claim, do it outside on your deck, or sit in a cozy chair to do it. Comforting surroundings help offset the negative experience you have of completing the task. And this isn't something you naturally think to do. You have to purpose to do the unpleasant task in a pleasant environment. If you just push through, you may get it done, but you reinforce the experience as something really unpleasant. And if it's something you have to do again, it may be harder to push through the next time because you remember how much you hated doing it the last time. So, with this strategy, you're trying to make yourself hate it less, so there's less resistance to doing it the next time.

**Для Напарника:** Обоснование уютного фона и костра на экране Фокуса: сессия должна ощущаться приятной, чтобы к ней хотелось вернуться.


### Срочное вытесняет важное: отложенное само становится срочным  ·  [05:01](https://youtu.be/yj6_1t1PAcE?t=301)
**Мысль:** Мы идём за срочностью независимо от важности, а отложенные дела превращаются в пожары.

> We tend to be driven more by the things that are urgent, regardless of their importance. And things can become urgent simply because they were put off for a later time. So then, they end up taking a priority over the things that could have been more important, but just not urgent.

**Для Напарника:** Предлагать на Доме не список срочного, а одну важную, но не горящую вещь как следующий шаг.



# 🎬 How To Think Like A Game Designer — Game Maker's Toolkit


### MDA: механики, динамика, эстетика (чувства)  ·  [01:33](https://youtu.be/iIOIT3dCy5w?t=93)
**Мысль:** Механика (правила) порождает динамику (действия игрока) и эстетику (эмоции). Меняя число (запас патронов), каскадом меняешь поведение и чувства.

> Alright, so... way back in 2004, , three game designers got together and wrote an academic paper, featuring a framework that would prove to be perfect for analysing the mechanics in games. It's called MDA, and it breaks games down into three, neat sections: mechanics, dynamics, and aesthetics. Mechanics describes the actual workings of the game: like the rules, the systems, what the buttons do, and all of the individual stats and numbers. For example, one mechanic could be the maximum amount of ammo the player can hold - and we could set that number really high. Then, dynamics is how the player acts and behaves, in response to those mechanics. So because the player has tonnes of ammo, they may storm into battle, and shoot wildly in the general direction of every enemy they spot. And, finally, aesthetics - not to be confused with the graphics or the art style. Here, aesthetics is how the player feels when acting in that way. Their emotional response. So this wild shooting could make you feel reckless, powerful, unstoppable - basically, like this. *Machine gun fire* To put it another way - mechanics happen in the code, dynamics happen in the player's actions, and aesthetics happen in the player's feelings. Now, unfortunately, game designers can't just go inside the player's head and directly alter their actions or feelings. But they can change the code - and because MDA describes a casual relationship, these changes will cascade down and alter the dynamics and aesthetics. For example, if we tweak that knob the other way, and massively limit the player's ammo stash, that's definitely going to change the player's behaviour. Now, they will act cautiously before heading into battle, or might not engage enemies at all. Every shot fired will be done with careful consideration, and more time will be spent hunting for ammo in the environment. And this will lead to completely different aesthetics: like disempowerment, fear, and caution. So, this MDA framework takes mechanics - and then puts them into a wider context, which asks 'how do they make the player act, and how do they make the player feel?'.

**Для Напарника:** Анализировать каждую механику Напарника по цепочке: правило, что делает человек, что он при этом чувствует (облегчение, гордость, давление).


### Flower: убрали фичи, мешавшие спокойствию  ·  [05:04](https://youtu.be/iIOIT3dCy5w?t=304)
**Мысль:** Уровни, заклинания, ресурсы и таймеры вырезали, потому что они шли против намеченных эмоций покоя. Привычная «веселость» не всегда помогает нужному чувству.

> So MDA lets us see game mechanics as powerful vessels for delivering emotions to players. And so when borrowing mechanics from other games - or making entirely new ones - you can pick ones with associated dynamics and aesthetics that will compliment the rest of your design - and avoid ones that will clash. For instance, designer Jenova Chen says that the game Flower once had a level-up system, spells you could cast, resource management, and time limitations - expected features of other video games. But they all had to go, because they went against the intended emotions of relaxation, calm, and peace. Chen says "we’ve played so many games growing up these bad habits form. A lot of the time we like to make things very fun, but fun doesn’t always help the emotion you want to deliver".

**Для Напарника:** Для Напарника, чья эмоция тепло и присутствие, вычёркивать геймификацию, создающую гонку и тревогу (лидерборды, таймеры-давилки).


### Эстетика субъективна: счёт мотивирует одних и стыдит других  ·  [10:07](https://youtu.be/iIOIT3dCy5w?t=607)
**Мысль:** Система очков вызывает азарт у одного и чувство оценки у другого; давление времени бодрит одних и вызывает тревогу у других. Это зависит от навыка и склада.

> And finally - and perhaps most importantly - aesthetics are, ultimately, subjective. A scoring system might make one player feel competitive - and eager to replay every level. But it could make another player feel judged - and eager to get a Steam refund. Time pressure might feel fun and exhilarating to one person - but anxiety-inducing to another. And this is often related to skill and ability. Take the new Tango game, Hi-Fi Rush: it clearly wants you to feel like a rockstar as you brawl along to the beat - but as someone with zero musical ability, I just felt like a bumbling idiot.

**Для Напарника:** Не делать счёт, оценки и таймер-давление обязательными: дать способ скрыть цифры и видеть только мягкий прогресс.

# 🎬 How your brain's executive function works -- and how to improve it | Sabine Doebel — TED


### Контекст и группа: дети ждут дольше, если «их группа» ждала  ·  [05:11](https://youtu.be/qAC-5hTK-4c?t=311)
**Мысль:** Вера, что группа ждала второй зефир, повышала выдержку и ценность ожидания; контекст важнее «хорошей» или «плохой» исполнительной функции.

> Now let me give you an example from my research. I recently brought in a bunch of kids to do the classic marshmallow test, which is a measure of delay of gratification that also likely requires a lot of executive function. So you may have heard about this test, but basically, kids are given a choice. They can have one marshmallow right away, or if they can wait for me to go to the other room and get more marshmallows, they can have two instead. Now, most kids really want that second marshmallow, but the key question is: How long can they wait? (Laughter) Now, I added a twist to look at the effects of context. I told each kid that they were in a group, like the green group, and I even gave them a green T-shirt to wear. And I said, "Your group waited for two marshmallows, and this other group, the orange group, did not." Or I said the opposite: "Your group didn't wait for two marshmallows and this other group did." And then I left the kid alone in the room and I watched on a webcam to see how long they waited. (Laughter) So what I found was that kids who believed that their group waited for two marshmallows were themselves more likely to wait. So they were influenced by a peer group that they'd never even met. (Laughter) Pretty cool, isn't it? Well, so with this result I still didn't know if they were just copying their group or if it was something deeper than that. So I brought in some more kids, and after the marshmallow test, I showed them pictures of pairs of kids, and I told them, "One of these kids likes to have things right away, like cookies and stickers. And the other kid likes to wait so that they can have more of these things." And then I asked them, "Which one of these two kids do you like more and who would you want to play with?" And what I found was that kids who believed that their group waited tended to prefer other kids who liked to wait for things. So learning what their group did made them value waiting more. And not only that, these kids likely used executive function to generate strategies to help themselves wait, like sitting on their hands or turning away from the marshmallow or singing a song to distract themselves. (Laughter) So what this all shows is just how much context matters. It's not that these kids had good executive function or bad, it's that the context helped them use it better.

**Для Напарника:** Социальное присутствие (кот-напарник, «мы вместе») повышает способность держаться; фраза «мы с тобой в этом».



# 🎬 I downloaded the Finch self care app! Here's how that's going. (Unsponsored review.) — Crash The Robot


### Пользователь с СДВГ: приложение мягко установило новый базовый уровень  ·  [03:18](https://youtu.be/gEA7hxHfJ_8?t=198)
**Мысль:** Человек с СДВГ и депрессией, которому тяжело вставать, за пять месяцев мягко выстроил базу (питание, гигиена, чистота) и держит её.

> I've been using the free version of Finch for approximately 5 months now and as I've said it's helped me a lot I might even say it's changed my life I downloaded it because ADHD low-key clinical depression and a general lack of energy had me struggling to get out of bed and do anything Finch slowly and gently helped me establish a new Baseline of eating pretty regularly doing okay in the personal hygiene department and keeping the kitchen and bathrooms clean I've since managed to maintain this Baseline reasonably well despite my brain being full of chaos goblins and sadness.

**Для Напарника:** Цель Напарника: «базовый уровень» мелких шагов, а не героические рывки; тон без давления.

> To sum up my guess is that the finch app might not be very motivating to people who don't care much about fashion or home decor or folks who need the threat of consequences or punishments in order to get things done but for me it's been great.

**Для Напарника:** Не переобещать: Напарник для тех, кому подходит тёплый, поощряющий подход.



# 🎬 I Gamified My Life For A Week using Habitica — John Mavrick Ch.


### Награды в реальной жизни: не должны позволять избегать задач и не деньги  ·  [02:56](https://youtu.be/Zo0adzv9t98?t=176)
**Мысль:** Реальные награды за золото могут позволить увильнуть от задач и быстро истощить кошелёк; продуманная система вознаграждений непроста.

The only problem is, I don't want them to let me avoid certain tasks since that would go against my reason for using the app. I also don't want them to be monetary-based since I would drain my wallet way too fast. I'll get back to it some other time.

**Для Напарника:** Награды Напарника (остров, предметы) не должны отменять усилие и не должны быть денежными; связаны с действием.

**Для Напарника:** Лёгкий социальный слой (общие предметы/остров) усиливает ценность без давления.
