**Мысль:** Золото и опыт напоминают о реальной награде за дело; рост персонажа помогает увидеть собственный долгосрочный рост; история для персонажа усиливает погружение.

> Aside from the in-game benefits, Habitica's aspects of gamification help me be more mindful of the impact my actions have on improving my life. For example, whenever I receive my gold and experience rewards, I'm prompted to also think about the real-life rewards that accompany completing a task, which helps me find solace amidst the daily grind. Furthermore, as my character levels up, I can't help but visualize my own long-term growth achieved from actually getting things done. I didn't go too much into it, but if you're creative enough, I would consider creating a story for your character to make the process more immersive.

**Для Напарника:** Остров как видимый след проделанного; нарратив мира усиливает связь с реальными делами.



# 🎬 I Realized My Game’s Rewards Were Boring — RookieDev


### Два вида награды: делает сильнее и меняет, как ты думаешь  ·  [01:49](https://youtu.be/sW2U1_2_zNE?t=109)
**Мысль:** Запоминаются награды, меняющие взгляд на систему: перегрев из наказания превращается в тактическую возможность. Награда привязана к тому, что игрок сделал, поэтому её можно проследить.

> I started noticing that there are two very different kinds of reward in my game. Some rewards just make you stronger. Other rewards change what you're thinking about. And those second ones are the ones I remember. For example, Stellar Breaker has these things called discoveries. You get some of them from doing specific things in the game. Destroy Mercury and you can unlock Mercury core. That gives the cannon more damage while heat is low. Pretty simple. But I like that reward more than giving the player a random pile of currency because I can point at it and remember where it came from. I destroyed Mercury. Mercury gave me this thing. There's a little connection there. Then there's furnace seal. You get it from a major boss. Normally overheating your cannon is bad. You push too far, the cannon locks. Now you wait. Furnace seal gives your fleet extra damage while that lockout is happening. And that one I really like because now overheat isn't just a punishment anymore. Maybe I want to overheat. Maybe I push it on purpose right before a good damage window. The reward didn't just increase damage. It changed how I look at a system that was already there. That feels much better to me.

**Для Напарника:** Награды острова привязывать к конкретному поступку (закончил шаг на утреннюю тему -> появился такой-то объект), чтобы игрок помнил, откуда оно.


### Важная веха должна открывать возможность, а не ещё один процент  ·  [04:59](https://youtu.be/sW2U1_2_zNE?t=299)
**Мысль:** После 20 минут усилий хочется сказать Теперь я могу это, а не получить 1.15x. Простые числовые бонусы допустимы, но не на ключевых вехах.

> The problem is when every important milestone gives me another number. If I spend 20 minutes reaching something important, I want at least some of those moments to make me say, "Oh, I can do this now." Not, "Oh, 1.15x.".

**Для Напарника:** Вехи на острове (первая неделя, 10-я сессия) лучше выдавать как новое действие или существо, а не как счётчик.


### Лучшие достижения влияют на игру ещё до получения  ·  [07:46](https://youtu.be/sW2U1_2_zNE?t=466)
**Мысль:** Достижения-челленджи заставляют придумывать, как выполнить условие, и тем самым уже меняют поведение, в отличие от простых отметок о достигнутой стадии.

> I think my achievements have the same split. Some of them are just milestones. Reach stage 500. Reach stage 750, reach stage 1,000. Those are fine. They're useful as markers, but they're not really the achievements I enjoy designing. There's one called vent timing. You have to use coolant vent when your cannon is almost overheated. That means you actually have to understand the heat system. Skeleton Crew asks you to beat a boss while leveling only a small number of ships. Frugal Ascent asks you to reach a certain point without buying run upgrades. Monod Doctrine pushes you toward getting almost all of your fleet damage from one class. Those are more fun to me because the achievement starts affecting you before you unlock it. You look at the requirement and think, "Okay, how am I going to do that?" Now, the reward has already changed how you're playing.

**Для Напарника:** Мягкие необязательные цели (например, начать шаг до 5 минут) как цель-приглашение, без стыда за невыполнение.



# 🎬 I Studied 1,460 Onboarding Flows. Here's What I Found. — Mobbin


### Показать, что открыли ответы: персональный план и срок цели  ·  [03:43](https://youtu.be/Qsq-Sj_rojU?t=223)
**Мысль:** После квиза приложения возвращают человеку результат (план, дату достижения цели, подобранный контент), и продукт ощущается рабочим ещё до использования.

> Some apps don't just collect answers during the quiz. They actually show you what those answers unlocked. So, in Endless onboarding, you answer six questions, and then they show you this. You haven't even used the product yet, but it already feels like it's going to work. Byte Pal does the same thing. After the quiz, they build your personal plan and then tell you exactly when you'll hit your goal. Brilliant shows you courses that are personalized to your responses. As soon as you finish your onboarding flow, your home page is already populated with only the content that you want to see. Here's another one by Speak, a language learning app. They ask you what language you'd like to learn and your goals. Then, in one simple screen, it tells you, "In 2 months, you'll be able to communicate while traveling in France." There's a simple graph showing that speaking helps you reach your goals faster than reading. The steps before this screen already had you speaking instead of typing.

**Для Напарника:** После ответа на вопрос в чек-ине Напарник сразу меняется (кот реагирует, остров подстраивается). Конкретные сроки достижения цели не обещать: это давление и риск стыда.



# 🎬 I Studied 1000 Landing Pages, Here's What Works in 2026 — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Решения принимаются эмоцией, потом оправдываются логикой  ·  [05:02](https://youtu.be/PcwuZJNJny4?t=302)
**Мысль:** Человек приходит с чувством (фрустрация или мечта об улучшении); образы счастливых людей резонируют (2,5% → 5%).

> Lesson number two is that people buy with emotion, not logic. We like to think as humans that we're logical decision makers, but the reality is that people tend to make decisions with emotion first, then they rationalize it with logic. So when a person comes to your landing page, they're feeling something. Maybe they're frustrated by some deep rooted problem in their life, or maybe they're dreaming of an endstate improvement in their life. Whatever it may be, that is the feeling that you need to tap into on your page. One of the easiest ways you can do this is through the form of images. Right? As the saying goes, a picture is worth a thousand words. And this couldn't be more true for your landing page. The human brain processes images 60,000 times faster than text. We did this for education client who caters towards children where you can see scattered throughout the page are tons and tons of photos of smiling and happy children that really resonates with their target audience, which is parents, and really hits home. This was one of the many changes that we made on this client's landing page that took them from a 2.5% conversion rate to a 5% conversion rate on meta ads and basically book them out with leads.

**Для Напарника:** Образы на лендинге — тёплые и живые (кот, остров, уют), а не абстрактные скриншоты.



# 🎬 I Studied 500+ Gamified Apps (Here's What Actually Works) — Tim Gabe


### Strava: тысячи выигрываемых микро-соревнований вместо одного глобального рейтинга  ·  [03:15](https://youtu.be/LXX_qOA5D8E?t=195)
**Мысль:** Выигрываемость — сильнейший предиктор соревновательной мотивации; размер соревнования нужно конструировать.

> Strava didn't build one global leaderboard, they built thousands of hyper-local micro-competitions. The hill on your morning route, the segment outside your office. These competitions are winnable and winnability is the strongest predictor of competitive motivation. A 2022 Science Direct study confirmed this. Kudos on Strava, the small social acknowledgement when someone completes a run, increase future run frequency. And the social side has compounded fast. Strava's clubs grew 59% in 2024 alone, and the platform handed out 14 billion kudos in 2025. Strava's lesson for founders is direct. Enable users to compete more locally. Engineer the size of the competition rather than inflating empty metrics.

**Для Напарника:** Не применимо социальное сравнение при СДВГ; адаптировать как «выигрываемые» личные цели: побить свой вчерашний результат на 5 минут.


### Кольца Apple Watch: принцип замыкания (completion drive) даёт 49.5% изменения поведения  ·  [09:04](https://youtu.be/LXX_qOA5D8E?t=544)
**Мысль:** Недозаполненный круг создаёт открытую петлю, и мозг хочет её закрыть; награда несёт реальный результат, а не театр вовлечения.

> Now, the Apple Watch thing I told you was possibly the most important part of this entire video. Here's what they actually did. They built the entire system around one principle, completion drive. And that's how they got 49.5% behavior change in 160,000 people. The activity ring system is possibly the most underrated gamification design pattern in consumer tech. They've got three rings: move, exercise, stand. Close all three to complete your day. The system runs on one principle from cognitive psychology, the Gestalt principle of closure. The brain is hardwired to perceive incomplete patterns as demanding completion. A 90% filled circle creates an open loop, and the brain wants to close it. Users who regularly close rings are 48% less likely to experience poor sleep quality. This is exactly what great gamification design is all about. The mechanic produces a real-world positive outcomes, not engagement theater. And here's the part most founders miss. Apple's rings work because they make you want to finish what you started. Anticipation toward closure. That's the engine.

**Для Напарника:** Кольцо прогресса сессии и дневной остров как замыкаемая форма; тянуть к завершению, а не пугать потерей.


### Компетентность против «театра бейджей»: награждать за реальное улучшение навыка  ·  [10:33](https://youtu.be/LXX_qOA5D8E?t=633)
**Мысль:** Метаанализ: геймификация усиливает автономию и связанность, но слабо влияет на компетентность, главную для долгой внутренней мотивации; Peloton/Chess.com дают обратную связь о мастерстве.

> Pattern seven builds directly on this. I call it competence versus badge theater. The 2024 Springer Nature meta-analysis on gamification found something brutal. Gamification reliably improves a user's perception of autonomy and relatedness, but has minimal impact on competence, the one psychological need most tied to long-term intrinsic motivation. Most apps engineer recognition and forget to engineer mastery. Peloton is the cleanest counterexample of this. Members who use the social and output features work out 15% more frequently, but the mechanism isn't competition, it's competence feedback. Output is measured in real time. Personal records gets auto-flagged. The 100 ride badge means something because it represents 100 actual rides. That's not theater, that's evidence of skill development. Chess.com runs the same play with ELO ratings. Garmin's training readiness and body battery scores do it in fitness wearables. Build mechanics that signal you got better at the actual thing, not mechanics that signal you opened the app a lot.

**Для Напарника:** Показывать на Доме/Мире реальные признаки роста навыка фокуса (дольше держишься, быстрее стартуешь), а не количество заходов.



# 🎬 Idle Games: An Analysis — Game Soup


### Не обязательно «весело», чтобы быть затягивающим  ·  [01:27](https://youtu.be/2lEDS6Of1i4?t=87)
**Мысль:** Инкрементальные игры не очень веселы по механике, но очень притягательны: прогресс ради прогресса удерживает без эмерджентных взаимодействий.

> Clicker games are simultaneously some of the simplest games and some of the most addicting games I've ever played I'd hesitate to call Idol games fun because their mechanical structure necessarily foros the traditional player to game interactions that allow for an emergent experience but just because something isn't insanely fun doesn't mean that it can't be insanely compelling.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник может притягивать ощущением прогресса (остров растёт), не требуя развлекательной механики.


### Flow не описывает кликеры: притягивающий опыт, не игра  ·  [03:32](https://youtu.be/2lEDS6Of1i4?t=212)
**Мысль:** Нет навыка и сложности, чтобы строить график потока; автор считает кликеры скорее притягательным опытом, чем игрой.

> We've already talked at length about flow Theory and video games on this channel in the past the general idea being that as long as you're balancing player skill and game difficulty to stay within this special range here your game is probably pretty enjoyable but if we try to plot out what a game like clicker Heroes might look like on this chart we run into a few major problems there's no specific skill that a player needs or will learn and there's no level of challenge that can restrict your progress so we actually can't assess our flow through either axis so do skill and challenge really have nothing to do with it I mean you can certainly watch a film or listen to an album without any prerequisite skill or challenge but what about a video game this is why I tend to believe that clickers are more accurately defined as compelling experiences rather than compelling games.

**Для Напарника:** Не пытаться обязательно держать пользователя в потоке; приемлемо создавать лёгкое притягательное присутствие.


### Качественный успех: никто не говорит тебе, что ты провалился  ·  [07:40](https://youtu.be/2lEDS6Of1i4?t=460)
**Мысль:** В Dark Souls и Hearthstone игра считает ваши победы и поражения; в idle-играх нет условий победы или провала, и успех определяется субъективным восприятием прогресса.

> Important advantage to idle games that many modern AAA titles lack a subjective or qualitative success to give you an idea of what I mean by qualitative success let's look at the counterpart quantitative success if you die to an enemy ambush in Dark Souls you invoke a fail state which involves the game explicitly telling you that you died and you get sent back to the last bonfire at the end of your journey you can count up all the times you died and have a quantitative measure of all the times the game called your failures along the same lines when you defeat an opponent in Hearthstone this is an unqualified success a quantitative measure of your Victory the other guy of course gets a quantitative failure State when he loses but Idol games by contrast have no Victory or failure conditions you can't reach a point where your reflexes or logic aren't sufficient enough to continue likewise there's no amount of reflexes or logic that will get you to the end credits because they don't exist either so the barrier for entry is effectively zero but with all the Allure and satisfaction of sweet arbitrary progression and since games do lack Victory or failure conditions then whether you're succeeding or failing is entirely up to your own mind regardless of your actual efficiency your perception of progress in an idol game is unique to you and completely uninfluenced by those pesky quantitative measures that come from the game itself.

**Для Напарника:** Прямая поддержка принципа «без стыда»: не показывать счётчик провалов и невыполненного; прогресс определяется ощущением человека.



# 🎬 Idle Games: An Analysis — Game Soup Analysis


### Качественный успех: нет победы и поражения, всё решает восприятие игрока  ·  [07:40](https://youtu.be/SEBTKjD0x7M?t=460)
**Мысль:** Без состояний провала барьер входа нулевой, а каждый апгрейд ощущается успехом без болезненного контраста поражения. Игрок по умолчанию идёт в своём темпе.

> Important advantage to idle games that many modern AAA titles lack a subjective or qualitative success to give you an idea of what I mean by qualitative success let's look at the counterpart quantitative success if you die to an enemy ambush in Dark Souls you invoke a fail state which involves the game explicitly telling you that you died and you get sent back to the last bonfire at the end of your journey you can count up all the times you died and have a quantitative measure of all the times the game called your failures along the same lines when you defeat an opponent in Hearthstone this is an unqualified success a quantitative measure of your Victory the other guy of course gets a quantitative failure State when he loses but idle games by contrast have no Victory or failure conditions you can't reach a point where your reflexes or logic aren't sufficient enough to continue you likewise there's no amount of reflexes or logic that will get you to the end credits because they don't exist either so the barrier for entry is effectively zero but with all the Allure and satisfaction of sweet arbitrary progression and since games do lack Victory or failure conditions then whether you're succeeding or failing is entirely up to your own mind regardless of your actual efficiency your perception of progress in an idol game is unique to you and completely uninfluenced by those pesky quantitative measures that come from the game itself more often than not an idle game becomes more and more rewarding the more attention you're actively putting into it's just as easy to play clicker Heroes for an hour in a full-screen window as it is to leave it running in a browser Tab and just check on it every few minutes and even after tens or hundreds of hours idle games can still be compelling every upgrade feels like a success but you never feel that contrasting sting of failure you're encouraged by default to take things at your own pace whether it's 5 minutes a day or 5 hours there are no complex controls that require your dexterity no complex puzzle that require your full attention and in general idle games will just keep on trucking until you've got some time to come back and manage them.

**Для Напарника:** Базовый принцип без стыда: любой шаг считается успехом, нет красных состояний провала; игрок сам определяет темп.



# 🎬 If You Don't Understand Psychology, You Don't Understand Landing Pages — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Продавай глубинное желание, а не результат  ·  [01:15](https://youtu.be/s_wGr2TYeHU?t=75)
**Мысль:** Результат (больше денег, меньше веса) описывает, что человек получает, но не зачем: безопасность, спокойствие, желание быть привлекательным, играть с детьми. Именно «зачем» запускает действие.

> You've probably heard the advice sell outcomes, not features, not deliverables. People buy outcomes. And while this is true, I like to go one layer deeper, which leads me into principle one of this video, which is to sell the core desire, not outcomes. Let me explain. So, I see landing pages all the time, right, that sell the obvious outcome, things like make X more money, lose 20 lbs, get clearer skin, etc. And there's nothing wrong with this on the surface level. But, the issue is that this only describes what the person gets. It completely skips why the person actually wants it. And that why is where all of the buying actually happens. Take the example of someone trying to earn more in their career. Look, a lot of people obviously care about the money, the monetary aspect, but they don't care about the money itself on its own. What they're actually buying into as a byproduct, right? If you think deeper, it's not just the money. It gives them that sense of security, that sense of safety, and the peace of mind. Another great example is weight loss, right? Obviously, the benefit of weight loss is people want to lose weight, right? That's their end goal. But, go a couple layers deeper. Why do they want to lose weight? What they're actually buying is they want to feel attractive again, right? Or they want to be able to play with their kids 20 years into the future and live a long, healthy life. So, the outcome is just the surface. The core desire underneath it is what actually makes somebody take action.

**Для Напарника:** В тексте: не «повысь продуктивность», а «наконец-то перестать чувствовать вину и расслабленно жить»; тон про облегчение и спокойствие.


### Life Force 8: восемь биологических желаний как карта для заголовков  ·  [02:33](https://youtu.be/s_wGr2TYeHU?t=153)
**Мысль:** Забота о близких, свобода от страха и боли, социальное одобрение, превосходство, комфорт, выживание и долгая жизнь и др.; заголовки привязываются к конкретному желанию, что дало заметные приросты.

> Is called Life Force 8, and it's coined by Drew Whitman. The premise of Life Force 8 is that there's eight core desires that every human is biologically wired with, and by tapping into these core desires, you're able to drive people to take action. So, here are all of the eight principles. I'm going to go through them rapid-fire with examples. Desire number one is care and protection of loved ones. This is the innate primal human desire to ensure the safety and prosperity of your loved ones, whether that's your family, your kids, your husband, your wife, girlfriend, boyfriend, etc. Desire number two is freedom from fear, pain, and danger, right? So, wanting to feel safe and secure. So, we hit on both of these for our pool fence client in their headline, where we said, "Protect your kids," right? Which appeals to desire number one, and don't wait until it's too late, which appeals to desire number two. Next is desire number three, which is social approval. This is the innate human desire to be accepted, liked, validated by others. So, for our lawn care client, for example, in the headline, we say, "Get a lawn your neighbors will envy," right? Which appeals to this desire. Desire number four is to be superior and winning, right? so, things like status, success, etc. So, for this film producer course, right? On their landing page, we went with the headline, "Travel the world on global sex and connect with top creators," right? Which appeals to that sense of status, and we showed course members filming Brian Shaw, who's a famous celebrity. Desire number five is sexual companionship, right? Wanting to feel attractive and desired. So, for our Med Spa client, we specifically pulled a testimonial about all of the compliments that this person started getting, right? We intentionally picked this out. Desire number six is comfortable living, right? Wanting things like physical comfort, a nice home, relaxation, right? Things like that. So, for a bathroom remodeling client, the headline focuses on precisely that, right? Focusing on the comfort with an image to complement. Desire number seven is survival, enjoyment of life, and living a long fruitful life. So, for a personal training client, the headline focuses on precisely that beautifully. And lastly, number eight is enjoyment of food and drink, right? It's a little bit rare, but here's an example for our nutrition client that used this. Applying this one principle has gotten us some insane conversion lifts because we're always operating under this mindset of how do we appeal to these desires.

**Для Напарника:** Для Напарника наиболее подходят «свобода от страха/боли» (без вины и стыда), «комфортная жизнь» и «социальное одобрение» (мягко); примеры с сексуальным подтекстом не применимы.


### Неприятие потерь: потеря ощущается вдвое сильнее выигрыша  ·  [08:13](https://youtu.be/s_wGr2TYeHU?t=493)
**Мысль:** Сильное закрытие показывает, что человек теряет, не действуя; заголовок про потери дал результат у логистического клиента.

> The fourth psychological principle is loss aversion. In fact, typically losing feels twice as bad as winning feels good. So, when you think about your landing page, right? Most pages just talk about what you gain. A stronger close also will show the visitor what they have to lose if they don't take action. So, here's a good example for our shipping client, right? They solve the problem of basically helping, you know, e-commerce companies recover hidden fees that they're actually owed from UPS and FedEx. So, we lead with the headline on their page that says, "You're losing 30k to 50k on every $1 million shipment. You just don't know it yet." And then right underneath that, we proceed to talk about all of the different things that they're losing out on as a byproduct of not fixing this pressing issue.

**Для Напарника:** Не применимо: давление потерей (теряешь стрик, остров угаснет) противоречит принципу «без стыда и вины». Допустимо лишь как мягкое «твой остров ждёт».



# 🎬 If You Don’t Understand Psychology, You Don’t Understand Web Design — Sam Crawford | Web Design Expert


### Эффект умолчания: предвыбранное сохраняют большинство, и это ответственность  ·  [08:27](https://youtu.be/J7LWfqWbx1o?t=507)
**Мысль:** Донорство (opt-out ~90%) показывает силу дефолта; ставить по умолчанию то, что честно посоветовал бы другу, иначе выиграешь клик и потеряешь доверие.

> Whatever option you preselect for the visitor, most of them will keep because at the end of the day, we're all wired to take the path of least resistance. The most cited example of this is actually not from the web at all. Countries where organ donation is set up so that you're enrolled unless you opt out have consent rates up around 90% while countries where you have to actively opt in, they sit way, way lower. Same people, same values, only thing that changed was which box was already ticked. Now, let's apply that to a website. A great example is the billing toggle that defaults to annual rather than monthly or the plan that arrives already marked as recommended and preselected for the visitor. That streaming service that plays the next episode unless you stop it. None of these force anyone, they simply set the starting point, and the starting point carries enormous weight because most people just won't opt out of the default. This is also where you carry a responsibility because the same power can be used against people and the brain comes to resent it when it notices. So, as an example, a pre-ticked box that signs someone up to something that they didn't want is the kind of default that, okay, yes, it wins the click, but it loses the trust both in the short and in the long term. The scale and I guess the nuance is to set defaults that serve the visitor. E.g., the plan that fits most people, which is usually the choice that you'd honestly recommend to a friend. What tends to happen here is the easy path and the right path tend to be the same path. So, look across your site, every place where a choice is being made, and then ask yourself what's currently selected by default because in practice, you are making that decision for most of your visitors, whether you intend to or not.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает «25 минут — рекомендуем» на Фокусе; никаких предотмеченных подписок.



# 🎬 INBOUND Bold Talks: Amber Case "Calm Tech: Designing for the Internet of Things" — HubSpot Marketing


### Вибрация осанки вместо уведомления: личный сигнал не вызывает сопротивления  ·  [10:08](https://youtu.be/0WOMe6cg0yc?t=608)
**Мысль:** Напоминание текстом раздражает и вызывает «нет, не буду»; тихий буз постепенно меняет поведение.

> You don't need to use a disembodied voice to get your point across right you can use something like haptics or a sense of touch so this is my favorite example of that I can see people kind of like straightening up right now so this is a smart posture sensor and you wear it around your back and when you're slouching it sends you a buzz right it's like a mini version of your mom being like sit up straight that's bad posture like sit there but what it does is it's a personal alert so it's not giving you a notification on your phone hey you need to sit up straight which you would then resent and say no I'm not going to sit up straight it just sends you a buzz and so over time you just sit up straight normally I'm not worrying that right now I should probably do that but it's a really fantastic device and it's a really great way to use other senses than just Sight and Sound in order to give somebody an alert.

**Для Напарника:** Мягкая хаптика на события (старт/финал), а не текстовые упрёки; согласуется с принципом хаптики на события.



# 🎬 Instructional Design Basics: Cognitive Load — Instructional Design Tips


### Внутреннюю сложность нельзя убирать, её нужно дозировать  ·  [02:21](https://youtu.be/1jvnKTtmqF0?t=141)
**Мысль:** Внутренняя нагрузка это сам процесс обучения, преодоление которого даёт чувство достижения; управляется нарезкой (chunking).

> This intrinsic load is the process of learning ultimately it's the complexity of what you're trying to get your head around and overcoming that gives the user a real sense of achievement so we don't want to take it away even if we could what we do want to do though is manage it the main way we do that is again probably one of the most foundational things instructional designers learn chunking we break down large complex Concepts or pieces of information into smaller more manageable pieces this might be all delivered in the same modality but in different sections this might be split up further this might be a syllabus rather than a single module or it might be multiple syllabuses across multiple courses the important thing is that we recognize that we cannot remove intrinsic cognitive load but we can manage it by breaking content up.

**Для Напарника:** Дробление задач на микрошаги сохраняет чувство достижения, а не лишает его.



# 🎬 Is Finch Worth Downloading? | Honest Review | Living with Sight loss — Seren Jaye | Otters Have Pockets


### Награда за скучное и трудное, признание усилия и хаптика даже за часть  ·  [01:39](https://youtu.be/4SE9h3K3n-M?t=99)
**Мысль:** Пользователь с трудностями в рутине (чистить зубы, встать с кровати) получает энергию и признание за простые шаги; даже частично выполненная повторяющаяся задача даёт хаптик-поздравление.

> I love that these ones are kind of the examples because they're so simple and actually it recognizes that sometimes they do take a lot of effort some days it's just really difficult to get out of bed so getting some sort of recognition from it is amazing then I tend to clean my teeth and I'm absolutely rubbish at cleaning my teeth so this is actually one that I definitely put on here because it's so important and you can set them so that you have to do them more than once a day so obviously I do cleaning my teeth twice a day I'm very particular about how I do my teeth it's not something that I enjoy I mean lots of people would enjoy it's a particularly mundane task I hate the I hate the feeling of the toothpaste in my mouth I hate the sound just everything grosses me off so getting some little energy for doing something that I really find cool is very much needed or I just wouldn't wouldn't be able to bring myself to do it so even when you're doing tasks that you have to do two of it's still gives you the little haptic feedback to like congratulate you on doing it even if you've only done technically half of the task.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник может признавать усилие в мелких шагах («встать — уже шаг») и давать хаптику на событие завершения даже частичного шага, без стыда.


### Награда за действие снимает зависимость от внешнего успеха  ·  [04:35](https://youtu.be/4SE9h3K3n-M?t=275)
**Мысль:** Когда результат непредсказуем (видео может не зайти), небольшая внутренняя награда за сам факт действия поддерживает мотивацию.

> Especially as they're so hit and miss with like whether they're actually going to do well like I'm I'm not really very familiar with YouTube so I don't know for me what does well and what doesn't yet because I've got that little bit of encouragement it doesn't matter as much if the video doesn't do as well because there's still some motivation behind it other than just interacting with people.

**Для Напарника:** Награждать за сам запуск сессии, а не только за результат; сообщения «уже хорошо, что начал».


### Цель «сделай что-то приятное» с осознанием причины  ·  [04:56](https://youtu.be/4SE9h3K3n-M?t=296)
**Мысль:** Приятное действие любого масштаба, от конфеты до скалолазания; важно осознавать, что делаешь это ради себя.

> One of my tasks that I really like is my do something that makes you happy because this can be literally anything it can be really tiny like give myself a little sweet treat or it can be like going climbing like I really enjoy going climbing with my partner but sometimes it's nice to remember that you need to do that like you can just do it as you go along but actually it's quite nice to recognize that's what you're doing and that's why you're doing it to make yourself happy.

**Для Напарника:** В чек-ине Дома можно предлагать «одно приятное дело» как законную цель дня, не только продуктивные.


### Чувство «не хочу врать питомцу» как мягкое обязательство  ·  [06:25](https://youtu.be/4SE9h3K3n-M?t=385)
**Мысль:** Привязанность к питомцу создаёт честность и мягкое давление: отмечать нужно реально сделанное, иначе кажется, что обманываешь друга.

> Because like I need to put it away before I go to bed I was tick all the things I'm going to do off upstairs before I go and then it forces me to do it because otherwise I feel like I'm lying and I don't want to lie to my little bird.

**Для Напарника:** Присутствие кота как причина быть честным с собой; но формулировать без вины (внутренняя ответственность, а не упрёк).



# 🎬 Making big ideas happen with design — Dive Club 🤿


### Расплывчатый риск включает «бей или беги»; конкретизация переводит в режим решения  ·  [03:22](https://youtu.be/tWePWqWXff8?t=202)
**Мысль:** Когда много «а если», человек играет безопасно; превращение неосязаемого страха в конкретные вопросы и недорогие действия выводит из режима «страшно и абстрактно» в режим решения проблем.

> This is just evolutionarily biologically how humans behave when we assess risk we're going to fight or flight we're going to start to shut down we're going to start to do this Mental Math of you know what could this cost me if I screw up this big product am I going to get fired am I going to not get that promotion right and if there's too many wh ifs I'm going to play it a lot safer and so by taking that intangible thing and making it more tangible by saying how are we tactically going to mitigate this now how what do we need to learn what questions do we need to ask how can we do that in a cost- effective way that gets people out of this I don't know this is scary this is too abstract mode and gets that brain into problem solving mode of well we if we just went and talked to 20 people then we would feel pretty confident that we're doing the right thing.

**Для Напарника:** Стартовый экран Фокуса: превращать расплывчатую большую задачу в один конкретный, дешёвый шаг («открой файл», «5 минут»), чтобы убрать паралич и стыд.



# 🎬 Mechanically Speaking: Shooting in First Person - [game array] — Mason C Miller


### Воспринимаемая сложность выше реальной держит в потоке  ·  [03:21](https://youtu.be/13iMO9s-q0c?t=201)
**Мысль:** Скрытые поблажки (ghost jump, снисходительные хитбоксы) повышают воспринимаемую сложность и сохраняют воспринимаемое мастерство игрока высоким.

> Or more specifically, the balance between the player’s perceived skill and perceived challenge. In 1987 Csikzentmihalyi helped publish what is now known as the Experience Fluctuation Model. The top right corner, where perceived skill and perceived challenge are both higher than average, is where flow is most likely to occur. Game designers need to do whatever they can to help the player reach that upper right most section. But it’s important to mention that perceived challenge isn’t the same as actual challenge. Let’s look back at jumping. In that video we said it’s important to make 2D platformers easier in subtle ways with ghost jumps and lenient hitboxes. What we’re really doing here is raising the game’s perceived challenge higher than its actual challenge. By doing that, we keep the player’s perceived skill higher than his actual skill and by doing both, we keep the player in the top right corner of the Fluctuation Model and closer to being in Flow.

**Для Напарника:** Подстраивать шаги Напарника, чтобы задача казалась достижимой: микрошаги, мягкие допуски, незаметные поблажки.


### Мотивация компетентностью  ·  [06:30](https://youtu.be/13iMO9s-q0c?t=390)
**Мысль:** Чем компетентнее себя чувствует игрок, тем больше хочет этого; исследование 2009 года: ключевой мотиватор шутеров — компетентность, а не насилие.

> By making these weapons more powerful when used by the player, but easier to play against, we increase the player’s perceived skill. As a player’s perceived skill grows in tune with a game’s challenge, they begin to feel more and more competent. The more competent they feel, the more they want to feel competent. This theoretical cycle is called Competence Motivation. In 2009 researchers found that competence motivation was the key motivating factor in violent shooter games, not the violence itself.

**Для Напарника:** Ключевое чувство для Напарника — «я справляюсь»: делать так, чтобы каждая сессия оставляла ощущение компетентности.


### Поток держится на быстрых решениях в конце каждого цикла  ·  [09:28](https://youtu.be/13iMO9s-q0c?t=568)
**Мысль:** В Doom цикл всегда начинается и заканчивается выбором; в паузе игрок формирует ментальную модель и принимает следующее решение.

> But it really isn’t about moving either. It’s about decision making. If you look at the entire combat cycle, it always ends and starts with a choice. You choose to shoot. Feedback tells you whether or not you hit. You make another choice. To keep firing or change up. Eventually the reload, the rate of fire or some other mechanic forces you into cover, to retreat or strafe. The combat cycle ends. And when the combat cycle ends there’s just enough time for the player to create a mental model of the game. It’s off this mental model that he makes a choice. It’s ultimately these quick fire decisions that keep us in flow and make FPSs so addictive. Simply put: we have to present the player with clear and interesting decisions at the end of each combat cycle.

**Для Напарника:** В конце каждой сессии давать один ясный интересный выбор (продолжить, отдохнуть, следующий шаг), а не длинное меню.



# 🎬 Mobile App Onboarding: The good, the bad and the ugly — Pulsate HQ


### Первый успех празднуй тёплым голосом, а не сухим «добавлено»  ·  [04:25](https://youtu.be/Zqgul4DTdes?t=265)
**Мысль:** Вместо скучного «to-do added successfully» пишут живую реплику с «хай-файвом» и сразу предлагают следующий шаг, закрепляя положительное подкрепление.

> Spike feelings of accomplishment we talked about this in our loyalty video and how you can use this your advantage so when I add the first to do when a book that flight make a big deal out of this we try to go overboard you know if they add that for us to do don't say you know to do added successfully and a very boring voice you want to be like you know Hera high fives congrats you've just added your first to-do item now why not assign a date to it and then you're moving on to the net the next success milestone.

**Для Напарника:** Первая завершённая сессия: кот отвечает тепло и по-своему, плюс один мягкий следующий шаг; без формулировок про нехватку.


### Люди скачивают приложение за результатом, а не за функциями  ·  [07:23](https://youtu.be/Zqgul4DTdes?t=443)
**Мысль:** Бенефит-онбординг говорит о причинах загрузки и явных результатах, и это повышает активацию.

> Terms of onboarding methods there are a variety of ways that you can do this I personally love using benefit oriented onboarding because really you're speaking to users in terms of the reasons why they downloaded your app you're giving them the benefits what they're going to get out of this and remember people don't download your app for features users are focused on outcomes so when you explain the reasons that they didn't have downloaded your app the benefits that they're going to get and those explicit outcomes that they're going to achieve they're much more likely to stick around and activate inside that experience.

**Для Напарника:** Лендинг и первый экран: «помогу начать, когда не получается», а не список механик.



# 🎬 Neuromarketing 2.0 & The Persuasion Engine: AI, Behavioral Science, Neuroscience and Marketing — Roger Dooley


### Убеждение — это снижение сопротивления, не давление сильнее  ·  [13:01](https://youtu.be/54vpeQBiP4w?t=781)
**Мысль:** Лучший ход часто — убрать причину колебаний: путаницу, неопределённость, недоверие, усилие, страх ошибиться.

> It ties everything together. A lot of persuasion isn't about pushing harder, it's about reducing resistance. This was a central idea in my book Friction, and it carries straight into this AI conversation. Marketers tend to think persuasion means adding more benefits, more urgency, more claims, more influence triggers. Sometimes that's right. But often the better move is to remove the thing that's causing hesitation in the first place. Confusion. Uncertainty. Distrust. Effort. The fear of making a mistake. The feeling that the company doesn't understand me. AI is really good at helping you find these resistance points. You can ask it what objections a skeptical customer might have. You can ask what's unclear, what feels risky? What would make someone stop before clicking? Those questions are useful for sales copy, sure, but they apply to almost everything. A donation page, a renewal notice, an onboarding flow, a pitch deck, a customer apology. The same human brain is on the other end, and that brain has limited attention, avoids effort, scans before it reads, is sensitive to fairness, and really hates uncertainty. This is where behavioral science and empathy come together. Persuasion isn't just about getting someone to say yes. It's about understanding why they might say no, or do nothing, or get angry, or lose trust, and then designing the experience so the right action feels easier, clearer, safer, and more aligned with what they already care about.

**Для Напарника:** Старт сессии: убирать трение (меньше полей, предзаполненные значения), а не добавлять мотивационные слова.



# 🎬 Neuromarketing: Decoding the consumer mind | Shikher Chaudhary | TEDxMICA — TEDx Talks


### Система 1 и 2: 90% решений принимает подсознание  ·  [01:43](https://youtu.be/m8XBkwLPgLQ?t=103)
**Мысль:** Эмоции решают, а сознание только оправдывает; быстрые решения — подсознание, медленные требуют усилий.

> In Nobel Prize winner Daniel Kahneman's excellent, excellent book, Thinking, Fast and Slow, he outlines two distinct modes of thinking, system one and system two. System one, he postulates, is thoughts from the subconscious mind. They're basically fast thoughts. Think of things like understanding and hearing language. System two conscious thinking, on the other hand, is from the conscious mind. It's slow, it's robust, and it's far more effortful. Think of things like learning a new language, or creating something new, or parking in a tight spot. Higher-order thinking is essentially where system two thinking comes in. Now, we all know this fact at some level, but we don't like to think that we're all very rational creatures who make our decisions after careful, conscious deliberation. But that is far, far from the truth. As it turns out, about 90% of decisions we make throughout the day are from the system one subconscious responses. Our emotions are making the decisions for us, and all our conscious mind can do is basically justify those reasons.

**Для Напарника:** Первые секунды экрана решают: эмоциональный тон и красота важнее аргументов и объяснений.

**Мысль:** Онбординг не обязан содержать туториалы; нужно найти минимальный объём информации, без которого нельзя начать.

**Мысль:** Одна история с такси для пожилой женщины разошлась по сети и дала больше маркетинга, чем реклама. Дизайн и забота влияют сильнее, чем функции.

invested so much into delight and i'm not you know i know delight is a very fancy word but the point was really they were trying to do a lot of things like destiny is all the same story they were trying to do many things to get more customers and bring more features and more characters and speed up the delivery and all the things and one thing that was incredibly successful and way more # 🎬 Progression Systems - How Good Games Avoid Skinner Boxes - Extra Credits — Extra Credits


### Долгосрочное планирование и возможность откатить ошибку (re-spec)  ·  [00:59](https://youtu.be/S5camMoNw-o?t=59)
**Мысль:** Система прогресса, которая даёт обдумывать решения днями, удерживает интерес вне игры; возможность перераспределить за небольшую цену оставляет стимул думать, но не губит опыт.

> First off, they can add a long-term strategic component to the game. Most of our in-game decisions are usually pondered for a few minutes at most, but a progression system gives the player an opportunity to plan out and contemplate game decisions for hours, or even days. To use the simplest example, let's compare the talent tree in a game like "World of Warcraft" to the system some JRPGs are still using too frequently where stat boosts and new abilities are awarded to characters at each new level in a predetermined, unchangeable fashion. Anyone who's played an MMO knows how much time they spent thinking about their build; how they were gonna maximize their damage output, or squeeze out the last few points of healing. And that's an enjoyable part of the MMORPG experience: it adds to the game, and it gives you something fun to brainstorm about even during those dull parts of the day where you aren't able to play. It keeps you engaged with the game even when you aren't at the machine. That's just the simplest example, but using your progression system to provide an extra layer of long-term strategic play can add a great deal to a game experience. And, especially if you feel compelled to use a timer or an energy system, at least provide your players with the engagement of plotting what they're going to do with their energy while they're waiting, rather than just creating forced downtime. Be aware, though: in order to make a progression system engaging, you've got to reward the player for thinking through the strategic problem you're presenting them, but you've also got to give the player some way to backtrack if they're not happy with their solution. This is why World of Warcraft allows you to "re-spec" your character for a small cost. This way, there's still an incentive to think hard about the strategic problem of building your character, but you can't completely ruin your own experience by making mistakes.

**Для Напарника:** Выбор того, что строить на острове, можно менять; ошибка выбора не наказывается.

> To help with this, it's useful to think about the emotions that a mechanic evokes, rather than the mechanics themselves. So with XCOM 2's contentious turn timers, Firaxis was hoping that by forcing players to speed up, they would take more risks and end up in interestingly sticky situations. But, as I've discussed before, there are other routes to this same feeling, but with different - and less annoying - mechanics. Like rewarding fast-play in Mario and Rabbids, or ramping up challenge over time with Invisible Inc's brilliant alarm system.

### Навыки растут от повторения, креативность от заботы и намерения  ·  [09:04](https://youtu.be/gr3GKcwG9s8?t=544)

Of course, your disciplines and the skills you have learned are wonderfully important, but just know they will tend to naturally win the competition for your attention. My encouragement is to be committed and proactive in caring for that deep creative part of yourself that is easily overlooked. To develop an unconditional respect for your creativity, which will sustain you during the times when the ideas are either sparse or not very good. Our professional skills develop with repetition. Our creativity develops with deep care and intention.

### Качели силы: дай почувствовать мощь и снова подними сложность  ·  [05:18](https://youtu.be/S-JsFaJhs88?t=318)
**Мысль:** Игрок переходит от «слишком слаб» к «одним ударом», затем вводят более крупные цели; такое повторное «разгон-торможение» ощущения силы держит интерес и не наскучивает.

> Damage versus health. This is what A Game About Feeding a Black Hole essentially is. Is as you're playing it, you can go back and forth between being more powerful or less powerful than the things you're trying to break. Breaking bigger things that have more health give you more money, so you're incentivized and rewarded to go after them. But then on the tree, the fun thing you get to do is you get to have this push and pull effect where at one point you were able to one-shot everything, but then we pull up the health again, so now you can no longer one-shot them again. And then you can go back and forth, and so you get this teeter-tottering effect. It's what games like Path of Exile do and Diablo. It's what I think World of Warcraft does, especially in classic, where you at one point you start a new zone and you're too weak to be able to beat everything. You get a couple upgrades, and now you're able to go back and kill those same enemies that were just kicking your butt. So, doing this damage versus health effect, Shell Divers does this very well as well. You're you're DPSing these creatures, and you slowly get upgrades in the tree to be able to DPS them more and more and more, and eventually you're able to kind of one-shot stuff. And now you feel really powerful, but what do they do? They introduce a new zone where now you find bigger stuff. And the bigger stuff doesn't linearly scale with the amount of stuff. So, it's like it's this exponential thing, so you're not even you're encouraged to then be able to go and try to do this slower-paced gameplay where you're breaking bigger and bigger objects and then go find the upgrades. Yeah, where all of a sudden now you're one-shotting that stuff again. And then they introduce another tier where now it's even harder to break, and you have this nice push and pull effect, which gives you this longevity of keeping the player interested, keeping the player engaged so that they are not ever becoming too bored. And you're kind of playing with their perception of how powerful they are. If a player all of a sudden feels like a god mode and you just don't have any way to kind of push against that, especially if you're doing one of these kind of A Game About Feeding a Black Hole or kind of Shell Divers like, you need them to feel powerful, and then you need to pull that back down so that they can feel powerful again, and then you pull it back down. And it's just kind of revving the engine up and down on this player's power that ends up feeling pretty good.

> How can you take a different route to that? Instead of using very strongly black hat techniques into shaming people into not taking that action you don't want them to take, how about you use maybe a little bit of those black hat, but you also balance it with white hat into what you want them to do rather than shaming them for not to not do this action. Show them what are the good things. How you can do some core drive one epic meeting and calling. how you can use some core drive 3 creativity and feedback so that people can actually do those actions because they want to do the action. You convince them of doing those actions. Keep in mind that using the white hat way in these ones will not necessarily drive immediate action. So you maybe combine a little bit of black hat ethically towards taking the action, not away from not taking the desired action by shaming people into feeling stupid. You want people to feel smart and to not regret that decision later. This is very important. That also opens the door for maybe in the future I want this. You know, you respect the user's agency. Long-term loyalty does not come from tricking people. It comes from trust, not traps. Or the final concept I want to leave you here with is if you need to trick people into using your product, then the problem is your product is not good enough or it's not there to solve any real problem. # 🎬 Text Appeal: Language That Makes – Not Breaks – Your Product Design with Erik Wong — Conversions


### Пиши от выгоды пользователя, а не «Google нужно...» (+32% согласий)  ·  [14:35](https://youtu.be/lfZCI-SOS2g?t=875)
**Мысль:** Продуктоцентричный язык игнорирует мотив; нужно ответить, зачем пользователю говорить «да».

> If you're writing this sentence it's your job to understand what the benefit is or what the users goal is and to convey that in language that they will understand that they'll get all right so here's another example this is a real-life example again from Google Maps it's a screen where we're asking the user to opt in to saving their location and right off the bat you can spot the vagueness of that headline get the most from Google Maps what does that really mean it sounds good but what's what's the actual value or what's the benefit and then in the main text below the sentence actually starts with Google needs to periodically store your location to improve Rao correct and they did that blah right Google needs to Google needs to who cares what Google needs if yeah I'm serious if you're the user and you're reading this you only care about what you need and I see this a lot product centric language so let's see the before and after on this one here in the new version the headline is still vague but it's more or less overshadowed by this new content here it calls out specific benefits to the user and these benefits are also phrased from the users point of view see suggestions for places to explore versus the products point of view Google needs to the UX writer who worked on this language had to lead her team through some pretty difficult but worthwhile conversations really zeroing in on the question of why would a user want to say yes in the end millions more users were saying yes thanks to the revamp on the right to the tune of a 32 percent increase in opt-ins.

# 🎬 The ABC's of High-Converting User Onboarding Experiences — ProductLed


### Силы прогресса (Jobs to be Done): боль и притяжение против привычки и тревоги  ·  [00:24](https://youtu.be/tMea1lPSCTc?t=24)
**Мысль:** Две силы толкают к изменению (боль текущего и притяжение нового), две блокируют (привычка и тревога перед новым); онбординг должен усиливать первые и снижать вторые.

> To build a high converting onboarding experience, one useful concept is the progress making forces from the Jobs that we've done framework. According to this framework, there are four main forces that influence users as they go from the Current Situation to a New Behavior. There are two forces that direct users to adopt a new behavior. That's a portion of the current situation whether it's painful for them or the pull of the new solution, whether it's innovative or something new. And there are also two forces that block change. This is the habit of the present and the anxiety of the new solution,.

**Для Напарника:** У человека с СДВГ главная блокирующая сила это тревога начала; интерфейс и тон кота должны снимать её, а не добавлять давления.



# 🎬 The Beginner's Guide to Gamification (19 of 90): White Hat vs Black Hat Gamification — Octalysis Prime by Yu-kai Chou


### Белая шляпа даёт долгосрочность, но без срочности; чёрная даёт срочность, но плохое послевкусие  ·  [02:41](https://youtu.be/qOzJAdxyzSs?t=161)
**Мысль:** Нужен баланс; Battle Camp сразу после чёрной шляпы (доплата за босса) вознаграждает белой шляпой, чтобы человек не чувствовал себя заложником.

> Gamification doesn't mean it's necessarily bad. A lot of people voluntarily put themselves into black hat gamification in order to for instance go to the gym more often or eat healthily and it's you're probably going to make money for the company because it works on you. But just like gambling and other addictive behaviors, you don't feel like you're in control and therefore in the long run when you can leave the system you will leave the system. So even though white hat gamification is powerful and makes people feel good, there are limitations to it. The biggest problem with white hat gamification is that there's no sense of urgency. So if I tell you go out and change the world, you're all excited like, "Yeah, I'm going to change the world." But I'm going to first eat a nice breakfast. I'm going to brush my teeth. Right? No urgency, even though you feel powerful. But if I take out a gun and point at your head and say, "Go out and change the world. I'm going to kill you right now." You're probably going to still going to go change the world, but you're probably not going to go eat your breakfast. You're probably not going to brush your teeth. And you also don't feel good. So when you can escape my grasp of killing you if you don't change the world, you will want to. So one has urgency and one has long-term engagement. See, when you have too much black hat gamification, you always want to balance it with white hat gamification. And sometimes, you know, you have a lot of white hat and people are engaged, they're happy, but you're like, how come my numbers aren't improving as quickly? How come I'm not monetizing or whatnot? And usually in that case perhaps you should look into some black hat game techniques because it does drive results. So one example is well portrayed in the game Battle Camp that my friend Charles J made. So in battle camp you'll have a troop where 25 members are fighting this boss for about 8 hours. And so throughout 8 hours everyone's working hard putting their energy. And so at one point maybe after 7 hours 15 minutes people realize oh we can't beat the spots. We're only at 80% of damage. So then now the player has to make a choice. Do you just give up and have everyone 25 people wait waste eight hours of their time or do you spend $5 and you be the boss. So at this point it's it's really a loss and avoidance type of motivation. So people spend money. But so people will spend and afterwards as you can imagine they don't feel really good. Even though they beat the boss, they felt they were held hostage. Like they had to do it or else everyone's going to waste their time. Now, luckily in the game, immediately after the game showers them with white hat motivation, which is putting onto making them feel accomplished. Wow, you just beat this amazing boss. Hey, you just got this amazing award. But more importantly, there's social influence. Everyone's like, "Wow, you're the hero of our troop. You spent money and you saved our entire troop. You're awesome." And so the user feels, hey, I guess I guess that was great. that was that was $5 well spent. And so as a result, they're more likely to come back and spend more money because of that balance. So whenever you implement any type of black hat techniques, it's very important to add in the white hat stuff right after to a company and make sure you drive long-term behavior.

**Для Напарника:** Нам нужна лёгкая срочность без давления: мягкое приглашение «окно сегодня открыто», а завершение сессии всегда сопровождать тёплым признанием.

# 🎬 The Onboarding Fix That Gets More iOS Users to Pay — Jamie

> Something we also want to be doing during our onboarding sequence is having heavy personalization when possible, as we can turn the app into a specific version for that user. If we were to take this a next step, we can actually use their onboarding answers to influence settings in the app, so we can customize it for that specific user. A popular and effective way to do this is to use two to four simple questions that make the user feel seen, whether it's their goals, their struggles, their levels, their timeline, or their preferred style. Then we can reflect back to it during the onboarding sequence, so we can really show that we've listened to them there.

# 🎬 The Psychology of Idle Games — Cardboard Mountain


### Почему трудно уйти: вложенная ценность, динамика, лёгкое оправдание  ·  [05:56](https://youtu.be/QXXLbS6yklQ?t=356)
**Мысль:** Endowed value: чем больше вложено времени, тем ценнее кажется игра. Постоянно открываются новые цели, а сыграть ничтожно мало, так что оправдать продолжение легко.

> But surely players would just quit once they realize there's nothing to it right not quiet it's hard to justify quitting for three reasons firstly the endowed value effect plays a big role you ascribe value to your current game because of how much progress you've made in it so far the longer you've played the more value you ascribe to it basically people become emotionally attached to things they've dedicated time and attention to secondly they're hard to quit because of their Dynamic nature as you progress the nature of the game changes new mechanics and upgrades are constantly unlocked the players are continually presented with new goals finally it's incredibly easy to justify continuing to play it takes so little to let the game keep idling players who get bored or frustrated with the game tell themselves they'll check the game less often or they'll justify their compulsion to keep playing because of how little time each session of gameplay takes but you're still playing it unless you delete the app entirely.

**Для Напарника:** Остров как накопленное вложение даёт естественную привязанность; микросессии и малый порог входа облегчают возвращение без давления.



# 🎬 The Secret Ingredients of Great Hospitality | Will Guidara | TED — TED

> Because up until then, I had been so focused on excellence, on all the little details that go into making a meal great, that I somehow hadn’t realized something really important: That in restaurants, our reason for being is to make people feel seen. It's to make them feel welcome. It's to give them a sense of belonging. See, in restaurants, the food, the service, the design, they're simply ingredients in the recipe of human connection. That is hospitality. And I realized, if we could be unreasonable in our pursuit of that, we could give people the kind of experiences they would remember forever. It was only then that I realized I wasn't actually in the business of serving people dinner. I was in the business of serving them memories.

**Для Напарника:** Кот-напарник: главное чувство продукта — «меня заметили и приняли», а не эффективность; реплики должны замечать детали, сказанные пользователем.



# 🎬 The Strange Psychology of Cozy Horror Games — Games and Devs


### Безопасная база и опасная зона питают друг друга  ·  [06:32](https://youtu.be/n3UwMqLukdw?t=392)
**Мысль:** Уютная петля даёт снаряжение, чтобы выходить в тяжёлую зону, а тяжёлая зона даёт редкие материалы для расширения базы.

> Developers merge them by establishing dynamic tension. Instead of a flat line of constant terror, a cozy horror game splits its world in two distinct zones that completely rely on one another. The safe haven, your cozy home base. This is where you heal, craft, and organize your stuff. Even better if it is where you can let your creativity flow. The wilderness, the horror zone. This is where the terrifying mechanics live and where you must venture to get resources. These two create a beautiful harmony. The cozy gameplay loop gives you the upgrades and the gear needed to brave the horror zone and the horror zone provides the rare, exotic materials required to expand your cozy base.

**Для Напарника:** Дом как безопасная база, Фокус как трудная вылазка: усилия в Фокусе приносят материал для роста острова, а Дом даёт отдых.


### Dredge: риск добровольный и привязан к награде, сохранения щедрые  ·  [09:46](https://youtu.be/n3UwMqLukdw?t=586)
**Мысль:** Самые ценные награды только ночью, но игрок сам выбирает остаться; автосохранение на каждой стоянке снижает цену ошибки.

> The game balances it by keeping your automated checkpoints incredibly forgiving. Every single time you dock at a save zone to sell fish or repair your boat, or just to skip the night, the game saves. The brilliance of Dredge is that the horror is tied to a risk-reward mechanic. You choose to stay out while it was dark because the rarest, most valuable fish only bites at night. The game doesn't force you into horror. You can stay in your comfort zone and play it slow.

**Для Напарника:** Необязательный вызов (длинная сессия, сложный шаг) приносит редкую находку, но можно играть медленно, без наказания.



# 🎬 The Streak Was Never a Reward. It Was a Threat. — The Manipulated Mind


### Стрик задуман как угроза, а не награда  ·  [00:33](https://youtu.be/SqpIzOTczG0?t=33)
**Мысль:** Награды можно игнорировать, угрозы нельзя; защита уже имеющегося тянет сильнее, чем погоня за новым. Это измеримая особенность мозга.

> Not how do we reward people for coming back tomorrow? A different question, quieter and colder. How do we make people afraid of what happens if they do not? Because rewards are optional. You can walk away from a reward and feel fine. Threats are not optional. Something in you refuses to just let them sit there. Think about the last time you protected something you already had, compared to the last time you worked hard to get something brand new. Which one pulled at you harder? Which one kept you up later, phone in hand, just to be safe, just to check one more time? If you are honest with yourself, protecting the thing you already possessed felt more urgent than chasing something you did not have yet. That is not a quirk of your personality. That is a documented, measurable feature of how the human brain values loss compared to gain.

**Для Напарника:** не применимо: стрик как угроза противоречит тону «ноль, а не минус». Напарник показывает накопленное и не наказывает за пропуск.

> Which is, if you like the superego, it's a little part of the brain that has no control over what you do, no control over the -- doesn't see the senses, doesn't control the muscles. It looks over what's going on. It's that little voice in your head that's watching and saying, "That's good. That's bad." Or, "Why are you doing that? I don't understand." It's that little voice in your head that's the seat of consciousness. Here's a great reflective product. Owners of the Hummer have said, "You know I've owned many cars in my life -- all sorts of exotic cars, but never have I had a car that attracted so much attention." It's about attention. It's about their image, not about the car.

# 🎬 The UX Psychology Behind Apps People Can’t Stop Using — uxpeak


### Эффект цели: не начинай с нуля (карточка 10 штампов, 2 уже стоят)  ·  [01:41](https://youtu.be/2TlIg3VokY8?t=101)
**Мысль:** В автомойке карточка с двумя уже поставленными штампами завершалась почти вдвое чаще; прогресс 20% с первым шагом отмеченным вместо 0% даёт ощущение импульса (LinkedIn profile strength).

> This tip will change the way you think about onboarding entirely. Researchers at Columbia ran a study at a car wash. One group of customers got a loyalty card with eight empty stamps. Fill all eight, get a free wash. The second group got a card with 10 stamps, but two were already filled in. Same eight washes needed, but the group that started with two stamps completed the card at nearly double the rate. That's the goal gradient effect. The closer people feel to finishing something, the faster they move toward it. And the critical insight is this, you can choose where the starting line is. Look at this onboarding screen. 0% complete, five empty stamps. It's telling the user you haven't started and there's a lot ahead. That's deflating, even if the actual work is minimal. Now, this version. Same form, same fields, but progress already shows 20%. The first step is checked off. The user can see exactly where they are and how close the finish line is. They didn't do any extra work. You just reframed account creation as step one instead of treating it as a separate event. 0% feels like standing still. 20% feels like momentum. And that feeling is what separates the users who finish onboarding from the ones who drop off at the second screen. LinkedIn does this brilliantly. They show you a profile strength meter from the moment you sign up, and it's never at zero. The rule, never start a user at zero. Find something they've already done and count it. That artificial head start creates real motivation.

**Для Напарника:** Не показывать пустой остров/счётчик: засчитывать вход в сессию или решение начать как первый «штамп»; ноль подавать как «ноль — это не минус».

> Well, for me, and I think especially being in the mental health space, it's been about user reviews. So, our rating, we've been able to maintain a 4.8 out of five rating. And this is really, really important to me. users leave comments about how Rooted has helped them go back to work, go back to school, and just reclaim confidence in their lives. We've also realized that based on usage that we're able to now take a panic attack and users are feeling better from the panic attack within under 2 minutes. It's just been really exciting to work on that and kind of see really how effective Rooted is and what users say about it. I think actually our focus on tracking user reviews and the quality of rooted over revenue has been a big factor as to why we've grown.

**Для Напарника:** Мерить Напарника по реальному эффекту (довёл шаг до конца, стало легче начать), а не только по времени в приложении.


### Слушай пользователей, береги себя, празднуй мелкие победы  ·  [13:17](https://youtu.be/VfNRd5Rk0cM?t=797)
**Мысль:** Пользователи знают, на что фокусироваться; выгорание от постоянной интенсивности; жалеет, что не отмечала мелкие моменты, оказавшиеся лучшими.

> I would suggest that you listen to users very closely. They will have the answers you need. They'll help you know what to focus on. And I believe, especially in the mental health space, at the end of the day, that's who we're most responsible. Launching Rooted with the MVP and getting that original feedback. I mean, to this day, I still read all the user reviews, even though I have somebody else do the responses now. I still go in and I make sure I know what's going on and just to have a pulse on what users are saying. I'd also recommend taking care of yourself along the way. I think there were definitely quite a few points where I almost reached burnout. I was building something really intense and that level of intensity was with me at all times. And I think I looking back I wish I celebrated a few of the smaller moments more because now I realize that those were like the most awesome moments whereas really I was just focus on the next thing and ready to get to the next thing. So I would recommend taking care of yourself and also celebrating the small wins.

**Для Напарника:** Сама идея Напарника: отмечать малые победы; и команде отмечать вехи, не только гнаться за следующим.



# 🎬 This is how you treat ADHD based off science, Dr Russell Barkley part of 2012 Burnett Lecture — Adhd Videos


### Проблема - задержка последствий; нужно приближать их во времени  ·  [02:25](https://youtu.be/_tpB-B8BXk0?t=145)
**Мысль:** Социальные последствия всегда отложены; решение - искусственные близкие последствия и внешняя подотчётность.

> What we do know is we are not to excuse you from your mistakes because the problem you're having is not with consequences so why would I excuse them the problem you're having is with the delay to the consequence all important social consequences are delayed consequences and that's your problem time so the solution to anybody's problem with an executive deficit is to tighten up accountability to make you more accountable more often to other people with more consequences artificial as they may need to be but I need to bring consequences very close to you in time so I'm not going to excuse your behavior I'm actually going to hold you more accountable than other people and that is why we do bod allows me to sprinkle artificial consequences all throughout the environment to improve your functioning and that means that the success of my intervention is based on the willingness of other people in the natural environment to make those changes if they're not willing to build ramps so to speak to build the scaffolding it's not going to work the stakeholders.

**Для Напарника:** Немедленная тёплая реакция напарника на действие. Подотчётность реализовать через присутствие кота, не через наказание (не применимо: штрафы и стыд).
