# 🎬 This Psychological Trick Makes Rewards Backfire — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Квесты в Don't Starve научили играть, но убили свободную игру  ·  [01:19](https://youtu.be/1ypOUn6rThM?t=79)
**Мысль:** Игроки оптимизировали ради квеста, избегали риска и потеряли мотивацию, когда квесты кончились; решением стали тонкие подсказки в UI.

> When Klei was making the initial prototype for its sandbox survival game Don't Starve, they quickly realised that testers had no idea how to play the game - and they instantly became stuck. So the testers were given a few hints - and once they got over the hump, they were able to experiment, explore, and started to have a lot of fun. In response, Klei decided to create a series of small, tutorial-like quests to help players get started. Survive this many nights. Find this many items. That sort of thing. And it worked! But only so much as players learned how to play the game. Because beyond that, the quests were a complete and utter disaster. Klei discovered that players focused exclusively on those quests, and thought of everything else as a noisy distraction. They optimised their play in really boring ways in order to finish the quest at hand. They avoided doing anything risky, because it meant they might fail. And then they became completely demotivated the second the quests ran out. Klei says "In structuring the game as a series of explicit tasks to be completed, we taught the player to depend upon those tasks to create meaning in the game". In the end, Klei solved its onboarding problem by tweaking the UI to give players subtle hints about how to get started - such as highlighting the most important items you can craft. But the quests were left on the cutting room floor - leaving players to learn for themselves. Because if a game is about experimentation, exploration, or player-guided discovery - explicit goals can limit a player's creativity and imagination.

**Для Напарника:** Онбординг Напарника: тонкие подсказки вместо цепочки заданий «сделай X, получи Y», чтобы не подменить внутренний мотив.


### Цель, которую ставишь сам, сильнее заданной; рекорды бесконечны, чек-листы конечны  ·  [03:11](https://youtu.be/1ypOUn6rThM?t=191)
**Мысль:** Личные рекорды и сравнение с распределением других мотивируют сильнее порога достижения; чек-лист можно закончить и остановиться.

> Okay, here's another story. Zach Barth makes problem-solving puzzle games about designing your own automated machines, like Exapunks and Shenzen I/O. In these games, you can make the machines however you like - if it works, it works. But it's actually really fun to go back in, and see if you can refine your creation to make it, say, smaller or faster. So in Zach's first two commercial games, Spacechem and Infinifactory, he added a few Steam achievements that encourage this sort of optimisation - like the Spacechem achievement "Beat the assignment "No Thanks Necessary" in under 2200 cycles." But, in all the games released after that - those achievements are completely gone. What's up with that? ZACH: "We wanted to add achievements because that was back when achievements were cool. That was back before I thought achievements were awful." ZACH: "The thing i don't like about them is that the game already has a reward system. We have something that's far more meaningful and far less arbitrary than a random threshold." What Zach's talking about is a bounty of metrics that you can use to gauge how well you've done. There's your own personal score. There's leaderboards that compare you to your Steam friends. And there are these brilliant histograms that show you how your solution stacks up in comparison to every other player. All of these - the strive to beat your personal best, or the drive to do better than other players - are extremely strong motivators to do better. As Zach says: "a goal that you set yourself is way more powerful than a goal someone else sets for you". So if a game is about improving yourself, a personal or social goal can be a stronger motivator than a set threshold. My final story comes from the adorable track-laying puzzle game Mini Metro. The game's developers wanted to focus on personal growth and high scores. And so - according to UI designer Jamie Churchman - the team specifically tried to avoid these goal and reward meta structures as they can become a "means to an end". For example, the game does have unlockable cities - which is just to limit player choice at the beginning of the game. But Jamie acknowledges that some people will play each city until the threshold, unlock the next one, and when they've unlocked all the cities they feel like they've finished the game and can stop playing. We should remember that goals are a checklist that can be completed. And like with Don't Starve, some players will exclusively rely on the game to give them purpose and direction. But measurements of your skill - such as leaderboards and scoring systems, have no finish: you can continue to improve on your personal best forever - which partly explains why we can still play Tetris after three decades.

**Для Напарника:** Показывать личную динамику («ты сегодня на 2 минуты дольше»), а не пороги и достижения; без таблиц лидеров, чтобы не стыдить.


### Эффект сверхоправдания: награда за то, что и так нравится, убивает интерес  ·  [05:55](https://youtu.be/1ypOUn6rThM?t=355)
**Мысль:** Дети, которым пообещали награду за рисование, потом рисовали меньше и хуже; внешняя мотивация слабее и держится, пока есть награда.

> To truly understand what's happening here, we need to take a quick detour into the world of behavioural psychology. When thinking about motivation, one of the most popular models is the idea of extrinsic and intrinsic motivation. To make it simple, extrinsic motivation is when we are doing a task for reasons beyond the task itself - usually in order to receive a reward. Or, as that's better known: a job. On the other hand, intrinsic motivation is when we do a task for its own sake, simply because we find it enjoyable or meaningful. Or, as that's better known: a hobby. Intrinsic motivation is shown to be far stronger - and it lasts longer too. People can enjoy a hobby for a lifetime. Extrinsic motivation will only last as long as the rewards are there. Just see if someone will still work in your factory after you stop paying them. And this bring us back to that classroom from earlier. Okay, so the point of the study was that the kids had already shown interest in drawing before the study began. They were intrinsically motivated. Then, they were asked to make a picture - and, like I said, one group was promised a reward, and the second group wasn't. Afterwards, the researchers continued to watch the kids in their classroom for a couple weeks and found that the children who received a reward for their drawing? Well, they showed much less interest in drawing afterwards. And their pictures were of a lower quality too. Which is - wow, way to burn a bunch of kids, science. This is called the overjustification effect. And there's a huge body of evidence that says when extrinsic motivation is attached to a task that we already find intrinsically motivating, we suddenly become way less interested in the task. And other studies show rewards also make people less creative, worse at problem solving, more prone to cheating, and may lose motivation entirely once the rewards stop - even though previously they were happy to do it for its own sake! Whoops!

**Для Напарника:** Не превращать внутреннее желание сфокусироваться в задачу за очки; награды острова — следствие, не обещание.


### Цели и достижения в «внутренне мотивированных» играх сужают творчество и требуют вечной подпитки  ·  [07:42](https://youtu.be/1ypOUn6rThM?t=462)
**Мысль:** Явные цели ограничивают, ставят порог и обязывают разработчика постоянно подкидывать новые награды.

> Because there are certainly games that lean more towards intrinsic motivation. Like games that focus on exploration, creativity, expression, and growth. There are games where you set your own goals and expect no rewards in return. And so when more extrinsically motivating systems - like explicit goals, progression meters, and achievements - are added to these games, our motivation can take a hit. We become blinkered to creative solutions. We're less motivated to improve ourselves. We put an arbitrary threshold on how much we attain. And developers now need to create a constant drip feed of new goals and rewards, or risk losing us entirely.

**Для Напарника:** Не заваливать Дом ачивками и квестами; поддерживать любопытство, а не чек-лист.

> There is also a difference between choosing entertainment and drifting into entertainment. Suppose you finish your work and think, "I'll watch videos for 30 minutes." You intentionally choose it. That's different from opening the app without thinking, scrolling for 90 minutes, and later wondering what happened. The second experience often feels worse because you didn't feel like you were actually choosing. You were continuing. That distinction is useful.

**Для Напарника:** Старт сессии и завершение должны ощущаться как выбор человека («ты решил начать»), а не как автоматическое продолжение.



# 🎬 TINY HABITS by B.J. Fogg – Animated Book Summary — Upgraded Mentality


### Почему мы срываемся: берём слишком много, а начальная мотивация отступает  ·  [00:32](https://youtu.be/ijInaXZy0pU?t=32)
**Мысль:** Мы откусываем больше, чем можем прожевать; когда импульс угасает, привычки ломают волю, и мелкие срывы превращаются в снежный ком.

> We don't know why we keep failing to make changes that we so desperately want behavioral expert bj fogg does we bite off more than we can chew normally we take on more than what we can possibly handle initially we are driven by whatever force motivated us to make this change but initial motivation ebbs and our old habits start to break down our will once the momentum is gone it becomes hard to maintain and we slip back into old patterns tiny habits are the key to sustainable change.

**Мысль:** Мотивация хороша для разовых подвигов, но смена привычки требует устойчивого повторяемого поведения.

> Motivation is fleeting and something that sounds great one day like losing 20 pounds doesn't sound so great the next one day it seems like you have the ability to lose those 20 pounds then a cheeseburger tells you otherwise and finally you need a prompt to make that action clear so let's look at motivation more closely motivation is great for one-off feats especially of strength but there is a reason so many people sign up for a gym but never actually go over a hundred million people sign up for online education every single year yet only 10 percent of them graduate the motivation to start was there but not the behavioral habits to keep going see habit change isn't just a motivated effort one day it requires sustained consistent behavior not one-off feats of strength or willpower let's take saving as an example you want to start an emergency fund of a thousand dollars in case of a car accident or unexpected doctor's visit great idea regardless of how motivated you are most of us can't just put a thousand dollars into a savings account you need to develop the behaviors to help you save that thousand dollars you could stop eating out cut your cable bill in half or make your coffee instead of buying starbucks every day behavior change will get you to that thousand dollars in the long run not motivation.

**Для Напарника:** Копировать поведения, а не вдохновение: Напарник строит ритуал и маленькие шаги, а не мотивирующие речи.



# 🎬 Tiny Habits: B=MAP Behavior Model & Dark Patterns — Cognitive Dose


### B=MAP: убрать моральный груз и сделать действие крошечным  ·  [01:57](https://youtu.be/wVUuikXjFlE?t=117)
**Мысль:** Фогг снимает с провала привычки моральный вес: поведение происходит, когда сходятся мотивация, способность и триггер. Мотивацию (обезьянку) обходят, делая действие крошечным и привязывая к якорю.

> Culturally, we are completely saturated with shame around our habits, right? When someone fails to stick to a diet, society tells them they just lack willpower. >> Yeah, a personal moral failing. >> Exactly. But Fog, FOG, fundamentally removes that moral weight. He introduces the Fog, FOG, behavior model, which states that behavior happens when three things converge: motivation, ability, and a prompt. So, B = MAP. >> Right. And what makes this unique is how he treats the M, the motivation. >> Yeah, he calls it the motivation monkey. Because it's completely volatile. You might feel highly motivated to run 5 miles after watching an inspiring documentary, but, you know, that motivation just craters the next morning when your alarm goes off. >> It's too tied to shifting emotional states. >> Exactly. So, the framework brilliantly tells us to essentially engineer it out of the equation. You make the ability incredibly tiny, like doing two push-ups or flossing just one tooth. So, it takes less than 30 seconds. >> And then you use an existing anchor moment as the prompt. >> Right, like brushing your teeth. But here is the final crucial step. After the tiny behavior, the user has to immediately perform an artificial celebration. >> A fist pump or saying awesome out loud. >> Yeah, to create a feeling of shine. The book claims this creates an instant dopamine rush that wires the habit into the brain, bypassing the need for long-term repetition. The prime example is the Maui habit, waking up and immediately saying, "It's going to be a great day.".

**Для Напарника:** Шаг в Напарнике делать микроскопическим («открой файл»), привязывать к якорю дня и давать небольшое тёплое подтверждение без вины за пропуск.

**Мысль:** Мотивации к нелюбимому не бывает, поэтому старт надо технически провоцировать: таймер, другой человек, кусок на 5-15 минут.

> Number seven is self-starting skills i am a procrastinator i do not feel motivated to start things so i had to learn how do you self-start and what i thought i would do is i would wait until i was motivated i do not do that anymore i learned to not wait until i was motivated because that doesn't work i'm never motivated to do something i'm not motivated to do so i procrastinated a lot and i had to learn how to self-start i in doing that i learned to use timers i learned to use accountability that i'll talk about more in a minute i learned to chunk things down into small chunks that i could conceive of myself actually doing it you know five minutes 10 minutes 15 minutes of something rather than thinking you know i'm going to study for so i would study for long periods of time but in order to start i had to think of it as a smaller chunk so anyhow i had to build skills for starting skills for starting it's not just hey choose to start it's just you just need to motivate yourself i don't do that so i had to trick myself into starting.

# 🎬 UI UX Кейс Duolingo | Как управлять пользователями с помощью дизайна — Nexfield Academy Lab


### Прекоммитмент: конкретное обещание на малое время  ·  [04:12](https://youtu.be/ldaEjdjuO-g?t=252)
**Мысль:** Чем конкретнее и достижимее обещание (3, 5 или 10-15 минут в день; 7, 14, 30 дней), тем меньше откладывания; не «хочешь тренироваться?», а «3 или 7 дней в неделю».

> Pre-commitment is a motivational strategy where the core idea is that people promise to do something in the future. Studies show that by making a commitment, we feel the need to remain consistent and are less likely to procrastinate. The more specific and achievable the pre-commitment is, the more likely we are to follow through. Duolingo asks users to set a clear goal before they start learning. First, they are asked to promise to spend 3, 5, or 10-15 minutes a day studying, and then to specify the desired number of days of continuous learning: 7, 14, 30, 60, and so on. If you want to add pre-commitment to your interface, follow Duolingo’s example and set specific goals. For instance, let’s take the design of a fitness app. Don't ask the user, "Do you want to work out? " with answers like "Yes" or "Maybe," but rather provide much more specific options. Be more specific: do you want to exercise on our machine 3 or 7 days a week? This way, you avoid abstraction and give the user a specific number of days that is easy to evaluate.

**Для Напарника:** В онбординге спрашивать конкретное: «сколько минут готов выделить» с малыми вариантами, без обещаний «навсегда».


### Таблица лидеров не подходит всем: публичный провал вредит  ·  [05:07](https://youtu.be/ldaEjdjuO-g?t=307)
**Мысль:** Для приложений привычек, где есть риск публично провалиться, соревнование приносит вреда больше, чем пользы.

> Social motivation or competition increases physical endurance, adherence to good habits, and even reaction speed. In Duolingo, this principle is implemented in the form of a leaderboard. As soon as you reach a high enough spot on the table, you advance to the next league. And there are quite a lot of leagues, which keeps users interested. A leaderboard is a proven way to maintain audience engagement, but it is not suitable for all projects. For example, it is inappropriate for apps that count days without alcohol or cigarettes. The risk of failing in public will do more harm than good. Think carefully about whether such competition aligns with your goals, brand, and audience.

**Для Напарника:** не применимо: лидерборды и сравнение с другими противоречат тону без стыда; прогресс только личный.

> When you first open the app it shows you a prompt that's saying take a deep breath and that perhaps a user before they enter the app and that overall I think enhances the app experience from the very beginning and then it shows you the different types of goals you might want to achieve through the app and I really like how it allows you to select multiple goals as well and then if we quickly log in or you might like to create a new account and it then brings you to this page which adds ashes how frequent you might want to practice it has some really nice encouraging microcroppy at the bottom as well.

**Для Напарника:** Первый запуск: тёплая фраза перед первым вопросом, цели с мультивыбором, поддерживающие подписи.


### Журнал-путь собирает всё сделанное по дням и позволяет заметить закономерности  ·  [13:27](https://youtu.be/KIb99pO8ZKI?t=807)
**Мысль:** Всё, что делал человек, появляется под днём, и он может связать действия с самочувствием.

> Because I think this is a really well thought out anything that you do in the app it appears here underneath the corresponding day and it allows you then to go back over time and you know recognize patterns and make connections between what you did that day and how you felt.

**Для Напарника:** Мир как летопись: показывать пройденные сессии по дням, с привязкой к настроению, без оценки.



# 🎬 What EXCEPTIONAL Onboarding Actually Does - Building Successful Apps — Philipp Lackner


### Холодный пользователь: у него нулевой кредит терпения, длинный онбординг = потеря  ·  [00:55](https://youtu.be/kouFJkcLKAQ?t=55)
**Мысль:** Новый пользователь не знает, полезно ли приложение, поэтому терпимость к трате его времени минимальна: регистрация без необходимости, десять разрешений, длинные туториалы заставляют уйти. Потеря происходит не из-за функций, а из-за онбординга.

> And the moment a user opens your app, they are completely cold at this point. They've opted in downloading your app kind of, but they still have zero ideas whether this app is really beneficial for them and their life. So, fresh users who don't have the context about how useful your app could be don't really have a very high tolerance against you or your app wasting their time. And onboarding very often is an absolute waste of time for from the user's perspective. If the user simply has too much too much time before even being able to get something from the app. So, they maybe have to sign up even though you they technically wouldn't need that account in order to use your app in some way. They have to accept 10 different permission prompts. They have to click through a long tutorials where some kind of speech bubbles keep on appearing everywhere. Then, what they will do is they will quit again and that is the moment you've lost the user, not because the functionality of your app is bad, but because your onboarding is trash.

**Для Напарника:** Первый заход в Напарника: никакой регистрации и разрешений до первой ценности. Человек с СДВГ уходит ещё быстрее; цель — первый шаг сессии за секунды.



# 🎬 What is a Gameplay Loop: Steamworld Dig 2 — The Game Analyst


### Три шага петли: действие, награда, предвкушение  ·  [02:34](https://youtu.be/mq4IFZeMFaM?t=154)
**Мысль:** Награда - основной источник мотивации, а предвкушение (новые инструменты) заставляет с нетерпением возвращаться к действию.

> Step one or the action is the thing that the players will be doing throughout the game which in this instance is digging for materials this is the meat of the game that is a focal point of it all step two decay the reward is how the player is rewarded for the work they put in this example the reward of money and less tangibly a bigger tunnel and progressing story are considered the rewards this will be the prime source for motivation for the player moving forward to continue playing the game the final step three is the anticipation the purpose of this step is to make players excited to get back to step one and start the cycle anew the example in this case is receiving new upgrades and equipment making players want to play with their new tools in the action step of the next cycle.

**Для Напарника:** После награды (рост острова) показать намёк на то, что станет возможным дальше, чтобы тянуло начать следующий Фокус.


### Психология петли может служить удовольствию или эксплуатации  ·  [09:03](https://youtu.be/mq4IFZeMFaM?t=543)
**Мысль:** Те же приёмы могут снижать стресс или эксплуатировать склонность к зависимости; дизайнеру нужно идти по тонкой грани.

> The psychology aspects mention prior can be used for good intentions to create a game meant to cause enjoyment and relieve stress however it can also be used adversely to prey on negative addictive aspects of people's personalities games that prey on the addictive natures of people are doing a disservice to the industry as a whole it is a thin line that needs to be walked by designers to not treat their players as creatures to prey upon.

**Для Напарника:** Этическая рамка: используем петли для помощи фокусу, не для вовлечения ради вовлечения.



# 🎬 What is body doubling? How do you Body Double? | Learn to Thrive with ADHD | Adult ADHD Coaching — ADHD Coach Mande John - Learn to Thrive with ADHD

**Мысль:** Ощущение обязательства перед живым человеком даёт то, чего мы не можем дать себе; особенно для скучного, раздражающего и страшного.

> Showing up for that other person is. the key to it. All right. Showing up for that other person is what we're not doing for ourselves or we're learning to do for ourselves. That's what my clients are learning to do with me. That's what I had to learn to do when I learned to manage my time and keep my appointments and be on time. So I had to learn to show up for myself, but sometimes I still need that other person when it's especially boring tasks or frustrating tasks or kind of scary ones where I'm not sure what to. It's really nice to go ahead and schedule those in.

**Мысль:** Импульс встать разбивается о присутствие другого; микро-ответственность помогает пережить дискомфорт.

> I like to leave my camera on. You have the choice of leaving your camera on or off. You have the choice of having your microphone on or off. And so what I will do is turn off my microphone so I can listen to music or do whatever I'd like, and I leave my camera on. The reason I like to leave my camera on is because. there's someone on the other side, likely with their camera on. And if I get up and get a drink of water, like my brain tells me I want to go do or get up and go do something else I'm gonna be distracting. And so when I have that thought or that urge to go do something else, I realize there's someone on the other end that can see me. And so I continue to do my work. It's kind of a little bit of. Accountability on the other side, which is really nice. And so I can get through that discomfort of wanting to get up and go do something else pretty easily because there's someone on the other side, kind of counting on me to stay focused.

**Для Напарника:** Кот виден во время сессии и «замечает» уход (мягко, без упрёка), что создаёт осознанность.

# 🎬 What Makes a Game Cozy? ~ Design Doc — Design Doc


### Нет заданного пути: ключевые предметы разбросаны в нескольких местах  ·  [03:10](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=190)
**Мысль:** Цели можно достигать в любом порядке, а предмет найдется в нескольких местах, поэтому прогресс возникает в ходе того, что интересно человеку; ничто не закрыто.

> As you go from place to place in the park, you’ll stumble into something new around almost every corner. You’ll find key items scattered throughout, unlocking new mechanics to use, but the game doesn’t limit you to only one place to find the items. These buds bloom and act as a trampoline to let you glide around easier, but the bucket you need to activate them can be found in a bunch of places throughout the mountain. Not just one. There’s a kid that needs a shovel to finish his sandcastle, but there are 5 different places where the game will let you find one. Scattering key items like that helps you complete tasks in the middle of doing whatever exploring you found most interesting. There is no set path up the hill and no set order in which you’ll meet people or complete tasks. It’s entirely at your own pace, and you can take or leave the elements that most interest you. You don’t have to complete everything either, there’s nothing really to gate you off from achieving your goals, save for one clearly marked place, and you just collect what you want to in whatever order feels right. Whatever you want to do is totally fine with the game.

**Для Напарника:** Дать несколько путей к одной цели на острове и в очереди шагов, чтобы начинать можно было с того, что тянет.


### Смотровые точки показывают, как далеко ты зашёл  ·  [04:35](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=275)
**Мысль:** Дымка на среднем расстоянии и обесцвечивание далёкого дают глубину и намёк на то, куда идти дальше; на вершине видно пройденный путь.

> The sense of depth that the game plays with is outstanding. From the game’s usual isometric angle, you don’t get a sense of it. You’re focused on the local area and what you can do, and where you can go. But every so often, you’ll reach one of these lookout points, and you can see just how far you’ve come. There’s a haze effect at medium range and a really smart desaturation and outline technique the game uses for things far off in the distance, hinting at something that might pique your interest. Maybe you should go over there, too?

**Для Напарника:** Мир/AppBackdrop: гряда на горизонте как дымчатая даль, а экран Мира как смотровая точка, показывающая пройденное.


### Свой темп: задержаться или рвануть, оба варианта верны  ·  [05:07](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=307)
**Мысль:** Игра принимает и медленное общение со всеми, и быстрый подъём на гору: это ваш отпуск.

> Or not, whatever you’re feeling. The whole game might only take you an hour or two to complete, but that’s really up to you. If you want to linger around talking to everyone you find and helping them with their problems, be my guest. If you want to blitz up the mountain, have at it. It’s your getaway. Do whatever feels right to you.

**Для Напарника:** Позволять Напарнику вести и спокойные 10 минут, и 50 минут, без оценки темпа.


### Уют и ясная цель совместимы  ·  [05:34](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=334)
**Мысль:** Grow Home имеет одну чёткую цель, но остаётся расслабленным: нет времени давления, нет боя, падение почти ничего не стоит, подбадривает бортовой компьютер, и можно останавливаться на видах.

> Games tend to feel cozier when they have low consequences, but that doesn’t mean they have to be these squishy, aimless things. They can still feel cozy while being focused and having clear goals. Grow Home is a very simple physics-based platformer. You’re a clumsy little robot named BUD who has to jump, climb, and glide his way around a huge plant to get back to his mothership floating high above the planet. The plant is filled with these branching buds. If you grab one, you’ll ride it as it grows and you can sort of steer the plant towards other things, like these glowing landmasses that give energy to the plant. Along the way you’ll find and collect some crystals that give BUD upgrades that help you traverse the world easier. That’s about it. Grow Home has a very single-minded overarching goal, but stays cozy with its relaxing cycle of jumping, climbing, growing plants, and collecting as you get closer and closer to the mothership. It’s more single-minded than A Short Hike, but it’s still very relaxed and lets you go about that goal at your own pace. The game uses a calm, ambient soundtrack, a low-poly art style, the occasional soothing song, and the mothership’s computer cheering you on to keep it’s relaxed vibe. There’s no time pressure, no combat, and hardly any enemies to get in your way, save for the occasional creature. Even if you died to it, though, there’s not much consequence, or much consequence from falling off of the plant for that matter. You’ll just get teleported back to the nearest beacon. It’s just you, the plant, and a ton of crystals to collect as you climb your way back home. But as you go up, pause for a second every once in a while and take in a vista. You’ve earned it.

**Для Напарника:** Цель сессии может быть чёткой (один шаг), а окружение расслабленным; кот подбадривает, а не оценивает.



# 🎬 What Makes A Good Game Companion? — Design Doc


### Ключевая механика привязана к партнёру; его возврат даёт настоящее облегчение  ·  [07:14](https://youtu.be/pfNzYJ0S8Oo?t=434)
**Мысль:** FLUDD и Cappy лишь временно забирают, и без них ощущается нехватка; возврат даёт глубокое облегчение.

> We can go beyond a guidebook and UI, though. Sidekicks can be the source of a lot of the player’s core abilities. A central gameplay mechanic might be tied directly to what the partner can do or can provide. Mario Sunshine has FLUDD, a robotic water pack that can spray water for attacking enemies, cleaning goop, or for moving around the world. The water mechanics were the big new thing added to separate the game from Mario 64, and what better way to teach you how to do them than to have your partner just tell you? Mario Odyssey's hat mechanic is its main draw, so slap some googly eyes on your cap, give it some dialogue and now you’ve got a partner. Cappy gives you tons of more movement and attack options. You can throw it forward, use it as a stepping stone to jump off of, and most importantly, possess the mind of whoever you see. It acts as a typical guide, too, giving out hints and showing you points of interest as you travel through the world. It feels a little like visiting all of these places with a friend. But sometimes you don’t know what you’re missing until it’s gone. Both Cappy and FLUDD are briefly taken away from you over the course of the game, and not having them is immediately felt. The movement mechanics they’re responsible for are fundamental to most of the puzzles and challenges the games throw at you, and without those mid-air movement options, you’re stuck with just your core Mario platforming abilities. It gets a lot tougher to move around, especially in Mario Sunshine’s case. The relief you feel when you get FLUDD and Cappy back is real, and it’s on a deeper level than just missing them being around.

**Для Напарника:** Привязать механику роста острова к коту. Не применимо: намеренно забирать кота как наказание.


### Спутник растёт вместе с игроком, становится полезнее и уверенней  ·  [14:11](https://youtu.be/pfNzYJ0S8Oo?t=851)
**Мысль:** Развитие Атрея вшито в геймплей: он усиливается со временем и ощущается живым человеком со своей волей.

> That character development is even baked into the gameplay. You as Kratos get to observe Atreus as he becomes more confident. As a sidekick, Atreus gets more powerful and useful over time to help you deal with enemies. He’ll help you translate Norse writing and give you bits of lore he’s learned. You also have to deal with Atreus’ fits of anger, overconfidence, and defiance, where he feels not just like a CPU-controlled puppet, but an actual person with a will of his own. Atreus as a sidekick shows off what you can do when you fully commit to treating your sidekicks as real characters, tying together what that should mean for the gameplay and the narrative, and shows off what it can look like for a character to progress alongside you as you both make a lifechanging journey together.

**Для Напарника:** Кот взрослеет вместе с островом и меняет реплики по мере накопленных сессий.


### Спутник, который теплеет со временем, даёт полезные, но ненавязчивые подсказки и удобные «хуки»  ·  [16:26](https://youtu.be/pfNzYJ0S8Oo?t=986)
**Мысль:** Постепенное сближение, умеренные подсказки, бытовые удобства (телепорт) и совместная атака укрепляют связь между героем и спутником.

> Her warming up to you over the hours doesn’t make her any less of a fun character either. By the time you get to face off against Zant for real, she doesn’t pull any punches. Plus, as a partner, she’s helpful, providing decent but not overbearing hints, quality of life hooks like teleporting you and objects around, as well as giving a fun team-up attack to help build a stronger bond between the two of you.

**Для Напарника:** Кот постепенно становится теплее и ближе по мере сессий; микро-полезности вместо навязчивых подсказок.



# 🎬 What makes some technology so habit-forming? | Nir Eyal | TED Institute — TED Institute


### Формула Фогга: мотивация, способность и триггер  ·  [03:46](https://youtu.be/11dYx_rW_Ks?t=226)
**Мысль:** Поведение происходит, когда есть достаточная мотивация, достаточная лёгкость и триггер; чем проще действие, тем вероятнее оно.

> Turns out there's actually a formula to help us predict the likelihood of these behaviors comes to us from a researcher at Stanford by the name of BJ fog who tells us that for any Behavior B we require three things sufficient motivation sufficient ability and a trigger must be present so we can plot these three elements on this graph motivation on the Y AIS ability on the X so if something is very easy to do it's on the far right if something is difficult to do it's on the far left and when we cross that red threshold and the trigger is present the behavior occurs every single time online offline your behavior your spouse or kids Behavior your customer or user's Behavior always requires these three basic elements of motivation ability and triggers and what we see happening today is that companies building habit forming Technologies are pushing that ability curve as far to the right as possible because what they know is that the easier a behavior is to do the more likely we are to do it.

**Для Напарника:** Снижать порог старта: один тап «начать» при открытии Фокуса, предзаполненные 25 минут.

> And finally variable rewards of the self variable rewards of the self are things that feel good that have this element of variability but don't come from other people and aren't about these information or material rewards these are things that are intrinsically pleasurable the search for Mastery consistency competency and control.

**Для Напарника:** Закрытие шага — самостоятельная награда: приятная анимация, звук, лёгкая хаптика; мастерство важнее очков.

# 🎬 What makes the perfect cozy game? — Adam Plays


### Доверие к праву на ошибку освобождает любопытство  ·  [05:07](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=307)
**Мысль:** Обучение через ошибку - универсальный индикатор хорошего дизайна. Когда страх провала уходит, игрок экспериментирует и вовлекается эмоционально, а не механически.

> But recoverability alone can be enhanced by actually teaching players with their failure. A great example of this is Slime Rancher, personally a like top three cozy game for me, I think. You can lose slimes and mess up plort economies, but the game never hard resets your progress. These mistakes do cost time and efficiency, but they allow you to learn optimal setups and gameplay mechanics naturally through trial and error. And if I'm being honest, teaching through failure is pretty much a universal good game design indicator. It's great for cozy games, but also great for all sorts of other games, too. Either way, both of these are important to build trust with the game. Once you trust that you're allowed to make mistakes and learn from them, you as a player can start experimenting and engaging with the game emotionally rather than mechanically. And that's exactly when the game starts to feel cozy. >> Once the fear of failure is gone, you can play more with curiosity, and that's a lot more interesting and relaxing to play with.

**Для Напарника:** Онбординг и Фокус: показывать, что сбой шага нормален, и тем самым разрешать эксперименты с длительностью и шагами.


### Побег: игрок сам задаёт скорость, нет срочности и наказания за паузу  ·  [11:04](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=664)
**Мысль:** Уют создаёт пространство, где время замедляется и давление уходит; важнее всего контроль. Необязательные цели вместо принудительных, общая цель лишь фон в голове.

> It's more about giving the player a space where time slows down, pressure fades, and the outside world just feels a little bit farther away. And the most important part of that is control. There are a lot of ways games can do this. The first of which is quite simply letting the player set the speed at which they play. There shouldn't be a constant sense of urgency. You shouldn't be rushing from objective to objective and you're not punished for just stopping and honestly doing nothing for a while. The world should kind of progress as you do. This can be done with things like optional objectives instead of forced ones. Maybe you have the overall goal from the very beginning, defeat Calamity Ganon, restore the community center, escape the planet. But this is kind of just a motivator in the back of your mind at most times. You're playing just for the sake of playing. You're experiencing and escaping to this virtual world.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус: длительность выбирает человек, нет сигнала срочности; план на день - мягкий фон, а не обязанность.



# 🎬 What Pokémon Did To Real Animals — Playlosophy


### Механика дружбы: игра вознаграждает за доброту к существу  ·  [02:58](https://youtu.be/w_qmsjD9WnU?t=178)
**Мысль:** Стат дружбы растёт от прогулок, ягод, боёв без поражения и открывает эволюцию; позже добавили поглаживания, угощения, лагерь, пикники.

> Generation 2 was the first leap the games made in incorporating Pokémon welfare as a mechanic. They introduced friendship, a stat that went up when you walked with a Pokémon, fed it berries, used it in battle without it fainting. Some Pokémon could only evolve when this number was high enough, which meant the game was now mechanically rewarding you for being kind. Then in Generation 6, we got Pokémon Amie, where you could pet your Pokémon on the touch screen, feed them treats, play mini-games with them. By Gen 8, you were camping with them. By Gen 9, you were having picnics.

**Для Напарника:** Рост острова и расцвет кота могут быть наградой за заботу и присутствие (неагрессивные действия), без штрафа за отсутствие.



# 🎬 What's your game's core loop? — Ivan Alexiev Game Design


### Петля создаёт предвкушение: работа сначала тяжёлая, потом работает на тебя  ·  [02:09](https://youtu.be/TzSp47iEFh8?t=129)
**Мысль:** В Splendor сначала много усилий, потом карты работают за тебя, и игрок чувствует прогресс. Повторяющаяся петля даёт планировать и ждать, а план и его исполнение делают награды лучше.

> At first, you'll do a lot of taking gems actions, but as you get more cards, you don't need as many gems, so the ratio is going to change. And so, you're going to feel that feeling of progress. And you're going to have players have in their heads this question of, oh, am I at some point going to get cards for free? And yes, you will. And that's part of like the game structure and what makes the game fun is at first you've got to work really hard to get what you want and then you don't need to work as hard because your cards are working for you. One thing that the core game loop or a loop in general does for games is it can build anticipation. And you can see that actually really well in games with rounds. So, when you have a round, typically, how often are you going to tell players, "Hey, let's do one full round and then you're going to get the game." Well, that happens because you do the same things over and over again. And once you do them once, then players start planning. They start anticipating what's going to happen next round. And the game suddenly gets interesting. You're not going to be doing different actions every single time. You're going to be doing the same actions, but it's going to you're going to be changing the context of those actions. you're going to be changing the power of those actions and those actions are going to be affecting each other which is a big part of what makes games fun. And so game loops can build anticipation because players can see, oh, I'm going to buy a card now and then later that card is going to come which might help me in this other part of the loop which is maybe combat like in Dune Imperium for instance. And then if I win at combat, then that's gonna make buying cards easier because I'm gonna get spice and then I'm going to get that other thing. And so players can start anticipating. They can start planning. One of the types of fun is making a plan and then executing it or making a plan and seeing if it's going to execute. Is great and it makes rewards all the.

**Для Напарника:** Ранние сессии заметно дороже усилием, а накопленное на острове со временем начинает приносить само: чувство прогресса и ожидания.



# 🎬 Who Gets to be Awesome in Games? — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Подарить фантазию сразу: простые вводы — эффектный результат  ·  [00:25](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=25)
**Мысль:** Batman Arkham и Spider-Man переводят простые намерения в красивые результаты: щадящие системы, подсказки, магнитное наведение. Спам кнопки выглядит круто, а не наказуем.

> And from my experience of playing this sort of game, it feels like there's two, distinctly different ways of delivering this fantasy. The first way is to simply give the fantasy to the player. Easily and immediately. Which is something we see in a number of recent super hero games: in the Batman Arkham games, the free-flow combat system makes it effortless to beat up baddies. And in Spider-Man, we don't need a lengthy tutorial to swing around the city with ease and grace. This is usually achieved by providing the sensation of being powerful, which happens through simple inputs that get translated into big, flashy animations. Cinematic set-pieces with pop-up buttons. Lenient systems that subtly fix your mistakes. Magnetic snapping towards enemies. Big, obvious indicators that tell you when to counter. Dopey AI that waits patiently to attack. And so on. These games work really hard to make you feel awesome, by translating your simple intentions into impressive feats on screen. Troy Skinner, producer at Batman-publisher WB Games, explains it like this: TROY SKINNER: When people come in they spam the punch-in-the-face button. In a lot of games you get punished for that. It makes you look bad. You fail. In this game you spam the punch-in-the-face button and you look awesome! You are in fact Batman effectively punching people in the face".

**Для Напарника:** В Фокусе и Доме любое простое действие (один тап «начать») должно давать заметный тёплый отклик, а не ошибку или штраф.


### Заслуженная фантазия силы слаще подаренной  ·  [01:50](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=110)
**Мысль:** DOOM Eternal требует мастерства; создатель сравнивает это с подъёмом на гору против лифта. Преодоление делает награду ценнее.

> Through skill and mastery. Which is the approach of this year's DOOM Eternal. That game has a very stiff learning curve with all sorts of mechanics and systems to figure out - including resource management, enemy weak points, weapon switching, lighting-fast movement, the flame belch, the chainsaw, the blood punch, and more. And until you master all of them, you're going to fail - and you certainly won't feel like the bad-ass demon slayer in the game's marketing. Which means that the opportunity to live out the power fantasy is withheld until you've proven that you have real skill and mastery over a complex set of mechanics. Hugo Martin, is the creative director on DOOM Eternal, and explains it like so: HUGO MARTIN: "Hey man I'm dying to these characters because I'm not doing certain things. I don't feel strong. It's like yeah: but you're gonna feel strong when you master how to beat them". So we can give the fantasy to the player, or we can make them earn it. But why would a developer choose one over the other? Well, Hugo Martin puts it very succinctly when he says: HUGO MARTIN: "We wanna give you something to master. Because the power fantasy that is earned is far more satisfying than the one that is just handed to you". By making the player actually overcome challenge, failure, and frustration, the end result will be way more rewarding. He compares the experience of mastering DOOM Eternal to that of climbing a mountain - it's much more satisfying, he says, to climb it yourself than to simply take an elevator to the top.

**Для Напарника:** Часть наград острова оставить за редкими находками и серией усилий, чтобы они ощущались заслуженными.


### Высокая цена входа отсекает людей до самой игры  ·  [03:25](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=205)
**Мысль:** Мастерство связано с мотивацией: если человек не чувствует себя компетентным, он уходит. Скотт Ригби сравнивает освоение управления с билетом в парк, цена которого для многих слишком высока.

> It's a fair point. And many of these superhero games do end up feeling shallow and patronising because they give the fantasy away too easily. But - when designers tie the attainment of a power fantasy to a certain level of skill - they ultimately end up having to ask who gets to live out the fantasy, and who gets left behind? As Troy Skinner explains... TROY SKINNER: "Because we are expert gamers, we say that mastery should come from overcoming challenge. You earn it. But remember everyone paid $60 and the majority of them aren't going to push through those barriers to get to the mastery. And mastery is tied to motivation so if they're not masterful, they're demotivated, they walk away". He argues that there are plenty of players who simply aren't willing to suffer through failure and frustration in order to experience the game's core fantasy. For example, Troy points to the bro gamer - casual players who have a very low threshold for suffering and want to feel competent immediately. And because they make up 60% of the console market, you ignore them at a cost. More importantly, however, there are players who simply aren't able to reach that level. In the book Glued to Games, behavioural scientist Scott Rigby talks about "control mastery" - which is the time and energy needed to learn a game's inputs and be able to turn intention into action. He compares this to paying for admission to get into a theme park: both are necessary steps in order to access the actual fun inside. But, Scott says, "when people are discouraged by a game’s controls, they don’t have the chance to feel competent at gameplay, because they can’t even get to the real game. For them, the price of admission to the fun of games is so high, they often stay outside the turnstile".

**Для Напарника:** Снизить цену входа в Напарника: первое действие за один тап, без обучения и настройки, чтобы человек быстро почувствовал компетентность.


### Дилемма: доступность против глубины  ·  [05:15](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=315)
**Мысль:** Лёгкая фантазия доступна всем, но кажется поверхностной опытным; заслуженная удовлетворяет, но закрывает дверь многим.

> Ultimately, then, we end up with a conundrum. If we make the power fantasy easily attainable, the game is accessible to all but can end up feeling shallow to more seasoned players. And if we force the player to earn the fantasy, the experience might be significantly more satisfying - but we lock out a considerable number of people. By making one type of player feel awesome, we lose the other.

**Для Напарника:** Для Напарника важно давать и мгновенный уют, и более глубокий слой для тех, кто хочет усложнять.


### Низкий порог входа и высокий потолок мастерства  ·  [07:20](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=440)
**Мысль:** В Bayonetta можно жать кнопки и выглядеть круто, но скрытая глубина даёт простор для мастерства. Skill floor и skill ceiling — разные оси.

> A second solution is to reward mastery - but not actually require it. For this one, let's look at Bayonetta. This is a game where you almost immediately feel empowered and awesome: thanks to great animation, absurd finishing moves, a very forgiving dodge, and a reasonably low level of challenge. You can basically just mash the controller against your face and Bayonetta will pull off a huge variety of awesome-looking attacks. It's easy to feel fluid, cool, and competent in this game. But there's so much hidden mastery in this game for those who are willing to put in the work. Things like the dodge offset system, and the huge number of possible moves - means that those who want to get combo mad can express their skill and mastery. That ends up being the real power fantasy which is, indeed, more satisfying… because it's earned. If we think about this by borrowing terms from e-sports: there's skill floor, which describes the base-line level of skill needed to be effective with that character. And skill ceiling, which describes the highest level of skill you can potentially express. If Spider-Man has a low skill floor, but low skill ceiling - and DOOM Eternal has a high skill ceiling and a high skill floor to match, something like Bayonetta strikes a better balance by having a low skill floor and a high skill ceiling.

**Для Напарника:** Награды острова доступны любому, а редкие находки и глубокие комбинации поощряют тех, кто вовлечён больше.


### Подготовь ожидания через нарратив и маркетинг  ·  [09:34](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=574)
**Мысль:** Если игра обещает сделать тебя всемогущим, провалы бесят; Skate честно говорит, что ты новичок, и сюжет зеркалит кривую обучения.

> Solution four is to simply prime players for failure and learning through the narrative and marketing. One of the issues with DOOM Eternal is that the game suggests that you are the most powerful, demon slaying bad-ass in the universe. I mean, you just ripped your way through hell in the previous game. And now, you're falling at the feet of the game's most basic demons? Ultimately, when games promise to let you play as Batman or Spider-Man, or the Dragon of Dojima, or the God of War it's priming us to be powerful and skilful: and so it does make sense that we immediately get to feel awesome. Anything else would just be discordant. And so if a game wants to make us earn the fantasy, it needs to explain that up front. A good example of this is Skate: a skateboard simulation game with a significant learning curve just to do a kickflip. But it doesn't suggest that you'll immediately start playing as a pro skater - instead, you're just a lowly amateur trying to get their photo on the 37th page of a magazine. The game's narrative - which is about slowly becoming more successful as a skater, neatly mirrors the game's challenging learning curve.

**Для Напарника:** На лендинге и в онбординге честно обещать маленькие шаги и тёплую поддержку, а не мгновенное преображение продуктивности.


### Несколько путей к успеху: навык или время и усилие  ·  [10:42](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=642)
**Мысль:** В Hades можно побеждать, подбирая удобное оружие или просто накапливая постоянные улучшения за долгую игру.

> And finally, solution five, is to provide multiple ways to win. I've been playing a lot of Hades lately. And in each run of this mythological roguelite, you pick a weapon - perhaps a sword, or maybe a bow and arrow. Some of these weapons are far easier to use than others: the shield, for example, lets you absorb enemy attacks and play in a very defensive and reactionary manner. Whereas the fists force you to get up close and personal with your enemies, which puts you at a significant risk. By having different ways to succeed, players can gravitate towards the weapon, the character, or the build - that works for them. Hades also lets you unlock additional power, health, and help - so it's possible to succeed not by getting better - but by simply playing the game for a long time. That's not my personal preference for how roguelikes should work: but I can see the advantage in giving players a different way to succeed. Through time and effort, rather than skill and mastery.

**Для Напарника:** Прогресс острова растёт просто от присутствия и регулярных сессий, независимо от «качества» фокуса, давая путь для тех, кому тяжело концентрироваться.


### Фантазия силы подвижна: растёт вместе с игроком  ·  [11:39](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=699)
**Мысль:** Помощники снижают XP, чтобы со временем их отключали; награды за новое оружие в Hades; сложность в Ori растёт сама. Каждый уровень навыка должен ощущаться сильным, но с ростом.

> Most of these solutions are about the same exact thing: letting players find their own way to feel powerful. That might mean using accessibility options and tuning down the difficulty, or it might mean ramping the game up to hard, playing with the most challenging character, and exploring the game's most complex rules. Both of those might be the optimum challenge for two completely different people. And something else that a lot of these solutions have in common is that they encourage players to always be pushing themselves to be better. To be moving towards more challenging and complex gameplay. In Forza, using those assist options reduces the amount of XP you gain, so you're encouraged to eventually turn them off as you get better at the game. In Bayonetta, scrappy play leads to rubbish trophies, so you're motivated to be more thoughtful with your actions. In Hades, there are rewards for trying out weapons you haven't used in a while. And in Ori, the game just naturally gets more complex, the further you go. Because the power fantasy, it turns out, is not one specific point on the learning curve: it's something more dynamic, that can move and grow as you do. You might feel the power fantasy immediately, and then feel it even more strongly as you challenge yourself to do better, to explore more of the game's systems, and to increase the challenge of the game. So ultimately, if the question is: who gets to be powerful? My answer would be: everyone. Not just those who are skilful. And not just those who want to feel immediate empowerment. But games should use design and systems to ensure that at every skill level, the player feels powerful and masterful. But always with room to grow and improve.

**Для Напарника:** Мягко стимулировать рост (например, предлагать длиннее сессию), не наказывая за короткие.



# 🎬 Why Are Horror Games So Cozy? — Mikey Lee


### Ностальгия и контроль как ритуал успокоения  ·  [07:41](https://youtu.be/IyLy-HUQMRo?t=461)
**Мысль:** Возвращение к знакомому как к любимым сериалам даёт чувство контроля, и это иная причина продолжать, нежели страх или азарт.

> And we also double-down on this when the nostalgia factor comes into play. A lot of these games we would have first picked up when we were younger, and this stuff was a lot scarier. And I'll never forget how overwhelming horror games were to me as a kid. I mean, I've waffled about that enough on this channel. Both first playthroughs back then would have been so much more intense than they are now. I still remember having nightmares and being physically ill because of how scared I was. A friend of mine used to tear out the pictures of horror games from magazines that his parents used to buy him just because he was scared that the creatures would climb out of it. At night, he'd even put the magazines outside of his bedroom door just in case. Coming back to them now is like revisiting that version of yourself, but you're not scared anymore, and you can just savor the feeling of who you used to be. I think horror is particularly powerful when it removes control, but having familiarity removes that as a factor. It's kind of like a calming ritual. It's like going back and watching those old episodes of The Simpsons or Always Sunny or something. We've spent so much of our lives in those franchises that going back and playing them lets those feelings that you felt originally wash over you again. Those spaces that used to feel threatening or totally overwhelming now actually feel pretty relaxing, and with the fear lessened, you start to feel more in control, and that is a completely different reason to keep playing.

**Для Напарника:** Строить причину возвращения на ритуале и чувстве контроля, а не только на дофаминовой неопределённости.

The more it'll resonate with people. Experts say that's why successful Gen Z branding isn't technically about a particular look or feel. It's about a brand's ability to convince a customer that using their products can help them express part of their identity to other people. The best Gen Z brands announce to us that they are Gen Z brands by being, in terms of their ethos, less like a billboard and more, more like a box of identity Legos. # 🎬 Why Cozy Games Keep Disappointing Us - And Who's Actually Getting It Right — JaeTeeGames


### Уют рождается из освоения последствий, а не из их отсутствия  ·  [01:16](https://youtu.be/Pu6dw07JEo4?t=76)
**Мысль:** Уютное чувство в Harvest Moon шло от порядка, наведённого над хаосом: сорняки превращаются в ферму. Дофамин идёт от самой работы, а не от отсутствия трения.

> Cozy wasn't always a marketing tag. It was a byproduct of hard work. If we go back to the late '9s to the era of Harvest Moon on the SNES or the N64, nobody used the word cozy. We called these farming sims or management RPGs. And if you actually go back and play Harvest Moon 64, it is stressful. You had a stamina bar that depleted rapidly. If you work too hard, you passed out. If you didn't water your crops, they died. And if you didn't feed your cows, they got sick. The cozy feeling didn't come from the lack of consequences. It came from the mastery of those consequences. It was a satisfaction of order over chaos. You took a field of weeds and rocks, which is stressful, and you turned it into a profitable farm, which is relaxing.

**Для Напарника:** Остров должен расти за счёт реальных усилий сессий (победа над хаосом задачи), а не выдаваться даром; но без наказаний за пропуск.

**Мысль:** Простая мини-игра должна иметь кривую навыка и ощущение роста. Если красиво, но тратит время впустую, это уже не уют.

> There is a difference between simple and mindless. Dardu Valley's fishing miniame is simple, but it requires focus and has a skill curve. You get better at it. Mano's crafting felt like a chore I had to do to unlock the next cutscene. It was friction without reward. If a game respects your eyes but disrespects your time, it isn't cozy. It's tedious.

**Для Напарника:** Действия на острове и в сессии должны давать ощущение мастерства или прогресса, а не быть механической повинностью.

# 🎬 Why People with ADHD Procrastinate (And 4 Brain-Based Ways to Get Started) — Dr. Tracey Marks


### Дефицит дофамина: усилия не подкрепляются и мотивация гаснет  ·  [02:52](https://youtu.be/Xp805f-j1VI?t=172)
**Мысль:** Механизм: при СДВГ дофаминовые сигналы не доходят, поэтому позитивное поведение не получает подкрепления и усилия не ощущаются как награда.

> So with ADHD, this reward system is impaired because you have less dopamine transport proteins to carry the dopamine along the track to get to its intended destination. It's like having a massive supply chain shortage when it comes to transporting your dopamine, so you don't get the proper dopamine signals to reinforce positive and productive behaviors. Without this reinforcement, your efforts don't feel satisfying and you lose motivation.

**Для Напарника:** Обоснование мгновенной, заметной награды за каждый маленький шаг (рост острова, реакция кота), а не только за итог.


### Четыре драйвера мотивации: интерес, срочность, вызов, новизна  ·  [03:39](https://youtu.be/Xp805f-j1VI?t=219)
**Мысль:** Мотивацию при СДВГ повышают интерес, срочность, степень вызова и новизна; дальний дедлайн не включает старт, срочность даёт толчок.

> Motivation in ADHD is driven by four factors, interest, urgency, degree of challenge, and novelty. Things are novel when they're fresh and new. So what you do to increase your motivation is to find ways to increase these four factors for any given task. The need for urgency is why you work better under pressure. If the deadline's too far away, you don't feel motivated to start. But urgency gives you the kick that you need to power through and get it done, or at least start, even if it feels stressful to you because you waited until the last minute.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-лист для механик Фокуса: у каждой сессии должен быть хотя бы один из четырёх драйверов (короткий таймер = срочность, новый ориентир на острове = новизна).


### Таймер создаёт срочность и превращает задачу в игру на время  ·  [04:38](https://youtu.be/Xp805f-j1VI?t=278)
**Мысль:** Таймер делает бесконечный проект ограниченным по времени, добавляет вызов «обгони таймер» и разрешает остановиться по истечении часа.

> A workaround to this is to find another way to create urgency, like using a timer while you're working. Let's say you have something that's due in three days. Rather than start it the night before, do part of the work using a timer. See if you can beat the timer. Using the timer turns it into a challenge for you which can also make it more interesting. Another thing the timer can do for your mindset is to take this project that may seem like it'll take forever and make it time limited. You've got one hour to do the work and after that hour you're done working, even if the task isn't complete. Then you plan to do something that you really enjoy, like spend 30 minutes on TikTok.

**Для Напарника:** Сессия Фокуса как ограниченный отрезок: «час, и ты свободен, даже если не закончил». Тёплый текст без вины при незавершённой задаче.


### Новизна через смену обстановки; приятная среда помогает удержать фокус  ·  [05:58](https://youtu.be/Xp805f-j1VI?t=358)
**Мысль:** Смена места (кафе) добавляет новизну, а тёплое настроение от среды может перевесить отвлекающие факторы.

> If you want to add novelty to something that was boring, try doing it in a different setting. Let's say you need to write a paper, maybe sitting in a coffee shop will make the process more interesting. You may say, "How can a person with ADHD "stay focused in a busy coffee shop?" Well, this may not be a great setting for you, but it's not a given that any busy setting will distract you because remember, with ADHD, you can hyper focus on things that you're interested in. So if sitting in a cozy coffee shop with the smell of pastries and people chatting makes you feel warm and fuzzy, that mood and attitude change may help you stay engaged with your task despite the distracting elements in your environment.

**Для Напарника:** Фон AppBackdrop (костёр, небо по часу суток) как «уютная среда» сессии; смена сцены по времени суток даёт мягкую новизну.



# 🎬 Why Your Brain Wants Videos You Don't Like — Curiocos


### Дофамин создаёт «хочу», а не удовольствие (Берридж)  ·  [00:20](https://youtu.be/E455eW6RqPc?t=20)
**Мысль:** Система желания (incentive salience) отделена от системы удовольствия; можно хотеть то, что не нравится, и скроллить без радости.

> But, that's a persistent myth. The real reason you keep scrolling is that the brain's motivation system has been separated from its pleasure system. Neuroscientists like Kent Berridge have shown that dopamine doesn't create pleasure. It creates wanting, a specific brain process called incentive salience. It's the force that makes a potential reward seem attractive and attention-grabbing, compelling you to go after it. >> >> Pleasure, or liking, is handled by a different system, driven by opioids and endocannabinoids. >> >> The two systems can be completely disconnected. It's why you can intensely crave a snack, eat it, and then feel unsatisfied. The wanting was stronger than the liking. In lab experiments, activating the dopamine system makes animals work obsessively for a reward, but it doesn't make them enjoy that reward anymore. They can be made to want something they don't even like. That feeling of your thumb scrolling on its own, chasing the next video even when you're bored, >> >> that's your dopamine system in action. It has learned that the cue, the flick of a thumb, might lead to a reward. >> >> This is driven by dopamine, which makes the cue itself seem attractive and attention-grabbing. It's not a quest for pleasure. It's a loop of pure, disembodied wanting.

**Для Напарника:** Награды в Напарнике строить на ощущении заслуженного и приятного (liking), а не на одном «хочу ещё»; не копировать крючки wanting.


### Effort discounting: трудные задачи обесцениваются после быстрых наград  ·  [05:10](https://youtu.be/E455eW6RqPc?t=310)
**Мысль:** После низкозатратных наград мозг переоценивает лёгкое и недооценивает отложенную выгоду сложной работы.

> This leads to another, more subtle effect. When tasks that require deep focus feel harder, our brains start to devalue them. This is called effort discounting. The immediate, low-effort reward of the next video starts to feel more appealing than the distant payoff of finishing a difficult project.

**Для Напарника:** Дробление задачи на микрошаги с немедленной мини-наградой за шаг делает трудное снова привлекательным.



# 🎬 WWDC25: Make a big impact with small writing changes | Apple — Apple Developer


### Веди с «зачем»: выгода в начале предложения, затем действие  ·  [07:13](https://youtu.be/iaafpzDJHS8?t=433)
**Мысль:** «Чтобы получать обновления брони, введите номер» работает сильнее, чем та же фраза наоборот: выгода стоит там, где её легче всего заметить. Приём подходит для ошибок, пушей и подсказок.

> Another small change you can make I like to call “lead with the why”. Messaging is most effective when you tell the people using your app why taking that next step will be fun, or interesting, or beneficial to them. I'll give you an example. I love crossword puzzles. I do them every day and can be a little competitive. So when I got a notification from Apple News+ Puzzles that said, “Keep your streak going by solving today’s crossword,” I went straight there. One reason this is an effective notification is because it leads with the why. You can think about this as, “To do or get one thing, first do another thing.” This shortcut applies in a lot of places, but I see it often in error messages, push notifications, or instructional copy, like tips. Going back to that crossword notification, the “why” in this case is “Keep your streak going.” And you do that by “solving today’s crossword.” I'll show you another example, this time for a fictional restaurant app that lets people get updates about their upcoming reservations. To get those updates, people have to enter their phone number. The first draft of this instructional copy might say something like what I’ve written here: “Enter your phone number to get reservation updates.” That's pretty good. There are clear instructions, and I even included the benefit: to get reservation updates. But it could be better. All I need to do is take the second part of the sentence, “to get reservation updates”, and move it to the front. “To get reservation updates, enter your phone number.” Now, the benefit of getting those updates is right at the front of this sentence, where it’s easiest to see and has the most impact. Take a look at the messaging in your app, and if you’re leaving the benefit for the end of the sentence, try moving it to the front. It only takes a moment to do, but really improves the impact of your message.

**Для Напарника:** Пуш и кнопки запуска: «Чтобы остров вырос, начни с одного шага» вместо «Начни с одного шага, чтобы остров вырос». Выгода должна быть тёплой, не стыдящей.



# 🎬 WWDC26: Craft clear names for features and labels in your app | Apple — Apple Developer


### Memories: ясность через эмоцию, а не объяснение  ·  [09:23](https://youtu.be/UFZvFqg6few?t=563)
**Мысль:** Человек, нашедший старое фото, не думает об алгоритмах, он ищет воспоминание; название встречает его там, где он находится.

> Remember, it doesn't have to pass all 3 criteria, but it's great when it does. The examples we've looked at lean straightforward and descriptive but that's not the only approach. The right name for your app might be something a bit more evocative or expressive. The Photos app has a feature that looks at your library and automatically surfaces moments that matter. A birthday, a trip, a regular Tuesday that turned out to be worth remembering. It's an algorithmic photo grouping. What would you call it? Think about who's on the other end of this feature. Someone who just opened up Photos and found something from five years ago they'd forgotten about. Or a video of a laugh they hadn't heard in years. That person isn't thinking about algorithms. They're looking for a memory. Memories works because it meets them there in a way that a technical label never could. It fits the tone of the app and the relationship people have with their photos. And while it is still straightforward, the clarity comes from emotion, not explanation.

**Для Напарника:** Подписи к росту острова и ориентирам формулировать через смысл для человека (твоё место, твоя находка), а не механику.



# 🎬 WWDC26: Principles of great design | Apple — Apple Developer


### Агентность и прощение: выбор, undo и осторожные прерывания  ·  [01:43](https://youtu.be/Xa9L2yx_QH8?t=103)
**Мысль:** Люди вовлечены, когда сами управляют темпом; ошибки неизбежны, поэтому нужна возможность отмены, а подтверждения только перед серьёзными ошибками. Прощение даёт уверенность исследовать.

> Agency is about putting people in control. People feel in control, when you let them do things their way. For example, do you want to take this next part? Oh, I'm okay, you can keep going! Okay! Offering choices is the best way to bring agency into your experience. An interface should never stand in the way of what someone is trying to do. Instead of guiding someone down a pre-determined path, let them dive right into your experience. And give them the autonomy to decide what to explore at their own pace. People are far more engaged when they have agency to control their own experience. Of course, that does mean people will make mistakes, or go down paths they didn't mean to. When that happens, offer forgiveness. People accidentally send, change, and delete things all the time. You can provide forgiveness by making it easy for them to undo any of their actions. And, when someone's about to do something destructive, double-check it's what they actually mean to do. In some cases, interruptions can be helpful. But use interruptions carefully, and only when someone is about to make a big mistake. Oops, I don't wanna do that! Just one sec here. Let's keep going. People really appreciate it, when you help them avoid disaster. Forgiveness supports agency because it gives people confidence that they can always recover from anything they try, and helps them feel capable, secure, and free to explore.

**Для Напарника:** Совпадает с тоном без стыда: дать откат шага/сессии и прерывать только перед необратимым; не вести по принудительному пути.


### Гибкость и персонализация, когда единого решения нет  ·  [10:07](https://youtu.be/Xa9L2yx_QH8?t=607)
**Мысль:** Аудитория различается по возрасту, языку, опыту, потребностям доступности; там, где одного решения нет, давать настроить расположение и скрыть ненужное.

> Another way make your design flexible, is to cater to the wide range of abilities people have. Get curious about who your audience is. How old are they? What languages do they speak? Are they a pro or a novice? Do they rely on accessibility features? You might not solve for every type of person on day one, but you can start examining how your experience can be more inclusive. Often, adding flexibility means you're not going to land on a single design solution that makes everyone happy. Sometimes, the best option, is to let people personalize your experience to suit their own preferences. Take something like controls. It's really hard to nail down a single layout that works perfectly for every person, so offer people the flexibility to rearrange them, to support their personal workflow. Or allow them to hide controls they never use.

**Для Напарника:** Настройки Фокуса (длительность, звук костра, хаптика) для разных сенсорных профилей СДВГ; дефолт разумный, выбор опционален.

**Мысль:** Рыбалка в Stardew ради процесса, а не награды, в отличие от квестов WoW; активности необязательны, без чувства вины, рутинны и без стресса.

> But one key mechanic project horseshoe is calling intrinsically rewarding activities which is BAS basically doing something not because of the general reward you'd get for doing it but because of the process of actually doing it a good example of this is World of Warcraft questing versus stardew Valley fishing most of the time in World of Warcraft you're doing the Quest for the reward whether it's better gear experience points story progression whatever but in stard to Valley at least for me I do the fishing Min game because I like it's almost a meditative practice I'm not really doing it to increase my fishing level or get money although that isn't an option it's mostly just cuz I like it cozy game mechanics are also many times optional again avoiding that sense of responsibility where you don't feel guilty for not doing something all of the goals or activities are low stakes and non-committal and primarily many of those activities emphasize that sense of mundane we talked about earlier chopping a tree fishing planting organizing Etc the routine that brings out that sense of coziness and that ties into what a lot of cozy game narratives are like running a farm or a coffee shop organizing a store or home these sort of low impact ambient non-stressful gameplay Loops.
